# Session & state: per-feature decision record

What each feature decided and why (when the obvious alternative was rejected), the maintainer's decisions, and the traps that caused real bugs. **The code is the source of truth**: when this file and the code disagree, fix this file. Traps common to all UI work (Log row arithmetic, `row_to_index`'s `top_offset`, emoji presentation, falsifying buffer tests, hit rects taken from the paint walk, inner-first hit registration) are in [where-we-are.md](../developer/where-we-are.md), "Conventions that bit us"; they are referred to here, not repeated.

## Sessions and the daemon

### Named sessions

- **One daemon per named session, and a session holds many repositories** ([ADR-0006](decisions/0006-one-session-holds-many-repositories.md)). The daemon keeps no per-repository state: every `Request` carries a `RepoId`, resolved to a `RepoRef` per call, and the lock map is keyed per `RepoId`, so unrelated repositories dispatch in parallel. Rejected: a daemon per repository. It simplifies nothing inside the daemon, one process per tab is the Electron footprint again, and a clone has no repository to belong to.
- **The boundary**: the daemon owns all repository state; a window owns the rest. The test is "would two frontends disagreeing about it be a bug?" Staging, yes. Which tab is focused, no (the CLI has no tabs). So the tab list (`sessions/<name>.toml`), the recent list (`recent.toml`) and favorites (`favorites.toml`) are plain files in the config directory, read through `gitpoulpi-session` with no IPC. Favorites are the user's, shared by every session; the lists are joined by exact path (the spelling `discover` produced).
- **Remembered tabs follow the last-used session, not the launch folder** (B7). Sessions are per repository root, so the tabs left open sit in the session file of the repository launched from last. `<config>/last-session` names it; `session_store::restore_launch_tabs` seeds a folder-derived session (no `-s`) from it when `general.remember_tabs` is on, then opens the launch repository (added if absent, active either way). An explicit `-s` session is its own world.
- **Which tab a start opens on is decided once, by a pure function.** `startup_tab::decide` (unit-tested) takes the launch facts (inside a repo or not, `--repo` given, `--launcher`, `remember_tabs`, the session's active tab, Radar offered) and the three `general.startup_tab_*` settings. Order: `--repo` wins; else `--launcher` picks `startup_tab_launcher` even inside a repo; else `startup_tab_in_repo` / `startup_tab_outside_repo`. Defaults keep the old behaviour (`current-repo`; `home`; `home`: starting outside a repo always showed Home, the remembered tab only sat in the bar). `last` is the session's *active repository tab* (Home/Radar are not persisted as "last"), and is Home with `remember_tabs` off or no tab. `radar` is Home when the Radar is off or not built, and the Settings dropdown then hides it. Trap: `run_windows` therefore calls `restore_launch_tabs(.., activate_launch = false)` first (the launch repository joins the tabs but the remembered tab stays active, so "last" can be read), and only activates the launch repository when the answer is `current-repo`. The launcher is told apart by `--launcher`, appended by `desktop::terminals::argv` (and the macOS scripts, Windows console shortcut) to every shortcut we write; old shortcuts lack it until `launcher::sync_shortcuts` rewrites them at a start, and a start without the flag is just a normal one. Windows traps (maintainer, 2026-10-21: pinned launcher opened Home): (1) `windows::status` used to ignore the arguments, so a shortcut written before `--launcher` read `Installed` and was never rewritten; it now requires the flag in the `.lnk` bytes. (2) Explorer pins a *copy* in `User Pinned\TaskBar` that keeps the old command line; `launcher::sync_shortcuts` also calls `desktop::sync_pinned` (copies with our marker get our target + arguments, copies identified only by our exe path just get the flag appended). (3) The double-click relaunch into the configured terminal appended the original args after `argv` (which already ends in `--launcher`), doubling the flag; `relaunch::relaunch_argv` dedupes. (4) `relaunch::infer_launcher`: alone in the console (`GetConsoleProcessList` = 1), or relaunched, counts as a launcher start even without the flag (a shell shares its console, so a typed start is never alone). The parent-is-explorer test was rejected: a pinned `wt.exe` shortcut's parent is `wt.exe`. None of the Windows paths run on Linux CI: the pure parts are unit-tested, the PowerShell spawn is gated by `refresh_icon_cache`. The CLI flag is hidden and global like `--cwd-file`.
- **`--repo <path>`** on a TUI launch stands in for the launch folder (`RunOptions::repo`); on any command it also steers the default session name, so an agent driving several repositories from one place joins the daemon an open TUI already uses.
- **`session list` decides "live" by connecting** to the port (250 ms), never by the `.port` file existing: a crashed daemon leaves its file behind, and only a clean shutdown removes it. It runs before any IPC client is opened (asking a daemon which daemons exist would be circular).
- **Discovery files have an owner, and a dead owner means a stale file** (2026-10-28, Windows slow start; `gitpoulpi_ipc::discovery`). A refused loopback connect takes about 2 s on Windows (instant on Linux), and an embedded daemon (the TUI's, the MCP server's) dies with its process, so a leftover `.port` made every start wait ~2 s on a dead port with "Loading repository...". Rules: (1) the embedded daemon writes `<name>.host` (its process id) next to the `.port`, never `.pid`: `.pid` means "standalone, `daemon stop` may kill it" and the updater asks such a daemon to shut down, both of which would hit the host process. (2) The TUI takes its files down on every way out (after the UI thread ends, so also on an error or a panic of that thread, and in the panic hook), only while the `.port` still holds the port it published. A kill or crash still leaves them. (3) Every reader (`ensure_daemon` of the TUI and of the CLI/MCP client, the `gp -a` / launcher scan) calls `discovery::live_port` / `remove_if_stale` first: a recorded process that is gone means delete the files and do not connect. A file with no recorded process (an older build) is probed. (4) Every loopback connect is bounded by `discovery::CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (150 ms), whatever the OS does with a refused port (PID reuse can make a dead owner look alive, so the bound still matters). (5) The scan over all port files (`join::scan_sessions_within`, run by the launcher and `gp -a`) probes them concurrently: its wait is the slowest probe, not the sum (20 leftovers were 6 s). `xtask perf --tui` has startup rows with and without a leftover port file (`perf-budgets.md`).
- **`GITPOULPI_CONFIG_DIR`** selects the config directory (tests, portable installs). It is not a layer of the config precedence ladder; it picks which `config.toml` the file layer reads. **Trap: never repoint it from inside a test.** It is process-wide and the daemon's `.port` file lives there: a `session_store` test that set it raced `ensure_daemon` in another test of the same binary and deadlocked it. Every `session_store` function has a `*_in(config_dir, ..)` twin for tests.

### Session collision, closed

- **The default session is derived from the repository root**: `gitpoulpi_config::default_session_name(cwd)` walks up to the nearest `.git` and returns `<basename>-<16 hex digits>` (FNV-1a over the canonical path). Why not one shared `default`: correctness was never the problem (every request carries its `RepoId`); **lifetime** was. With one shared session, closing either TUI took the daemon down under the other, and both fought over one `sessions/default.port`.
- It is a function of the **root, not the cwd**, so a CLI run deep in the tree and a TUI opened at the root land on the same daemon (architecture principle 3). A linked worktree gets its own session (its `.git` is a file; two working trees are two working trees). Outside any repository the start directory itself is used, so the name stays stable. `-s <name>` still forces two repositories onto one session.
- **Session names are Windows-safe with no `cfg(windows)`** (`sanitize_session_name`, applied to typed and derived names alike, since a name is a filename): an allow-list `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` (a deny-list misses control characters), trailing dots and dashes stripped (Windows drops trailing dots, so `work.` and `work` would be one file), reserved device names (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`...) prefixed with `_`, length capped at 64 with the last 17 characters reserved so the hash is never truncated. The hash is hand-written FNV-1a because `DefaultHasher` is not stable across Rust releases: a CLI and a TUI built by two toolchains would derive different sessions for one repository.
- **Trap: a `RepoId` is keyed on the repository root, never the cwd** (`repo_ref_for`). The TUI once built its id from the cwd while the CLI used the root, so a TUI started in a subdirectory and a CLI beside it keyed two `RepoId`s for one repository and never saw each other's mutations. The same rule holds at the trait: `discover` and `init` return what `discover` would return for the same path, because libgit2 hands back workdir paths with a trailing separator and `git rev-parse` does not. `RepoRef::from_root` once failed to strip Windows' `\\?\` verbatim prefix because the constant was spelled with one backslash.

### Daemon/IPC implementation status

- **Transport**: loopback TCP plus a discovery file, `<config>/sessions/<name>.port` (and `.pid` for a standalone daemon, `.host` for an embedded one), not a named pipe; the reason is on `gitpoulpi_ipc::transport`. Wire: 4-byte length-prefixed JSON, `Request` / `ServerMessage{Response|Event}`.
- **The token handshake.** Every connection starts with `Hello` carrying `{"token": "<64 hex>"}`; `server::handle_connection` runs `gitpoulpi_ipc::auth::authenticate` (generic over the stream, tested over `duplex`) before it reads anything else, answers that first `Hello` with the `DaemonInfo`, and otherwise replies `ResponseError { auth_required: true }` with the "requires authentication from a client of the same version or newer" text and closes. The token lives in `sessions/<name>.token`, removed with the `.port`, `.pid` and `.host` files (`discovery::remove_files`). Traps: a new client against an older daemon (no `.token` file) sends `Hello` without a token and is accepted, so taking over an older build still works; an older client against a new daemon gets the refusal text and, because its `Hello` failed, never tries a takeover. The handshake treats only `auth_required` as a refusal, so a test double that answers `Hello` with some other error still connects. The elevated updater, when it runs as another account, cannot read the token file: its shutdown request fails and it falls back to stopping the processes, as `stop.rs` already does. Update `job.json` / `status.json` and the installer's own file writes are left as they were: the elevated updater writes `status.json` as another account and the user must read it back.
- **One backend factory**, `gitpoulpi_daemon::backend_for`, used by every daemon start site. Otherwise which backend served a repository would depend on which frontend happened to start its daemon, with nothing on screen to say so. Default `git2`; `--backend system`, `GITPOULPI_GENERAL_DEFAULT_BACKEND=system` or `config set general.default_backend system` switch. `--backend` on a bare TUI launch is refused with a message pointing at the config key, not silently ignored.
- **Embedded vs standalone daemons.** The TUI starts an embedded daemon in-process when the discovery file names nothing alive, publishes its port, and the daemon lives as long as the TUI process (see "The daemon died with the window" below). The MCP server publishes too. **The CLI's embedded daemon does not publish its port**: a CLI process lives for milliseconds, and a discovery file pointing at a dead port costs every later client a failed connection; for the same reason it flushes the undo journal's write-behind before returning. `daemon start` spawns a detached `daemon run` that records its PID. `daemon stop` sends `Shutdown` and falls back to killing the PID only when that went unanswered **and** a PID was recorded; an embedded daemon never records one, so the fallback can never kill a client's own host process.
- **`Hello` and `Shutdown`** are answered by `server.rs` before dispatch. After connecting, the TUI link compares the daemon's git sha with its own and reports a build mismatch only when both are known: a false "different build" warning is worse than a missed one. A daemon of another build that cannot parse a request closes the connection; that surfaces as a lost connection with a hint, never as a hang. **Takeover**: a window that finds a daemon on a different build does not stop at the error (`daemon_link/takeover.rs`, called from `link_loop`, before `link_loop_with_port`). On a connection of its own it asks for `Hello` and `JobList`; with a job not finished it leaves the daemon alone and reports "jobs are running on the other build's daemon, retry with `r` or `gitpoulpi daemon restart`". Otherwise it sends `Shutdown`, waits up to 3 s for the port to close, removes the discovery file only if it still names the old port (`remove_if_ours`: an embedded daemon cannot remove its host's files), starts its own embedded daemon through `ensure_daemon_at` and connects; the Activity log and status line get one info line ("Restarted the session daemon: it was build @old, this window is @new"). Shutdown unanswered or the port still open after the wait: today's mismatch error, and no process is ever killed. Traps: the old window loses its link (expected; its `r` makes it take over back, but only once per `r`, because `link_loop_with_port` itself never takes over, so two builds cannot loop on their own); unknown sha on either side is never a takeover; the test seam is `Daemon::with_git_sha`, not an env var.
- **Every `OperationKind` is classified in one exhaustive match**, `OperationKind::traits()` in `gitpoulpi-core` (no wildcard arm, so a new variant fails to compile until classified), pinned by the golden table `crates/gitpoulpi-daemon/src/op_classification.rs` (flags N network, R read, S shared-lock network, C can leave conflicts, J journaled, P undo can rewrite pushed commits, H history, plus the `ChangeScope` its mutation reports). A wrong scope guess must be too wide, never too narrow.
- **Locking.** `server::RepoLocks` is a per-`RepoId` `tokio::sync::RwLock`. Reads take it shared and are spawned, so answers on one connection can arrive out of order (clients match by `request_id`); mutations take it exclusively, inline. tokio's lock is fair: a mutation waits for the reads admitted before it, later reads wait for it. Shared-lock network operations (Fetch, FetchAll, Push, TagPush, LFS fetch/push) run on their own task so reads are answered during a transfer; Pull and Clone stay exclusive.
- **Clone, Init and Discover are answered before repository resolution** (they have no repository to target). Init does not broadcast: nothing can be subscribed to a repository that did not exist a moment ago.
- **The conflict probe** runs after operations flagged C, **while the repository lock is still held**, so `Event::ConflictDetected` describes the repository as that operation left it, not as the next connection's mutation made it. It is a full index read, hence gated on the operation.
- **Typed errors cross IPC** as `ResponseError.kind: Option<TypedError>` (`#[serde(default)]`, so old peers still parse); the TUI's `call` turns it back into the typed `Error`.
- **Retraction kept on purpose**: `daemon_link::survives_a_repo_changed_event_without_deadlocking` was once recorded as flaky ("5 s deadline, about 16 s in isolation", which would mean it always failed). Nobody had run it. Re-measured on 2026-09-19: 6 filtered runs, 3 in-suite and 3 more with all 20 cores saturated, all green at 1.20 to 1.32 s.  **A number in a doc is not a measurement; run it before carrying it forward.**

### Live replication via the daemon

The daemon pushes `Event`s (`RepoChanged { scope }`, `OperationCompleted`, `ConflictDetected`, `PendingAutoStashChanged`, `PendingAutoStashGone`, `JournalChanged`, clone and LFS progress, `DaemonStopping`) to every subscribed client, so a CLI or MCP mutation shows up live in an open TUI. A `RepoChanged` triggers an immediate refresh; the 2 s poll (`AUTO_REFRESH_INTERVAL`) is only the safety net.

- **Scoped refresh.** `RepoChanged.scope` is a `ChangeScope` flag set (INDEX, WORKTREE, REFS, LOG, STASHES, WORKTREES, CONFIG, LFS, CONFLICTS; absent = `ALL`, so an older daemon means a full refresh). The link sends Status always and the other reads only when the scope names them; the render loop applies a result only when `outcome.scope` names it, so the previous log and refs stay. Events queued behind one are unioned into one refresh. The poll, a manual refresh and the first load are `ALL`.
- **No queueing behind a refresh.** After the first load, refreshes run on a spawned `Refresher`, single flight with "dirty again" coalescing; an action is sent the moment it is submitted. A generation counter (`Activity`) is bumped on every mutation; a refresh started at an older generation is dropped (its scope re-queued), so a stale read never overwrites a newer or optimistic state.
- **Optimistic staging** (`status_optimistic`): Stage/Unstage moves rows locally at once; the scoped refresh after the daemon answers replaces it; on error the copy is put back. Display only: the daemon owns the state.
- **In-flight feedback** has one mechanism, `Activity::begin` worded by `Action::activity_label`, shown after 150 ms (`activity::SHOW_AFTER`). `GITPOULPI_TEST_ACTION_DELAY_MS` delays every action for tests.

### The daemon died with the window: the infinite "Still loading..."

Maintainer report: a tab stuck on "Still loading... (large repository?)" forever while the CLI answered the same session in 0.3 s.

- **Root cause.** The embedded daemon was spawned on the caller's tokio runtime, which was the link thread's runtime, one per window. Every tab switch and every visit to the Repository Management tab is a window restart, so that runtime was dropped and **the daemon died with the window**, leaving the discovery file pointing at it. The next window could connect while the old daemon was shutting down; the connection closed under its first request, the reader task exited without failing the waiting `call`s, `do_refresh` never returned and `still_loading` never cleared.
- **Fix, three layers.** (1) The embedded daemon runs on `daemon_link::embedded_daemon_runtime()`, a process-lifetime `static OnceLock<Runtime>`; no window, clone or init task can take it down (tab switches also got 2 to 3 s faster). (2) `PendingRequests` closes when the reader stops (EOF, read error, unparseable message), dropping every waiting sender, so every `call` fails with "daemon connection lost: <reason>" instead of waiting forever. (3) `link_loop` returns the reason on `DaemonLink::failure_rx`; `ui_kit::pane_notice` lets a stopped link beat "loading", the panes say "Lost the connection to the session daemon ... Press r to retry", and `r` starts a fresh link (and a fresh embedded daemon if needed). A link that dies after a good load keeps the data on screen and reports in the footer.
- **Recovery**: press `r`, else relaunch (or `gitpoulpi daemon restart` if a standalone daemon serves that session). A daemon of another build is replaced automatically when idle (see the `Hello` bullet); `r` retries the takeover once its jobs finish.
- Pinned by `daemon_link/link_lifetime_tests.rs` and `tests/pty_loading.rs`.

### Loading state, not an error

`run_in_repo` seeds `log_result`, `status_result`, `refs_result`, `stashes_result` and `worktrees_result` with a placeholder `Err` until the first `RefreshOutcome`. The bug was rendering it: the Log printed `log error: backend` on every repository open. The fix is a `still_loading` flag the event loop tracks; **rendering reads the flag, never the placeholder's text**. Messages are drawn with `ui_kit::render_centered_message` (sized to the message, so a column width cannot clip it); past `LOADING_TIMEOUT` (15 s) the text says the repository may be large rather than looking stuck. Rejected: a loading/loaded/errored enum, since the many readers already treat `Err` as "nothing yet".

### Read caches

- **Daemon `ReadCache`** (`gitpoulpi-daemon/src/cache.rs`): `Log` and `Refs` answers per (repo, command, args), keyed by a fingerprint of `HEAD`, `packed-refs`, `config` and every loose ref (path, mtime, length; one stat each, no file opened), so an idle 2 s poll costs a directory walk instead of a revwalk. Mutations whose scope has REFS, LOG or CONFIG also `invalidate(repo)`, so the daemon's own writes never rely on timestamp granularity. Git's racy rule: if any fingerprinted file changed within `RACY_WINDOW` (1 s), nothing is served or stored. Only `Ok` results are stored. A Log or CountCommits request carries its hidden-refs filter (`LogOptions::hidden`), so each visibility is its own entry: hiding the archive and showing it again answers from the cache until a ref moves (`cache::tests::each_hidden_filter_is_its_own_entry_and_toggling_back_hits`); past `MAX_ENTRIES` the fast toggle back just costs a walk.
- **Commit stats** (`commit_stats`, the Log's Changes column) are cached per (repo, commit) and never invalidated (commits are immutable; the cache is bounded). The TUI asks only for rows on screen after each paint, debounced, so the first paint and scrolling never wait.
- **The selected commit's files** (`daemon_link::commit_files_worker`): `diff_files` answers paths without hunks (system git returns the full diff: a name-only diff would still cost a process and be a second format to keep consistent). The worker keeps only the newest selection (a burst of `j` collapses before any work), an LRU `CommitFilesCache` (128 entries, about 48 MiB), and at most one prefetch of the neighbors at a time, beside the urgent work, never in front of it.

### Workspaces and the workspace search

Plan and API: [workspaces.md](../developer/the workspaces design). Decisions and traps:

- **Workspaces are user data, not daemon state** (the boundary test: two frontends disagreeing about which repositories belong together is not a bug). `workspaces.toml` is read and written through `gitpoulpi-session` by the TUI, CLI and MCP alike, with no IPC; saved through a temp file and a rename. The only daemon trip is `workspace add` reading a repository's `origin` URL (a repository read goes through `GitBackend`). Recorded URLs never carry a credential.
- **`Config::profile_for` takes facts** (`WorkspaceDefault { profile, repo_is_member }`), because `gitpoulpi-session` depends on `gitpoulpi-config` and the membership test (`same_repo_path`) is in session. A workspace profile id that names nothing is saved and simply does not apply; it never breaks a commit.
- **The CLI never guesses a workspace from membership** (a repository can be in several): `commit` uses one only with `--workspace` / `GITPOULPI_WORKSPACE`. `--add`, not `--repo`, adds repositories at `workspace create`: the global `--repo` flag already means "the repository to act on".
- **One scorer.** `gitpoulpi_core::fuzzy` is the palette's three tiers; the palette's `score` is a wrapper (`Anchor::AfterCategory`), the search uses `Anchor::Whole` (a colon in `feat: x` is not a category). Positions are character indices into the *lower-cased* text.
- **The commit index never holds a repository lock.** A cold build is 250-300 ms for 50k commits; done under the shared lock it would make the fair `RwLock` queue a commit or checkout behind it. `search.rs` builds on a thread that reads the backend directly (reads of the object database are safe beside a ref update) and appends to the index under a lock held for the append only; a search that finds the index behind answers from what it has. `wait: true` (the one-shot CLI) builds inline instead.
- **Incremental by tips.** `commit_messages` takes the tips a previous answer covered and skips what they reach; a tip no ref reaches any more (amend, reset, deleted branch) makes the answer a full replacement, decided by one `rev-list --stdin` / one revwalk with `hide`. The system backend puts tips in through `--stdin` (thousands of tags do not fit an argument list). Rejected: tracking removed commits one by one.
- **Staleness is the ref fingerprint** (`cache::fingerprint_of`, the `ReadCache`'s own), plus an explicit mark from `ReadCache::invalidate` and from STASHES/WORKTREES mutations (the fingerprint does not see `.git/worktrees`). Inside the racy window (1 s) the fingerprint is `None`: the index then compares a hash of the ref targets, so a commit made a moment ago is still found. A worktree added from a shell appears at the next ref move.
- **`partial` means "never built, cut at `search.commit_limit`, or the last build failed"**, not "behind the refs": a stale index answers at once and tops itself up, which is what a keystroke wants.
- **Bodies match last.** A body is searched only when the subject did not match and the query has 3+ characters, below every subject tier, from a 512-byte lower-cased copy (memory: roughly 100 bytes plus the text per commit; `commit_limit` bounds it).
- **The search popup is an `InputMode` (`WorkspaceSearch`, `workspace_search/`), not a palette mode.** It shares the pickers' plumbing (`MainField` for the field, `popup_list::nav`, `PalettePopup` rects from the paint walk, `popup_list_mouse`), but the list has group headings, so the highlight index is a *display row* and `model::skip_headings` keeps it off them. The kind chips' rects live in a thread-local filled by the paint (`view::chip_at`), the same "captured from the draw" rule. Key `Ctrl+Alt+F` (`global.workspace_search`): `Ctrl+Shift+F` is Windows Terminal's find, `Alt+/` needs Shift on AZERTY, `Alt+F` is the merge editor's; `Alt+1`..`Alt+8` toggle the chips inside the popup (`Alt+0`: all), the theme picker's convention.
- **Scope**: `workspace_search::search_scope`: the active workspace's repositories (`GlobalSession.active_workspace`, resolved through `Workspaces`; an id naming nothing counts as none), else the open tabs. It reads the session file itself (one line, no `session_store` accessor) so the workspaces-in-the-TUI slice owns that file's API.
- **Fan-out**: one `Search` per repository of the scope on the window's own connection, `Requester::call_in(Some(RepoRef::from_root(path)), ...)` (the daemon holds many repositories, ADR-0006; a repository without a tab needs nothing opened), `wait: false`, `limit 40`, debounced 60 ms. Each fan-out is a *generation*; `pending` is cleared at the next one and `apply_answer` refuses an answer of an older one, so a slow repository can never paint over a newer query. A `partial` answer is asked again after 300 ms, doubling to 2 s, at most 12 times per query (a truncated index stays `partial` forever: the cap stops it). The spinner shows after 150 ms of waiting. `animating()` keeps frames coming only while requests or the debounce are outstanding.
- **Repositories and hosting kinds are matched in the window**, in memory, per keystroke: a daemon index is per repository, and the hosting caches (`.gitpoulpi/hosting-cache.json`) are the window's own files, read once on a thread at open (`gitpoulpi_hosting::cache_search`, a lenient mirror of the snapshot: a missing, newer, truncated or half-written cache is no hits). Never a network call. The daemon ignores `#`, `pr:` and `i:` (`ParsedQuery::hosting_only`, `SearchKind::PullRequest/Issue`: not in `SearchKind::ALL`, which stays the daemon's six).
- **Enter does not move the view itself: it hands a `UiIntent` to the agent-view machinery** (`UiViewer::go`, applied by `agent_view_tick` with `local_origin()`): `ShowTab` (repository, worktree), `ShowRef` (branch, tag, and `stash@{n}`), `ShowCommit`, `ShowHosting`. That gives, for free, the tab switch carried across the window restart, the Log paging a commit in when it is outside the loaded graph, the sidebar unfolding, and `Ctrl+N Backspace` going back. The origin `workspace-search` is the user's own choice: no "shown by" toast, no agent tab mark. Alt+Enter is Enter (no context menu is opened; see the plan's deviations).
- **A hosting row** needs the target tab's hosting list loaded (`HostingUi::reveal`): right after a tab switch the cache may not be in yet and the reveal says "not in the sidebar list"; the tab is still switched.

Workspaces in the TUI (slice W2, `workspace_ui/`, `repo_manager/workspaces.rs`):

- **`GlobalSession::tabs` is always the live list.** A workspace swap *moves* lists (`workspace_ui::store::switch`): the live one is parked in `workspace_tabs[<leaving>]` and the target's is taken out of `workspace_tabs[<target>]` (its existing repositories the first time). "No workspace" parks under the key `-` (`NONE_KEY`; no slug can start with a dash). Everything else (`gitpoulpi repo open`, `session_store::*`, the external-tabs poll) keeps editing `tabs` and needs no idea that workspaces exist. An empty parked list is kept (closing every tab must not refill from the repositories). A target id that names nothing is "no workspace".
- **A switch is a window restart like a tab switch, not a daemon operation.** `switch_to(target, go_to_tabs)`: the chip stays on the Home tab when it is on it, the page's **Open** goes to the workspace's active tab, closing the last tab keeps the workspace and shows Home (`close_window`).
- **The chip is hidden until a workspace exists** (or one is active), so a user who never uses workspaces sees an unchanged tab bar. It narrows to the dot, then is neither drawn nor registered (`tab_bar_with`'s one walk returns `TabBar::chip`). Its combo is `profile_bar`'s pattern: process-wide state, modal while open, rows at the front of the hit list.
- **The Home page is a page of `RepoManager`, not a second view** (`page`, `ws`): same title, buttons row, list, footer and hit machinery, one `render` walk. Keys and palette twins (`ManagementCommand`, `activate_selected`, `menu_selected`, `close_selected`) are page-aware, so a toolbar twin cannot reach the wrong page. `page` says whether the Workspaces view shows; it is the **Workspaces tab**, not a switcher on Home (see "Home, the Radar tab..." below): Home itself has one list and no page keys (`home.page_*` are gone). **Trap:** every other `ManagementEntry` (`Show`, `NewTab`, `Radar`, `ReleaseNotes`) must put `page` back to Repositories (`go_to_management`), or the tab bar shows two selected tabs.
- **The page's menus ride on the repository menu** (`RepoMenuAction::Ws`, `RepoTarget::workspace`): one menu slot, one set of key and mouse handlers.
- **Anything the Home tab must do after a restart is process-wide** (`apply::Pending`, taken by `RepoDialogs::open(ManagementEntry::Workspaces)`): "New workspace..." from a repository tab goes Home with the form open.
- **Daemon work is `workspace_ui::task`, off the loop, on its own connection** (like `clone_task`): saving a workspace reads each new repository's `origin` (credentials stripped), Fetch all / Pull all / Clone missing walk the repositories one after the other with progress on the Home footer and failures listed at the end. **Pull all is fast-forward only**: a walk must not stop on a merge in a repository nobody is looking at. One job at a time (`RepoDialogs::ws_busy`). Results come back through `RepoDialogs::poll`; a dialog effect's message is mirrored onto the Home footer, which covers the status line.
- **The Home page reloads on `apply::epoch()`**, bumped by every change made in this process. A `gitpoulpi workspace ...` run from a shell is noticed by `workspace_ui::watch`: a (mtime, length) fingerprint of `workspaces.toml`, one `stat` at most every 750 ms on the loop's tick (never a read, never the render thread's wait), which calls `apply::changed()` + `chip::refresh`. It is also a registered deadline (`next_deadline`), because the loop idles in `poll` and nothing else would wake it. The first look only records. Trap: that deadline wakes an idle window about every 750 ms for one cheap frame.
- **Profile resolution** (`profile_bar`): the repository's own by-hand choice, then the active workspace's profile when the repository is a member, then `general.active_profile`, the same order as `Workspace::profile_for` (a unit test pins the two together). The field names the source ("Work - from workspace Acme"), and is shown with a single profile when a workspace's default applies. `profile_bar::refresh_workspace` re-resolves whenever a workspace changed, so the commit author follows an Add/Remove from workspace without a restart.
- **Colors are theme roles by name** (`workspace_ui::COLORS`: accent, green, yellow, red, blue, purple), so a theme change recolors them; an unknown name (a hand-edited file) falls back to the accent.

## Command mapping: CLI, TUI, MCP

- **`GitBackend::resolve(repo, rev) -> Oid`** exists so the CLI never runs `git rev-parse` itself: that would put a repository read outside the `GitBackend` seam and let the two backends disagree on what `HEAD~2` means. Annotated tags peel to their commit in both backends (handing a tag object's oid to `branch_create` is the bug `refs()` once shipped).
- **One `GitBackend` mapping per subcommand** lives in `gitpoulpi-commands` and is run by both the CLI and MCP, so their surfaces cannot drift. `--json` emits the serialized domain type, errors included (`{"error": ...}`), so a script never parses English. The TUI shapes each request exactly as the command layer does (same `OperationKind`, same keys, same option defaults).
- **`Oid` serializes as its 40-character hex string**, not 20 raw bytes: it is the identity a human reads and an agent pastes into the next command. A malformed hex string is a deserialization error, not a zeroed oid.
- **Clone's destination is made absolute by the CLI** (the daemon may run elsewhere). **`--lines` without `--hunk` is rejected**: a line selection is always within a hunk.
- **`config`, `session`, `daemon`, `theme` and `shell-init`** start no daemon: they must work outside a repository.
- **Confirmation.** Only operations that destroy uncommitted work with no copy confirm: revert and `reset --hard` (like discard, branch delete, stash drop). Soft and mixed resets do not: they keep every byte in the index or the working tree and the move is in the reflog; a prompt there teaches the user to press `y` without reading. The menu paints exactly the confirming rows red from the same predicate (`log_action_confirmation`) the prompt uses. Taking a side of a conflict does not confirm: picking the right side undoes picking the wrong one.
- **A menu holds the commit's `Oid`, never a row index**: a refresh while the menu is open renumbers the rows, and `Reset --hard` on a row number moves the branch to a commit the caption never named.
- **Reword is commit re-creation, not a rebase** (maintainer decision 2026-09-24: edit any commit's message, confirm when already pushed). Only messages change, so both backends re-create the commit and its descendants on the ancestry path with their own trees, authors and dates, then move the branch in one ref update checked against concurrent moves. So: never mid-rebase (no abort path), merges stay merges, the index and working tree are untouched (no auto-stash needed), no hooks run, signatures are dropped. Both backends normalise the message with one function (`normalize_reword_message`) and write byte-identical objects (`reword_parity.rs`). Refusals are typed (`RewordRefused`). "Already pushed" is decided from the loaded graph; when the walk leaves the loaded page the warning shows anyway (a false warning costs a click, a missing one a rejected push).
- **Amend generalises reword** (`GitBackend::amend_commit`, `OperationKind::AmendCommit`, CLI/MCP `amend`, the TUI's Amend popup). Same walk and same refusals (`RewordRefusal`, plus `InvalidIdentity` and `InvalidDate`), so a request is validated once by `CommitAmend::resolve` in core, never per backend. One `CommitAmend` carries any subset of message, author name/email/date and committer name/email/date; a `None` keeps the commit's value. Only the target is touched: descendants keep their own authors, and the committer is "now" unless a `committer_*` field says otherwise (a re-created descendant never takes the override). Dates are `<unix> <+hhmm>` or ISO 8601 (`parse_git_date`, no datetime crate) and are stored in the zone given; the popup shows them in UTC and sends a date only when it was edited, so an untouched date keeps its original zone. It is one journal entry labelled `Amend <id>`, undone like a reword (ref restore); `reword` itself is `amend_commit` with a message only, and its journal label and reflog verb stay `Reword` / `reword:` (the amend verb is `amend:`). Trap: `CommitNode::timestamp` is the **author** date on both backends (`%at`); libgit2's `commit.time()` is the committer's and had made the Log's "authored" line drift after a rebase. `reword_parity.rs` compares the two backends' amended objects byte for byte, committer line included.
- **Fast-forward** of a branch not checked out is pure ref semantics; of the checked-out one, `merge --ff-only` semantics; refused for a branch checked out in another worktree. **Create branch checks it out** (a tag does not). **Patch create** is a read (it writes only to the file named and never broadcasts); **patch apply** is atomic (`git apply --check` first).
- **Ref drag and drop**: pressing a ref only arms a drag; it starts once the pointer leaves the cell, otherwise every click would drop a branch on itself. A held button is reported as `Drag`, never `Moved`, so anything following the pointer during a drag (highlight, OS cursor shape) lives in the `Drag` arm. `ref_drop_is_valid` is the one predicate behind the highlight, the banner and the drop menu (`ref_drop.rs`, also reachable from the palette). The open hand shows only while carrying; hovering a ref shows the pointing finger (owner: "c'est l'inverse").

## Repositories, tabs and opening

- **A repository switch is a restart of the window loop, not a live re-subscription.** `run_in_repo` owns a `DaemonLink` bound to one `RepoRef`; rebuilding it is a few lines, making dozens of per-repo states swappable in place is thousands. The daemon stays the same process. Consequence: anything that must survive a tab switch is process-wide (`install_*` settings, `LAST_FOLDER`, `ui_intent`, `tab_dir`), never a window local.
- **A window ends with `NextWindow::{Quit, Repository, None, Management}`.** "There is no repository any more" needed its own outcome: closing the last tab once removed it from the session file and left every pane still showing it.
- **The New tab (`+`, `Alt+T`) is the Home view with `RepoManager::new_tab` set**, not a tab of the session: it is never persisted, it lives only in the management window, and leaving that window (any tab, a picked repository) drops it. Its tab-bar slot gets `TabHit` index `tabs.len()`, so hover, `x` and middle-click reuse the tab hit machinery; the `+` is reserved room at the bar's right end (kept when tabs overflow, neither drawn nor registered when it cannot fit). "Return to the previous tab" is `ManagerAction::Leave` (the session's active tab was never changed), and a repository picked from it is the ordinary `SwitcherAction::Open`, which appends at the end and de-duplicates.
- **A window with no repository** subscribes to `no_repository_ref()`, a path that cannot be a repository, so every request fails identically and the panes draw empty; it shows the Repository Management view. Deliberately not the working directory: that may be the repository just closed. **Trap (2026-10-20):** "fails identically" meant the 2 s poll logged a WARN per repository request for ever (thousands an hour on Windows). The link now skips the first load, the poll and the event refresh while its repo is the placeholder (`link_loop_with_port`, `has_repo`), and per-frame requesters must gate on it too (`flow_tick`); a new per-frame `Requester` call needs the same gate (`pty_home_quiet_log` scans the app log for it). Defence in depth: the daemon logs an identical failure (same operation, repo and message) once per 5 minutes with a "repeated N more times" count (`dispatch/failure_log.rs`); the response is untouched.
- **Never record a non-repository**: `session_store::is_repository` guards `open_tab_in`, the one place every recording goes through (starting outside a repository once wrote the home directory into the recent list). Entries whose folder or `.git` is gone are shown grayed, not dropped, with different wording ("gone" vs "not a repo").
- **Repository facts in lists** (branch, dirty, ahead/behind, owner) come from `repo_probe` on its own thread (`git status --porcelain=v2 --branch` per row); nothing waits for git on the input loop. In porcelain v2, anything that is not a `#` header is a change (untracked `?` and unmerged `u` included).
- **Closing a tab never touches disk** (ADR-0006), so it is a `ConfirmAction::CloseTab`, not a daemon `Action`.
- **Clone and Init** run on their own threads, and clone on its own IPC connection: the link is bound to one repository and multiplexes its requests, a clone has none and is the only streaming operation, and a long clone must not delay a refresh. `init` refuses an existing repository root (`git init` happily re-runs, turning a mistyped path into a silent no-op) but allows a directory with files or inside another repository (git has no such rule). An empty `general.default_branch_name` means "say nothing", so git's `init.defaultBranch` applies.
- **Clone progress parsing**: `--progress` is required (stderr is a pipe); updates are separated by `\r`, so the reader takes raw chunks; strip the matched prefix by length, not by splitting on `:` (`remote: Counting objects:` has two colons, and every server-side phase was silently dropped). Monotonicity is enforced in the caller (git can restart a counter).
- **Folder picker**: hidden entries are read and marked, never dropped, so Show hidden is a render filter with no re-read. `.git`'s own layout satisfies the bare-repository check, so it is excluded by name from being marked a repository. New folder names reject a trailing dot or space and the DOS device names whatever the extension (`NUL.txt` is still the null device). Two fields drawn in one frame need distinct `FieldEditor` ids, or a click resolves to whichever was drawn last.
- **Worktrees**: GitPoulpi persists nothing; a worktree is git state read back by the refresh. `git worktree list` reports the main worktree and `git2::Repository::worktrees()` does not (pinned by a contract test); visibility keys on linked worktrees.
- **Following the tab's folder** (U8): "Quit & cd here" writes the last window's root to `--cwd-file` only when the window really ends (an unsaved editor cancels the quit); `gitpoulpi shell-init` prints the shell wrapper and starts no daemon. Restored tabs never go through `Open`, so `ui.tab_dir_follow = "auto"` opens no terminals at launch.
- **Why the TUI ends is recorded**: `gitpoulpi-tui-exit.log` beside the panic file, with the gesture (`quit_how`) and a ring of the last eight input events (`exit_log::RECENT`, a global because the record is written after an error unwound every local). User quits are recorded too (`q` over the Diff view once fell through to the global quit).

## Log and commit graph

The renderer, glyphs and measurements are in [commit-graph.md](../developer/commit-graph.md). Decisions and traps:

- **One terminal row per commit is a decision, not a limitation.** Two rows per commit would make lane continuity trivial but halve the history on screen, and the maintainer's reference keeps one row. Every Log row arithmetic site goes through `graph::LogRows` / `log_index_to_row` / `log_row_to_index` (stash rows shift every commit below them; the WIP row is one row).
- **Owner: the lanes must be connected completely ("pas de négociation"), with round markers.** Pre-made round glyphs always leave a gap (they ink inside the x-height), and sub-cell block art cannot draw a circle (two sub-rows per line is the only raster every font has). Hence the Graph column as an image where the terminal supports one, and a text fallback. Rules that survived every revision: a glyph in a lane's own column on a row the lane passes through must ink its cell top to bottom; **paint joins, glyphs decorate** (a background has no side bearings); a lane tint is a perceived-luminance step, not a blend percentage (only visible in a real window). No graph glyph may have an emoji presentation, a variation selector or a combining mark (they render differently per terminal and can be double width).
- **Compact graph (`graph/compact.rs`)**: a Graph column `COMPACT_W` (2) cells wide or less draws each commit's dot alone in the first cell, no strokes; one cell wider the tree is back. Purely a function of the width, nothing stored: the Log (`draw/log.rs`, text rows and the picture's rows) and the commit column's Graph part ask `graph::is_compact`. 2 is also the least width both resizes allow, and the commit column's *automatic* width never lands on it (`lanes_needed` asks for at least 3), so a linear history keeps its line until the user sizes the part down. The commit column's picture covers only the on-screen cells of its Graph part (cut at the left by a sideways scroll, at the right by the pane), so a cut graph keeps its pictures and avatars; it used to fall back to characters.
- **History order is topological** (`Sort::TIME | Sort::TOPOLOGICAL`, `git log --topo-order`): the lane layout assumes parents come after children; time order alone put branch tips on the wrong rows.
- **A freshly claimed lane always gets the next palette color**, also when it reuses a freed slot; merges keep their lane's color (a fixed merge color injected an extra hue on every merge).
- **Annotated tags**: `refs()` reports the peeled commit (`%(*objectname)` / `peel_to_commit`); otherwise a tag never matched any commit.
- **Column widths.** `LogColumnWidths` tiles the pane's inner width exactly and the table is built in one place (`log_table`) with `column_spacing(0)`: ratatui's default spacing of 1 made the solver shrink columns, so rendered and hit-tested edges disagreed by up to two cells. `Message` is never hidden: its room is reserved first and columns are given up whole in `LogColumn::SACRIFICE_ORDER`. A sacrificed column is not hidden; it returns when there is room. `Sha` wants 8 cells: the 7 digits and the cell the last column's grip sits on, so the grip never covers the 7th digit. **Growth order serves a column with a width of its own first** (a drag or a fit), then the rest in the fixed order: what a fitted column asks for is what it gets, up to `LogColumnWidths::fit_max` (inner width less `Message`'s reservation and every other live column's floor); the others fall back to their floors, never the reverse. Every x position of a column comes from one `LogColumnsGeo` (`log_table/geo.rs`; `CommandCtx::log_geo()`), never rebuilt by hand from widths and order.
- **Fit to content.** The paint walk feeds every row it builds (commit, WIP, stash) and the header into `NaturalWidths` (`WindowState::log_natural_widths`, reset each paint; `log_table/natural.rs`), in `display_width` cells plus the one gutter cell; Graph is at least `COMPACT_W + 1` (never fitted into the compact picture), the Ref pill is measured with a huge budget (the ghost pill is ignored), the Author with its avatar badge when avatars are on. `fit_column` / `fit_all` (`log_table/fit.rs`, pure over the override map) write the widths and return the status line; the double click on a grip (`mouse_left_menus.rs`, same cell within `DOUBLE_CLICK_WINDOW`, the precedent being the Radar's `divider_press`) starts no drag, fits, and re-lights the strip only if the pointer is still on the moved boundary. `Message` is never stored. The first press of the pair starts its drag and its release ends it.
- **The last column's grip** (`log_grip_rect`, `column_resize::Edge::last`) is the last column's own last cell in both resize modes and both registered as `HitTarget::LogColumnBoundary(last)`, so the highlight, the pointer shape and the drag arming are the same predicate as for every boundary (`resize_grab_at`); the pane border cell stays the divider's. Its drag is relative (`LogBoundaryDrag`: press x, start width; `last_grip_width`), capped by `fit_max`, `Message` absorbing: an absolute position would jump the column by the cell offset of the press inside the grip. Not registered under two cells or for `Message`.
- **Scroll layout** (`ui.log_columns_layout = "scroll"`). The same `LogColumnsGeo` serves both layouts (`log_table/geo.rs`): `spans` are the columns in the content (from the table's start, at their full widths: `LogColumnWidths::for_scroll`, stored `ui.log_column_widths_scroll` clamped to `[drag_min_width, MAX_COLUMN_CELLS]`, no `SACRIFICE_ORDER`, `Message` fixed), `offsets` the on-screen part clipped to the viewport (fully scrolled-out columns left out), `h_offset` the state (`WindowState::log_h_offset`, per window, never saved) clamped inside `compute` and written back by the frame; `screen_x` / `content_x` convert. Every hit rect (header cells, the hover column, tooltips, the pill rects, the ref popup anchor) comes from the clipped offsets and every grip (`grip_rect`) from the spans through `screen_x`, registered only while its cell is in the viewport. Painting (`draw/log_scroll.rs`): the block on the pane, then the table - only the columns that reach the viewport, each at its full width, `Flex::Start` - rendered into a scratch buffer (pre-filled with the pane's own cells, so the theme background survives) and the viewport's window blitted over the pane; a wide glyph on the last inner cell is cut to a blank, one cut at the left edge has already lost its first half; the selected or hovered band is carried over the blank space of a table narrower than the pane. The Graph picture drains `h_offset - graph_x` cells from every raster row, avatar pictures are drawn only where the Author column's left edge is on screen (initials elsewhere), and an inline ref-name box needs the Ref column whole. The first drag, key or fit pins every column's width (`pin_scroll_widths`) so only one column changes; the drag is absolute (`scroll_drag_width`: the pointer's place in the content), with no auto-scroll while dragging. The WIP summary being edited caps `h_offset` at Message's start. The bar is `ScrollBar::LogHorizontal`, pressed and dragged like the diff view's. Keys: `Shift+Left/Right` (matched before the bare `Right` arm, which ignores modifiers), the wheel only in this layout.
- **Column resize.** Grab strips live on the header row only (full-height strips stole row clicks). A boundary drag pins both neighbors and conserves their combined width (`boundary_drag_widths`); overriding only the left column let `Message` absorb the change and the grabbed edge stood still. `Message` is resizable; the overflow goes to the other elastic columns nearest first, deliberately uncapped (capping bounced it back onto `Message`).
- **Selection band** starts at the node, and the selected row never repaints the Ref column's pill background.
- **The WIP row** is one row on HEAD's lane. Its message cell **is** the composer's Summary buffer (one `String`, one caret, `ComposerField::WipSummary`), so nothing syncs. Selecting it always shows the working changes: enforced once per frame (`right_panel_for_log_selection`), not in each of the dozen handlers that move the selection. A focused text field wins over every pane shortcut (its key arms come first), or `Space` and `.` typed in the WIP field opened menus.
- **Stash rows** are hidden while soloing (the selection helpers index the unfiltered history while the table shows the filtered one; a stash row would be one more disagreement). Placement reads only `nodes[..=base]`, so appending a page never moves a placed stash; a stash whose base is not loaded (or whose base only a hidden ref reaches, so the walk no longer holds it) is not drawn. The TUI's stash includes untracked files (without it the WIP row stayed after a stash).
- **Search is a search, not a filter**: non-matching rows dim, so it composes with solo.
- **Ref pills**: a local branch and its remote counterpart on the same commit are one pill (two pills only when they diverged); a remote-only pill drops the remote prefix. The ref-to-commit connector (`ui.ref_connector`) only fills blank cells or draws under them, never adding or shifting a cell, so no hit target moves.
- **History paging**: `general.initial_commits_in_graph` is published through a process-wide atomic (`daemon_link::set_log_page_limit`) because the refresh runs on a task far from any `Config`.

## Refs sidebar

- **`refs_tree::ref_display_rows` is the only walk from ref names to drawn rows**; render, clicks, keys, the palette's "selected ref" and the drag resolver (`sidebar_ref_at`) all call it. Plain ref rows are not hit targets; they resolve through it.
- **Tree (`ui.ref_tree`, default on).** In REMOTE the remote is the top folder, and **where its name ends is decided against the actual remote list** (`split_remote_ref`, longest match), never at the first `/` (a remote may be named `grp/sub`). Tags are never hierarchical (`v1.0/rc1` is a version). Folded folders are per repository, in `repo-state.toml`.
- **Expand/collapse all and the filter** (`tree_fold.rs`): one `TreeFold` vocabulary (expand/collapse all, expand/collapse
  below a folder) for the refs sections and the right pane's lists; menus, keys and palette rows all end in
  `commands/folds.rs`. A **filter swaps the live collapsed set for an empty one** (`FilterOverlay`, synced once a frame in
  the tick) and parks the user's set; every folder with a match is therefore open, the saved set is the parked one (refs
  folds are saved by change detection in the tick, never at the toggle site), a fold made during the filter edits the
  overlay and is dropped when the filter changes or clears. Folders to touch are computed from *all* the list's files,
  never from the visible rows (an expand below must reach a folded subfolder).
- **Checking out a remote branch** (`refs_tree::remote_checkout_plan`): a local branch that tracks it, whatever its name, is checked out; else, if the name is free, offer to create, track and check out in one call (`BranchCreateTracking`); a taken name or an already checked-out branch is said on the status line. A double-click on a folder folds once (the second click is swallowed).
- **Hide and solo are persisted** in `RepoState::ref_visibility` (load-modify-save, so other fields of that file survive). They were once in-memory sets, lost on every tab switch. Folder bulk hide/solo, and a remote's solo, work **by expansion, not by pattern**: they act on the refs under the folder at that moment (hiding a remote is a pattern, see "Hiding a whole remote" below); a branch created later starts visible and not soloed. **Hiding changes the walk** (see "Hidden refs change the walk" below), not only the pills. A folder is "fully hidden" only when every descendant is; a click on a partial folder hides the rest (no third glyph state). Solo hides whole commit rows, so Stop Soloing wins the title bar's single button slot over Show All.
- **The hide mark is the eye** (the same literal as the context menu entry). Trap: U+1F441 is text-default and the selection's BOLD flipped it to color on the selected row only; ref section highlights therefore carry no BOLD (the leaf bolds its own name span).
- **`.gitpoulpi/` in the working tree carries its own `.gitignore` of `*`** (written only when absent): the first fold once made the repository dirty. The user's `.gitignore` and `info/exclude` are never touched. Only that folder gets it (`ensure_state_gitignore` skips any other name): the git dir and the user data folder are not scanned by git.
- **Where per-repository data lives** (`gitpoulpi_config::repo_data`, one resolver; `RepoState::path_for`, `workflow_path_for` and `hosting_cache_path_for` are thin wrappers, no caller joins a folder name itself). `session.per_repo_state_dir` is a `RepoDataLocation`: `in_git_dir` (default) = `<git common dir>/gitpoulpi`, `in_worktree` = `<repo>/.gitpoulpi`, `user_data` = `<OS data dir>/repos/<name>-<fnv64 of the canonical git common dir>`. Why the default moved: a folder in the working tree is unwanted noise (maintainer). Traps:
  - The **common** dir, not the worktree's own git dir, so every linked worktree of a repository shares one set of ref filters, fold state and caches (`commondir` is followed by reading files, no libgit2 in the config crate). The undo journal is the opposite (one per worktree git dir, see Undo): do not "unify" them.
  - The key keeps its name and takes the old string: `".gitpoulpi"` parses as `in_worktree` (a config saved in full by an older build spells the old default out, so it keeps the old place until the user picks another; an absent key is the new default). Unknown or empty = `in_git_dir`. No config migration.
  - **No `.git` to read** (a plain folder, tests): falls back to `<repo>/.gitpoulpi`, so `in_git_dir` never invents a `.git`.
  - **A shared `workflow.toml` can no longer be committed from the git dir.** The team file keeps working only in `in_worktree`; a committed `.gitpoulpi/workflow.toml` stays readable (and is written to) from its legacy place whenever the chosen place has no copy.
  - **Migration** (`repo_data_file` -> `migrate_legacy`): the first time a file is asked for, the chosen place lacks it and `<repo>/.gitpoulpi` has it, every file in the legacy folder that git does not track (`git ls-files`; if git cannot answer, nothing moves) is moved (rename, else copy + delete), never over an existing file; our own generated `.gitignore` is dropped; the folder is removed only if then empty (`remove_dir`). Tracked files are never moved or deleted. It runs on the first read, not the first write (the path function cannot tell them apart; moving on a read is harmless and keeps one code path), once per process and repository. Switching the setting back later migrates nothing in the other direction.
- **Hidden refs change the walk.** `LogOptions::hidden` is a `RefFilter { names, remotes, keep }` (`gitpoulpi-core`, shorthands exactly as `RepoState::ref_visibility` stores them: `x`, `origin/x`, `v1`). With `all_refs` and a non-empty filter both backends list branches, tags and remote branches, peel annotated tags (a tag of a tree or blob is no tip), skip symbolic refs (`refs/remotes/<r>/HEAD` mirrors its branch: a fetched repository's remote HEAD would otherwise keep a hidden default branch alive), and walk from the tips `domain::visible_tips` leaves: sorted by ref name so pages tile, deduplicated, **plus `HEAD`** (the WIP row, commits and amends refer to it: hiding the checked-out branch keeps its commits; the empty filter adds nothing, as before). System git feeds the ids through `git log --stdin` (not `--exclude`: it resets after each `--branches`, takes per-family patterns, and thousands of arguments break Windows command lines); libgit2 pushes each. An empty tip list is an empty graph without running git (`--stdin` with no input would fall back to HEAD). The empty filter is byte-identical to the old request (`skip_serializing_if`), so cache keys and goldens did not move. Client side, `graph::walk_filter` builds the filter (`keep` = soloed refs that are also hidden) and **one detector** in `WindowState::tick` compares it with the link's (`DaemonLink::set_log_filter`, a generation that moves only on change): on a change the lazy loader restarts, the depth goes back to the initial page, the total is forgotten, the held Log hash is forgotten (a walk that hashes alike must still replace it) and a refresh is asked; the old graph stays until the new one lands. That one place catches every site that mutates `hidden_from_graph` and the CLI's `RefVisibilityChanged` reload. The link seeds the filter from `repo-state.toml` at start so the first paint is already filtered. Every Log answer carries the generation it was read under (`RefreshOutcome::log_filter_gen`, `LogPageOutcome::log_filter_gen`): a refresh from an older one is not taken (`ReadHashes::for_filter_generation`) and a page from an older one never reaches `LazyLog::on_page`. `max_commits_in_graph` and the total now bound the visible history.
- **Hiding a whole remote is a pattern, not an expansion** (`RepoState::hidden_remotes`, a field of its own so a branch named like the remote cannot collide). The daemon's `SetRefVisibility` has `hide-remote` / `show-remote` (names are remote names; `clear` on hide/show also empties the set) and `replace` takes an optional `hidden_remotes` (absent = untouched, an older client cannot wipe it); every reply carries `hidden_remotes`. `RefFilter::remotes` is a prefix match on `refs/remotes/<remote>/` at a path boundary (so `archive` never covers `archive2/x`, and a remote called `grp/sub` needs no split), applied at walk time: a branch fetched later is hidden at once. Hiding absorbs the individual `<remote>/...` hides it covers; showing clears the flag AND every individual hide under it (the owner's state, every archive branch hidden one by one, comes back whole). In the window the remote rides in `hidden_from_graph` as the key `<remote>/` (a trailing slash no ref name can have), so every site that already carries the set (clear, count, the daemon submit, the CLI reload, `Show All`) carries the remote too; the one read predicate is `refs_tree::hidden_in_graph(set, name, remote_branch)` (pills, sidebar marks, reveal refusal, menu labels), and the writes are `set_remote_hidden`, `toggle_hide_ref` and `hide_folder` (a remote root row flips the flag; a branch or folder under a hidden remote refuses with "show the remote first"). A local branch named `archive/x` is not covered by remote `archive` (`remote_branch` says which). A renamed remote carries its hides (`submit_remote_form`), a removed one drops them when the remotes list lands (`drop_gone_remotes`). Trap: persisted names are shorthands, so a local branch `archive/x` and the remote branch `archive/x` share one `names` entry.
- **Reveal in graph** (`ref_reveal.rs`, ticked by `event_loop/window/reveal.rs`). A request (menu row, `g`, palette, or a single click when `ui.reveal_on_ref_click`) is one `PendingReveal` in `WindowState::reveal`; the tick reuses `ui_intent::plan::commit_step` to page the lazy Log until the commit is shown, then only moves the Log's *offset* (`log_follow = false`, selection and right panel untouched) and flashes the row (`is_hovered` style, 1.5 s, a deadline in `next_deadline`). A ref hidden from the graph is refused in the footer up front (a visible ref is always a walk tip, so a visible ref's commit is always reachable); a soloed-out commit says so after loading. The Log's own menus drop the row (`drop_reveal_row`). Submodules and worktrees have no commit in this graph: no row.
- **The status line's `loaded / total`** (`graph/total.rs`, ticked from `WindowState::tick_commit_total`). The total is `GitBackend::count_commits` (the `CountCommits` request, `all_refs` like the graph's own walk, `skip`/`limit` ignored), asked through the generic raw queue so the render thread only polls a `Reply`. It is **not** capped by `max_commits_in_graph`: the bar shows the real size of history even when the client declines to hold it. Traps: (1) the daemon caches it in the same `ReadCache` as Log/Refs, keyed by the ref-file fingerprint, so a plain 2 s refresh never recounts and a commit/fetch/branch move does (`cache::tests::the_commit_total_is_cached_until_a_ref_moves`); (2) the TUI asks once per *ref state* (hash of every ref's kind + target, `refs_key`), one in flight, and a key change mid-flight asks again after the answer lands; (3) once `LazyLog::is_exhausted` the count is skipped entirely, a short last page already says loaded == total, and the bar goes back to the plain `N commits`; (4) the old total stays up while a new one is counted, unless it fell behind what is loaded (placeholder `500 / ... commits` then, never `5001 / 5000`); (5) hidden refs DO change it: the request carries the same `hidden` filter as the Log (the key also moves with the filter generation, and `CommitTotal::forget` shows the placeholder meanwhile), so the total is the visible history; solo still filters rows client-side and does not; (6) Git2Backend delegates to system git on purpose, libgit2 has no commit-graph support (12 ms vs 213 ms on 50,000 commits; git without the graph file takes about 140 ms, so a repository that never ran `git gc`/`maintenance` pays that once per ref change, off the render thread). (7) `log.commit_count` (`"system-git"` default, `"libgit2"`, `"off"`) picks the method, live: the TUI publishes it through `live_settings` and sends it as the `method` arg of `CountCommits` (so it is part of the daemon cache key and a change recounts instead of serving the other method's answer); `CommitTotal::tick` forgets the total and any in-flight answer when the method changes, and `"off"` sends no request at all. `"libgit2"` is `gitpoulpi_git2_backend::count_commits_in_process` (revwalk with `Sort::NONE`, same three ref globs, no commit loaded by us), called by the daemon directly, so it works under either `GitBackend` choice and spawns nothing; the exception is a path-filtered count, which libgit2 cannot simplify and so still asks system git. Why it exists: on a machine where antivirus software scans every process start, the spawn costs far more than libgit2's ~250 ms background walk (50,000 commits).
- **Lazy Log pages are a fixed step** (`graph/paging.rs::LazyLog::next_page`): every page asks for exactly `commits_per_lazy_page` (shortened at `max_commits_in_graph`). It once grew with what was held (1000, 2000, 4000...) and a reviewer read that as a bug; `pty_lazy_graph_pages.rs` pins the 500/800/1100... sequence. A revwalk costs the same for any limit, but the setting is a promise, not a hint.
- **Update check, version chip, Release notes tab** (`updates/`, plan `updates-and-install.md` slice A). Pure logic (semver, channel filter, asset choice, throttle, skip) is `gitpoulpi_hosting::updates`; the archive names are `gitpoulpi_config::release`, shared with `xtask dist` so the two cannot drift. The TUI owns the process-wide state (`updates/mod.rs`, a `Mutex` read by the chip and the tab) and runs the check on its own thread, never the render thread. Traps: (1) `/releases/latest` never returns a pre-release, so the **list** is fetched and filtered by `updates.channel`; (2) the cache file `<data>/updates.json` holds `checked_at`, the newest release and the skipped version. The attempt is recorded even when the host failed (no hammering of a private repository's 404), the chip is derived from the file at every start, so "available" needs no request and stops by itself once the app is updated; (3) a failed **background** check is one `info` line in the application log and nothing else (a private repository answers 404 without a token: the stored github.com token is used when there is one); a check the user asked for says so on the status line; (4) `init` runs on every window restart (a tab switch), so the background check starts once per process (`STARTED`); (5) `updates.mode = off` hides the notice too and makes no request; `auto` behaves like `notify` until slice B; (6) `GITPOULPI_UPDATE_API` replaces `https://api.github.com` (tests only; test builds refuse non-loopback hosts anyway) and the PTY `common::launch` pins `GITPOULPI_UPDATES_MODE=off` so unrelated tests never ask; (7) the chip's rect comes from its paint (`updates::chip::paint`) and the Activity button and jobs indicator are painted in the room left of it (`chip::reserve` / `without`); (8) the Release notes tab is a Home view like the Radar (`RepoManager::notes`, `ManagementEntry::ReleaseNotes`), transient and always the selected tab while open, so from a repository window the window ends and Home starts on it; (9) the changelog is `include_str!`'d, the scroll target is the `## [<version>]` section, `## [Unreleased]` for development builds (a build with a commit hash from `build_version.rs`, which `xtask dist` disables with `GITPOULPI_DIST`) or when the section is missing.
- **Release signatures and security notices** (`gitpoulpi_update::signing`, `gitpoulpi_hosting::updates::{signing, notices}`, plan `security-hardening`). Every asset has a `<asset>.minisig` whose trusted comment is `file:<name> version:<v>`; `verify` takes the keys as a parameter, production passes `signing::release_keys()` (the committed `packaging/minisign.pub`, a list for rotation) and nothing else. Traps: (1) the **signature is checked before the checksum**, in `fetch::download_verified_with` and again by the elevated worker (`updater/swap.rs::stage_archive` downloads the `.minisig` itself, so a swapped file in the user's cache is refused); a staged-archive job carries `minisig_url` and a job without it does not parse; (2) the trusted comment must match **file and version**, which is the only thing that stops a valid signature being moved onto another file or an older release (the falsifying test skips that check); (3) `latest.json` is verified (`SourceSpec::Manifest`) before the hashes inside are believed, so a mirror must copy the `.minisig` files; (4) `security.json` is republished for the latest release without a build, so its comment names the latest version: it must be **not older than the running version** (`VersionRule::NotOlderThan`, a replay guard), not equal; (5) notices are fetched in the same throttled check, kept in `updates.json` only when verified, matched against the running version when read (`notices::affecting`), and never fetched for the `None` source, with checks off, or for a custom NuGet feed (only its `download_base` mirror); (6) the PTY tests cannot sign with the project key: debug builds (compiled out of release and dist builds) accept `GITPOULPI_TEST_RELEASE_KEY` (a public key file's text) as the only key, and unit tests inject keys through `gitpoulpi_testkit::signing::TestSigner`.
- **Section heights** are automatic (content) unless dragged; when space is short the tallest automatic sections give rows back first, then the dragged ones.

## Staging, diff and file views

- **Working-tree diffs use the backend's own numbering.** A `HunkId` or line index is resolved by the backend against a diff it fetches itself (working tree vs index to stage, index vs HEAD to unstage), so the view's `DiffSource` (`Commit`, `Unstaged`, `Staged`) decides what is offered, and a commit's diff offers no staging. Working diffs are re-requested on every refresh and looked up **by path**, never by index: staging the last hunk removes the file from that half's list.
- **The row map** (`DiffRow`, one per rendered row, from the same walk that renders) carries each row's hunk and the backend's line indices. Hiding whitespace rewrites a hunk, so the backend's index is carried per line. Only changed lines are addressable (like `git add -p`); a split row carries both sides; only the first row of a wrapped line carries it. A selection is confined to the hunk it started in: two patches in a row would be wrong, since the first changes the diff the second was numbered against. Any relayout drops the cursor and the selection.
- **Whole-file view = the diff plus the new side's blob**, not a diff with unlimited context: a diff fetched with more context has other hunks, and Stage Hunk would stage the wrong one. Gaps are filled from `read_blob` (commit, index or disk, per source).
- **The patch builder is byte-faithful** (`gitpoulpi-cli-backend/src/patch.rs`): it parses raw diff text, keeping each line's `\r` and the "\ No newline at end of file" marker. The old builder rebuilt patches from the parsed `FileDiff`, which had lost both, so staging a hunk of a CRLF file failed. `select_lines` takes a direction: for a reverse apply (unstage, discard) the preimage is the new side, so an unselected addition becomes context and an unselected removal vanishes; applying the forward rule made unstaging one line of a two-change hunk fail.
- **Discard.** `discard(paths)` handles several paths and untracked files (the single-file checkout cannot); `discard_part` (hunk or lines) edits the working-tree copy only, back to the **index**, not HEAD. Folder and global discard act on Unstaged paths only: silently unstaging a staged file first would be a surprising double action.
- **Conflicts are a state.** The third pane's title says `-- N CONFLICTED`; a conflicted path is listed in Unstaged only (porcelain v2 sets both XY columns, and the Staged copy offered an Unstage with nothing behind it); its menu offers the sides and drops plain Stage and Discard (staging an unmerged path without choosing is how a marker-filled commit happens).
- **File content and blame agree on what a line is**: `BlobContent::from_bytes` in `gitpoulpi-core` is the only splitter, and both backends' `blame` take lines from `read_blob` and only the attribution from git. `run_bytes` exists because `run` validates UTF-8 and file content is bytes. `blame` passes `--no-textconv`. Blaming the working tree is not supported (git's all-zero pseudo-commit is nothing to click through to); `log --follow` is not offered until both backends agree on it.
- **Scrolling traps.** A wheel scroll must move the selection with the offset, or the next frame's `clamp` undoes it; the reachable offset range is `[0, len - viewport]`. The gutter never scrolls horizontally, and `h_offset` is clamped against the longest line before the render (after it, the render sees one frame of a stale offset). The horizontal range is the text column, not the pane (`diff_view::h_scroll_max`).
- **Scrollbars** are drawn over the block's own border, so `inner` and every row-arithmetic function stay unchanged, and are painted from the same `scrollbar::Track` the mouse hit-tests (ratatui's `Scrollbar` widget computes its own thumb, a second rounding). A track click pages; a thumb drag scrolls.
- **File View and Editor are central views derived from state** (`file_panel::sync_central_view`), never set by hand. A commit-detail row resolves by path; nothing indexes the changed-files list with a row index (View all files lists the whole tree).
- **The editor saves atomically** (temp file in the same directory, `sync_all`, copied permissions, rename) and round-trips bytes exactly (see the next point). A dirty editor never closes except by Save or Discard, including on quit and tab switch. The daemon has no filesystem watch; a refresh after save updates the status.
- **The editor's format is encoding + line breaks, both undoable** (`editor_buffer::Format`, `editor_charset`): text is always Unicode with `\n` breaks, and `Format { charset, eol, eol_set }` says how a save writes it. Load: BOM, else UTF-8, else Windows-1252 (shown on the status line, never silently); a NUL outside UTF-16 is binary. `eol` is Lf/Crlf/Cr when the file uses one kind, `Mixed` otherwise, and a Mixed file stays raw (stray `\r` in the text) until converted. A format change is a `Change` carrying `(before, after)` formats, so undo/redo and the dirty marker (text *and* format against what was saved) need no second mechanism. Decoding never substitutes U+FFFD and encoding never writes `?`: an unencodable character refuses the conversion (and the save) with count and first line. A file the decode/encode pair does not reproduce (Shift_JIS has two codes for one character) keeps its bytes (`pristine`) until edited. `editor.eol` still forces a break on save, but an explicit conversion (`eol_set`) wins. The tab width is per file (`Doc::tab_cols`, falls back to `editor.tab_size`), so the editor draws through `*_in(.., tab)` helpers. Trap: `with_eol` is the test-only thread-local override; never `editor_prefs::install` in a unit test (the tests share the process).
- **The status chips are `Combo` popups** (`editor/status_menu.rs`): the encoding menu is two steps (reopen or convert, then which). While one is up it owns keys and mouse (the mouse prelude routes every event, moves included, and clears the hover behind it); the palette's `Editor: ...` commands open the same menus, anchored to the chip, or to the bar's right end when the bar is too narrow to draw it. Reopen with unsaved changes reuses the Save / Discard / Cancel prompt (`Then::Reopen`).
- **The diff and file views collapse the Refs pane** (`pane_layout::AutoCollapse`), edge-triggered; the automatic collapse is never saved, and a user toggle meanwhile is respected.
- **The side panes belong to the Log** (right pane and left Refs pane; one `AutoCollapse` per pane, `enter_view` / `leave_view` called from `tick.rs` once per frame around `sync_central_view`): the first frame with a non-Log central view (diff, file, editor, merge, image...) records the pane's collapsed flag; whatever the user does to it meanwhile applies there only, and the first frame back in the Log restores the record, whichever path closed the view. `persisted` returns the record while it holds, so the session file keeps the Log's state, never the in-view one. Two instances of the same struct rather than one two-pane struct: each pane has its own auto-collapse (`sync`, `saved`, `peeking`) and the call sites already pass the pair. Collapse only flips the flag (the dividers are untouched), so there is no width to save. Trap: the record is taken before a pane's own auto-collapse (merge editor for the right pane, diff / file / editor for the left) and restored after it, else the "before" is the wrong state, and the left pane's auto-collapse alone let a toggle made in the diff leak into the Log (it only restored when the user had not touched the pane); never patch an individual `central_view = Log` site (there are dozens).
- **Text selection** lives in displayed-text coordinates (line, char), not screen cells, so scrolling keeps it; hunk headers and notices have no segment and can never be copied.

## Remotes, hosting and integrations

- **No bare `--force` anywhere**: push takes `force_with_lease`; `force` survives only as a serde alias for the lease. After a non-fast-forward rejection, "Force push (with lease)" fetches first: the rejected push proved the remote-tracking ref stale, and a lease against it alone is always refused (the first version did exactly that). The palette's force push leases against the last fetch, like git.
- **Push rejections are parsed from `git push --porcelain`** (fixed, untranslated), never from stderr; a hook's `[remote rejected]` stays an ordinary error. Ff-only divergence is decided by `merge-base --is-ancestor`, not by git's message. Pull always passes its mode explicitly so `pull.rebase`/`pull.ff` cannot change it. Trap: "merge base == old HEAD" is also true of a merge commit whose first parent is the old HEAD; the parents decide fast-forward.
- **Default remote** per repository (`RepoState::default_remote`); `gitpoulpi_session::resolve_default_remote` is the one ladder for TUI and CLI: the chosen remote if it still exists, the checked-out branch's upstream remote, `origin`, the only remote, else ask.
