- **Leftover remote namespaces** (2026-11, owner report "no such remote: cm6" on Edit/Remove after a fork stopped being one): a *remote* is a name with a fetch `url`. Refs under `refs/remotes/<name>/` (and the tag mirror) with no such remote are a **leftover**: `GitBackend::leftover_remotes` lists them (`domain::leftover_remote_refs`, resolved like `split_remote_ref`, so a configured `grp/sub` keeps its own refs), and `remote_remove` on one deletes those refs (journaled by the ordinary `RemoteRemove` entry, undo restores them; the `HEAD` alias is deleted with `option no-deref`) instead of failing. The root cause behind the report was a disagreement: libgit2 listed a name declared only by `remote.<name>.pushurl`, `git remote -v` did not, so the git2 backend offered Edit/Remove and system git refused. Now both list only remotes with a `url`; a url-less declared name is a leftover (Edit gives it an address, Remove takes its config and branches); git2 sends edits of such a name to system git. `git remote remove` only deletes what the remote's fetch refspec maps, so `remote_remove` sweeps the namespace afterwards. TUI: the REMOTE root row of a leftover carries a muted "(not configured)" note (`RemoteUi::loaded` guards the first frame, when the list is still empty), its menu is `leftover_menu_entries`, the palette's Remove/Edit choosers list it and Edit opens the Add form with the name filled in. CLI: `remote list` marks it `configured: false`. The Activity Log report carries an `action:` line (the failed `Action` and its arguments), since the title alone ("The operation failed") did not say what failed.
- **Tag mirror**: one `git fetch` with an extra multi-valued refspec `+refs/tags/*:refs/gitpoulpi/remote-tags/<name>/*` (the user's refspecs stay in force). Trade-off: the mirror is pruned only with `--prune`. Removing or renaming a remote drops or moves its mirror (a removed remote's tags kept reading as pushed).
- **SSH key pin** ([ADR-0008](decisions/0008-per-profile-ssh-keys.md)): `core.sshCommand` per repository, built by one function (`ssh_key::ssh_command_for_key`) for CLI and TUI so they cannot spell it differently.
- **`gitpoulpi-hosting` decisions.** All HTTP goes through `HttpTransport`; tests use `RecordedTransport` (which also records requests, so headers and query parameters are asserted) and never open a socket. The API root is derived from the base URL, never configured beside it (GitHub moves its API to another host on github.com and to a path on Enterprise). Listings use token-scoped endpoints (`/user/repos`; GitLab `membership=true`, else every public project on the instance). Pagination stops on a short page, not on the `Link` header (proxies rewrite or drop it), bounded by `MAX_PAGES`.
- **The Clone dialog's repository list is saved per (profile id, host, connected login)** in `<cache>/clone-lists/` (`gitpoulpi_hosting::list_cache`, never a token; one file per key, written temp-then-rename, read leniently, capped at 5000 projects, descriptions cut, URL user info stripped, at most 32 files, pruned against the live profiles after each store). The fetch thread sends the saved copy (`ListPhase::Cached`), then the host's list (`Final`); a rejected token (`Unauthorized`, not `Forbidden`) deletes the copy and the dialog drops its list instead of keeping a stale one. Traps: the dialog's display order is always `groups::group` (API order never shows), so replacing the list *is* the merge, and what needs care is the view: `clone_dialog/anchor.rs` captures the top row and the highlight by identity (full path, header owner) and restores them by nearest surviving neighbour, keeping the highlight in the slot it was in. A fresh list is held while the scrollbar thumb is dragged (a swap under the pointer moves the rows being dragged). A Radar target missing from the copy is not used up: it waits for the host's list. Dropping the receiver cancels the fetch (a flag checked between a 100 ms poll), so a closed dialog stores nothing. Test mock: `MockHost::hold_lists` keeps the list endpoints shut (it counts waiting requests: the request log is written after the handler returns, so `saw` is false while held).
- **SSH keys are generated in process** (`ssh-key` crate), not with `ssh-keygen` (often absent on managed Windows images, and interactive by default). The private key is `0600` at creation on Unix (write-then-chmod leaves a readable window), LF always, and generation refuses to overwrite an existing key (replacing one revokes access everywhere).
- **Credential helper**: close stdin after the request (`git credential fill` reads to EOF) and inherit stderr (a helper can prompt, and an undrained pipe cannot block). Split `key=value` on the first `=` only (tokens contain `=`).
- **Tokens are not configuration.** `config.toml` holds `token_ref`; the token goes to the user's own credential helper through `TokenStore` (`git credential approve|fill|reject`) under the synthetic host `https://gitpoulpi.invalid/<percent-encoded ref>`: a reserved TLD never collides with a real credential, helpers such as GCM accept only known protocols, and a raw `:` in the ref would parse as a port. **Storing verifies by reading back**: with no helper, `approve` succeeds and stores nothing. The secret follows the id (renamed or deleted with its integration). No encrypted-file fallback (maintainer decision).
- **Maintainer rules for providers**: personal tokens only, no OAuth, no GitPoulpi account, opt-in per repository; nothing links without a pick, and an unlink persists. The credential-helper fallback applies **only to GitHub and GitLab** (GCM holds an Entra token for Azure and a git password for Atlassian). No token in a URL, cache key or error text.
- **Provider specifics** live in one family spec table (`spec_for`), not in scattered `match family`. Azure compares the organisation or collection root (two orgs on `dev.azure.com` are two profiles); Azure's rate-limit header counts TSTUs, not calls. Jira `KEY-N` references match only the linked key or a cached project key, so `UTF-8`, `SHA-256` and `ISO-8601` never become links. See [more-providers](../developer/the more-providers design).
- **Starting a pull request** involves no daemon and persists nothing; with no reachable token it opens the host's compare URL. `GITPOULPI_NO_BROWSER=1` stops browser launches in tests.
- **Avatars**: source order noreply, Gravatar, then the GitHub API (rate-limited, so last). One API call per author, with up to three of their visible shas, oldest first (the newest are likely unpushed and answer 422). Only a definite "no account" is cached; failures are not. The token goes only to the API host, never to an image host. Identicons are derived on the render thread from a cached miss, so switching the fallback is instant.

### The Radar

Plan: [workspaces.md](../developer/the workspaces design#api--what-was-built-slice-l1-radar-2026-10-14). User page: [radar.md](../user/radar.md). Decisions and traps:

- **A view of the Home window, not a page of the repository list.** It lives in `RepoManager::radar` (a `RadarSlot`, closed in a clone) and the loop forwards keys (`modal.rs`, ahead of the Home keys), the mouse (`mouse_prelude.rs`, ahead of the list) and the frame (`draw/overlays.rs`) to it; every outcome goes through one function, `commands::apply_radar_outcome`. The build without a provider swaps the module for `radar_stub.rs` (same surface, inert) like `hosting_ui_stub.rs`.
- **Off and closed are two different things.** `general.radar_enabled` (config, global) is one more filter of `build_features::offered(Some(Feature::Radar))`, so the palette, keymap and Settings registries hide every Radar entry without a per-site check; `go_to_management(Radar)` and `RadarSlot::open` also refuse, and the latter is where every worker and fetch starts, so a disabled Radar starts no provider work. Turning it off while the view shows closes the view on the next tick (`window/apply.rs`). `radar_closed` (session file, per session) only hides the tab; the commands stay and reopening clears it. Both are process globals published at window init (`install_radar_enabled/closed`) because the tab bar's paint walk has no config in hand; under `cfg(test)` they are thread-locals, so parallel unit tests never see each other's value. Trap: the reopen has to happen in `go_to_management` before the "no Home window yet" early return, since a repository window only ends and the Home window starts on the Radar.
- **Client side, like the sidebar.** `radar::worker` runs the sidebar's own `fetch_snapshot` per repository (so provider resolution, problems and the cache file are not duplicated), four at a time, on plain threads with their own runtimes. A round is cache first (answered at once, `cached: true`), then the hosts. A newer round makes the older one stop at its next repository (`AtomicU64`), and its late answers carry an old round id and are dropped.
- **Hosting stays opt-in.** A repository with no `pulls_source`, `issues_source` or pinned profile is answered without a request, carrying only the providers its remotes suggest (the "Link..." line). Only GitHub, GitLab, Gitea and Forgejo integrations are handed to the fetch, and a cache of another provider is trimmed, so no other provider can show (maintainer rule 6).
- **The cache is shared with the sidebar, carefully.** The Radar asks for open requests and open issues; it writes `hosting-cache.json` only when the cache holds those same views (`remember`), otherwise it would reset the user's PULL REQUESTS / ISSUES filter. A cache read is trimmed like the sidebar's after an unlink.
- **A host that says 429 or rejects the token is paused for the round** (`RateLimited` / `Reconnect`): the other repositories of that host answer from their cache with the reason. 403 on one repository (a missing scope) is indistinguishable from a bad token in `Problem`, so it pauses the host too.
- **Stale beats blank.** `apply_answer` keeps the cached lists when a live answer failed or was paused, and never lets a cached answer replace a live one.
- **Keys are host + project + kind + number** (`ItemKey`; a WIP row is the repository path). Two repositories on one project would collide, and a pin or snooze then applies to both rows; accepted (a clone and its worktree show the same items anyway). `radar.toml` is best-effort I/O in `gitpoulpi-session`, like favorites. A snooze is time (`until`) or a stamp (`updated_at`, or the WIP counters); the clock is a parameter everywhere, and `poll` rebuilds when a timed snooze passes. "Until tomorrow" is 08:00 local on the next calendar day (`SnoozeFor::ends_at_in(now, &tz)`, chrono's `Local` in production, a `FixedOffset` in tests; a DST gap falls back to 24 h). The last scope picked is `RadarState.scope` in the same file (`""` = the open tabs, absent = never picked); a deleted workspace falls back to the default.
- **Actions that need the repository's window travel in a process slot** (`radar::pending`, 20 s, taken once), the same trick as `ui_intent::carry` but with no daemon attach involved. The new window runs it in `radar_pending_tick` once the refs are in, through what the sidebar does: `HostingUi::checkout_pr_effect` (plain checkout when the branch is local and the request is not from a fork, else the head fetch), `worktree_seed` for the Create worktree dialog, `HostingUi::reveal` plus `reveal_sidebar_row` for Show, `HostingEffect::Focus` for Link, the New branch prompt for Start work.
- **Mentions, checks and conflicts** (2026-10-14). `PrFilter` / `IssueFilter` gained `mentioned` (GitHub: search `mentions:<login>`, the list rows kept by number; GitLab: `/todos?action=mentioned&state=pending&type=...`, filtered to the project, so a to-do marked done leaves the group; other providers ignore the flag, so the Radar only sets it for GitHub, GitLab, Gitea and Forgejo; Gitea answers it with the search filter `mentioned_by`, and asks `checks` like GitHub), and `PrFilter.checks`. `PullRequestSummary` gained `checks: Option<ChecksState>` and `has_conflicts: Option<bool>` (serde default, skipped when `None`). GitLab fills them from the MR list (`head_pipeline.status`, `has_conflicts`). GitHub's list has neither: with `checks` set it asks, for at most `STATUS_FANOUT` (15) open requests, the user's own first, the request (`mergeable_state == "dirty"`), the head's combined status and its check runs; a failing call ends the fan-out quietly. Cost on a refresh: up to 3 x 15 calls per repository, which is why it is the Radar worker's `fetch_extras` (after `fetch_snapshot`, merged into `snap.prs` by number), never the sidebar's fetch. The results ride in `HostingSnapshot` (`prs_mentioned`, `issues_mentioned`, serde default) and so in the hosting cache. `Group::Mentioned` takes what is not already Mine / NeedsReview / Assigned (PRs) or assigned / created (issues); `Who::Mentioned` filters on the item's own flag. The row's glyphs are `Hit::Status(row)`, inner-first, a click is a click on the row; the tooltip is the Radar's own dwell (`hover_since`, `tooltip::DWELL`, `paint_box`) and its keyboard twin is the status line's detail for the selected row.
- **Same-named repositories** get their parent folder in the chip (`scope::disambiguate`); two fixture repositories called `repo` showed as one before.
- **Account-wide rows** (2026-10-31, `radar/account.rs`, `account_fs.rs`). One job per integration with a `radar.*` list on, queued **after** the repositories so their pauses apply (same `IN_FLIGHT`, same pause map keyed by `Integration::host()`); the cache is `radar-account.json` (`gitpoulpi_hosting::account`), a hit of the same question is answered at once (`cached: true`) and a young one (`ACCOUNT_TTL`) is not asked again unless the round is `force` (Refresh, `r`; opening and a scope change are not). The Radar always asks with `include_drafts: true` so the `Drafts` checkbox is a local filter, not a new round (the profile's own `include_drafts` only drives the CLI). `Msg::Setup` opens each round: the ids being asked (an id absent from it drops its rows: a profile turned off), the offers (connected code hosts with every list off: the hint rows), and the `CloneIndex` (`(host without port, project)`, lowercase, from the scope, recents, workspaces and cached folder scans, read from each `.git/config` on the worker). `Board::build` adds the rows after the scope's: **the scope's row wins** (identity is host, project and number, case-insensitive), a known clone gives `Item.repo = Some(path)`, otherwise `None` ("not cloned": dimmed chip, status word, every action that needs a repository answers "clone it first"). A failed or paused answer keeps the earlier rows. The hint is a `Row::Hint` at the top of the All view (so it has a selection, a menu and `Enter`), not extra chrome: `CHROME_TOP` is unchanged. `[Turn on]` writes `integrations.radar.review_requests` straight to `config.toml` on the input thread (one small file, like the other saves from this view). Trap: the Review gate is `Item.review` (`review_view::supports`), not `Item.meta`.
- **WIP rows** come from `repo_probe` (one `git status` per repository on its own thread, with the process-wide cache so the Home tab's answers show at once).

## Preferences and config.toml

- **Precedence**: built-in defaults, `config.toml`, `GITPOULPI_*` environment, command-line flags (highest wins), implemented once in `Settings::resolve_from`. **A wrong value in any layer is an error, not a fall-through** (a silently ignored variable is indistinguishable from a broken one); the error names the variable the user typed. `source_of` reads the raw TOML, so a file that spells out a default is reported as the file's choice.
- **`LIVE_KEYS`** lists the keys something reads; `config get` reports value, layer and whether the key is live, and the Preferences screen derives its notes from the same list. A guard test (`every_editable_row_is_live_or_explicitly_coming`) walks every field; a "coming" row is unselectable, not just described.
- **`config set` edits the TOML document**, never a populated `Config` (saving that would write every key at its default and break `source_of`); the cost is that comments are lost. It writes the file layer only, so a one-off env override is never baked in.
- **Two scopes**: user preferences in `config.toml`; repo-specific ones in `RepoState` (`repo-state.toml` in the repository data folder, see "Where per-repository data lives"), the same per-repo store as ref filters and the pending auto-stash, with one path function (`RepoState::path_for`). Every repo field is an `Option`, `None` = inherit; writing a concrete value would silently override the user's global git config in every repository ever opened. `core.autocrlf` and `core.eol` write the repository's own git config through the daemon (`SetRepoConfig`, journaled, allow-listed to those keys).
- **Live settings** are process-wide statics (`RwLock`/atomics, `install_*` at window start and in `apply_live_settings` when Preferences closes). Rejected: threading `Config` into the link task, which outlives any snapshot and runs on another thread. `general.default_backend`, `ui.mouse_enabled` and `ui.image_protocol` need a restart and say so.
- **`general.active_profile`** got the full feature (a commit author override) rather than a "coming" label. On amend only the author changes; the committer stays whoever amends (git2's `amend` takes both, so the committer is `None`). A profile with an empty name or email is no override.
- **Config migrations** run in `Config::from_toml_str` (the one parse both `Config::load` and `Settings::resolve_from` use), and only for a new key the file does not set itself (e.g. `ui.diff_view_mode` into `ui.diff_layout` + `ui.diff_hunks_only`).
- **Layout sizes are user preferences, global to the session file, never per repository**, stored as ratios where the thing scales (`gitpoulpi_session::PaneLayout`, `sanitized()` on load: a hand-edited `divider2 < divider1` once underflowed). Drags write on release; views polled by the tick write only on change, never mid-drag. Collapsed panes and column order are choices, not sizes, and survive Reset Layout.
- **The gear and the tab bar's folder button**: the gear sits in the title bar just left of the quit button (a gap of 2 cells, 1 if tight, so a click aimed at it never quits), the version chip between the app name and the path; when they cannot clear the other buttons they are not drawn **and not registered** (a hit target with no glyph is a click that seems to do nothing).
- **AI is off by default and needs no account**; every entry point is hidden (not grayed) while it is off. `AiConfig::resolve(action, repo_override)` is the one resolution (a repo override over the global action settings). A composer plan is validated against the real change set (every changed file exactly once) and retried once with the reason.

## Sparse checkout

- **Cone mode only**, through `git sparse-checkout` in both backends (`Git2Backend` delegates: libgit2 has no sparse API). `GitBackend` has `sparse_checkout_status` / `_set` / `_disable`; `CloneOptions::sparse` makes `clone` run `git clone --sparse` then `sparse-checkout set --cone`, so the clone never writes the directories left out. Directories go through `normalize_sparse_dirs` (relative, no `..`, no patterns, at least one) *before* anything is cloned.
- **Not journaled, on purpose** (`SparseSet` / `SparseDisable`): no content is lost, "disable" is the way back, and the skip-worktree bits plus `info/sparse-checkout` are not something the undo vocabulary (refs, config sections, trees) can restore. Pinned by `gitpoulpi-daemon/tests/sparse.rs`.
- **The TUI never spawns for it on the poll**: the refresh reads `SparseStatus` only when `<git dir>/info/sparse-checkout` exists (a stat; it follows a linked worktree's `.git` file). It feeds the footer badge and the dialog's starting field.
- **Trap**: the Clone dialog grew two rows for the directories field (`clone_rect` 20 -> 22, hosted 27 -> 29). A refusal from git (uncommitted changes under a directory that would leave) comes back on the status line; the dialog has closed by then, like Create worktree.

- **Settings > Sparse checkout** (`preferences/repo_pages.rs`): the dialog's logic on a page, no new backend call. The page holds no daemon link: the event loop pushes `self.sparse` in (a changed state drops the unsent draft) and takes a `SparseRequest` out, sent as the dialog's `Action::SparseSet` / `SparseDisable`, so the in-flight label and the undo story (none) are the dialog's. **Reapply** is `SparseSet` with the *current* directories (`git sparse-checkout set --cone` rewrites the patterns and re-applies them, which is what `reapply` does), never the unsent text. **Enable** and **Update** are one button (one action, the label follows the state). "No paths means root files only" is deliberately not offered: `normalize_sparse_dirs` refuses an empty set for every client, so the page greys the button instead. Greyed buttons are `Field::coming` rows whose value is a page note (`repo_pages::is_note`, rendered as is, not behind "coming").

## LFS: "initialized" and the toolbar button

- **Initialized = this repository's own LFS hooks** (`LfsRepoStatus::hooks_installed`: `pre-push` or `post-checkout` mentioning git-lfs). `filter.lfs.*` is *not* the test: `git lfs install` at user level sets it for every repository, so a plain repository would look initialized. `lfs_ui::initialized` is the one predicate; the LFS settings page and the toolbar share the status the refresh already reads (`LfsRepoStatus`, daemon side, gated by `lfs_gate`; nothing new runs on the render thread).
- **The toolbar button** (`lfs_ui::shows_toolbar_button`) shows when initialized **or** `uses_lfs` (`filter=lfs` in `.gitattributes`, or an LFS object store): such a repository needs the menu (fetch, checkout) even with its hooks missing. It stays hidden while the status is unread, so a plain repository never flashes it; an LFS repository gets it when the first LFS read lands (the first load defers it). `LfsInstall` touches `ChangeScope::LFS`, so the status refreshes right after the page's button. The palette commands do not depend on it (`LFS: Initialize for This Repository` stays reachable).

## Auto-stash / auto-pop

Implemented daemon-side (`crates/gitpoulpi-daemon/src/autostash.rs`). There is **no `Operation::run` envelope**: `dispatch` runs a checkout, merge, rebase or pull as usual and acts only on a failure that looks like local changes in the way. `general.auto_stash`: `never` leaves the error; `ask` returns the typed `Error::NeedsAutoStash` (the TUI answers with a dialog, CLI/MCP with `--autostash`); `always` stashes, retries and pops. The steps are ordinary requests, each its own journal entry.

- **The rule worth keeping verbatim: a conflicted pop is never auto-resolved or retried.** The record becomes `Conflicted` and needs an explicit choice (resolve, keep the stash, or drop it). A pop that fails without conflicts (stale lock, "would be overwritten") is `PopFailed`, with git's message, and offers Retry.
- **The record** (`PendingAutoStash` in `repo-state.toml`) identifies its stash by the stash **commit id**; the index is only a cache, re-resolved by `autostash::reconcile` before reads, before every stash operation and after manual ones, so a stash that shifted is never confused with another, and a stash removed with plain git clears the banner (`PendingAutoStashGone`). It is written after the stash push (the id is only known then), not before the operation as first designed.
- **While a record exists**, operations that would bury or tangle the stash are refused (`refuse_while_pending`, typed `Error::PendingAutoStash`): checkout, merge, rebase, pull, fast-forward, cherry-pick, revert, reword, compose, redo, and undo unless Start over applies. Stash push and reset stay allowed: the record is keyed by commit, so stashing your edits, popping the auto-stash, then popping your edits is the way out of a stuck state. No override.
- **Popping when the operation ends**: an auto-stashed merge or rebase that stops on conflicts keeps the record `Applied`; `pop_when_ended` pops once nothing is in progress and nothing is unmerged, also when the operation was finished with plain git.
- **Start over** (`AutoStashStartOver`, also where Undo is routed while a record is pending and its checks pass) restores the pre-operation state under one lock, snapshotting the stash's paths first and putting them back if anything after the discard fails.

## Undo journal

User page: [undo-redo.md](../user/undo-redo.md); plan: [undo-redo](../developer/the undo-redo design).

- **Pure core, daemon glue.** `gitpoulpi-core::journal` holds the types and the decisions with no I/O; the daemon captures state, runs the plan through `GitBackend` and owns the file. Frontends only send `Undo` / `Redo` / `JournalState` / `JournalList`.
- **One journal per git dir** (`<git-dir>/gitpoulpi/journal.json`, so a linked worktree has its own; not in the working tree). A file of another version is reported and left byte for byte.
- **Write-ahead**: a journaled operation writes a `Pending` entry with its before state and pins, runs, then records the after state and `Done`. A `Pending` entry whose operation is no longer running (daemon killed) is `Interrupted` and never undone. Staging has no write-ahead (nothing at risk).
- **Three kinds of fact**: refs (one compare-and-swap `update_refs` transaction, config sections and symbolic remote HEADs included), index tree ids for staging, and path snapshots (trees of `index/<path>` and `worktree/<path>`) for discard, patch apply, delete, ignore, stash and LFS track.
- **Preconditions refuse and change nothing**: nothing in progress; HEAD, every changed ref, config section, stash, index tree and snapshot path as the entry left them; strict LIFO. History operations that move files use `reset --keep` semantics.
- **Pins** are refs `refs/gitpoulpi/undo/<ns><id>/<n>`; the namespace is per linked worktree because entry ids restart at 1 in every journal (two worktrees overwrote each other's pins). A pin to an object the repository does not have is refused by both backends (contract `check_update_refs_refuses_pin_to_missing_object`): the system backend's fast path writes loose ref files itself and libgit2's transaction writes any id, so neither got git's own check. Trap that surfaced it: after `git filter-repo` (rewrites every ref, pins included, and prunes the old objects) the refs snapshot and journal still hold the old ids, the next outside sync pinned them, and `git fsck` / `git branch -d` then reported invalid refs. `pin_all` already drops a pin that fails, so the entry is recorded without it.
- **Risk is a confirmation, not a refusal** (`NeedsConfirmation`: already pushed, uncommitted changes under a history entry); the CLI exits 3 without `--yes`. `expect: <id>` makes a request stale when another client moved the top entry.
- **Operations that exist for undo**: delete-file, add-to-gitignore and gitattributes-treat are daemon operations so they can be journaled. Still client-side writes: creating an empty file, the `*.orig` sweep, the editor's save.
- **Rule files (ignore, treat as binary/text)**: every Ignore row and every Treat row opens the same picker (`language_picker::PickerKind::RuleFile`, a `LanguagePicker`, so no new `InputMode`): the root file first and ticked, each folder's file down to the path's own, then the per-clone `.git/info/{exclude,attributes}`. `domain::rules` is the one place that decides the candidates and the line (`rule_pattern`: relative to the file's folder, anchored with `/`; `dir/**` for attributes since they never match a directory). A rule written for a pattern that already has a line is rewritten (`attributes_set` drops the contradicting `binary`/`text`/`diff`/`merge` tokens, keeps `filter=lfs` and friends). Treat as Binary is `binary`, Treat as Text is `text diff`: `diff` alone is what makes git (and libgit2) print hunks for a file with NUL bytes. The answer carries `effective` (re-asked of git with `check-attr`): a closer or per-clone rule can still override. Journaled through the same path snapshot as ignore, except the `.git/info/*` files, which are outside the working tree: written, not undoable (no journal entry). The menu row reads `binary_hint` (a process-wide cache, local NUL sniff until the daemon's `BinaryPaths` answer for the file on show arrives), because attributes, not content, decide. The File View still decides by content only: Treat as Text changes the diff, not the whole-file view.
- **Remote deletions are journaled (2026-11)**: `BranchDeleteRemote` / `TagDeleteRemote` are network operations the daemon still records (`dispatch_remote_delete`, like `FetchRefAsBranch`/`Pull`). Before the delete it asks the server (`remote_ref_value`, `ls-remote`, exact name: a tail match or the peeled `^{}` line of an annotated tag must not count) and records `RemoteRefChange { remote, name, old, new }` in `JournalEntry::remote_refs`; the delete is leased on that value (`remote_ref_update`, `--force-with-lease=<ref>:<old>`), so what is recorded is what was deleted. The old object is pinned (an annotated tag's own object, not the commit). `Step::RemoteRef` runs first in a plan (it is the step that can be refused and it changes nothing locally): undo pushes `old` back with an empty lease ("must not exist"), redo deletes with a lease on `old`; the daemon asks the server first and turns a stale lease or a moved ref into `UndoRefusal::RemoteRefMoved`, a missing object into `RemoteObjectMissing`. Both backends share it (the git2 backend delegates to the system one). Trap: the undo runs inside the blocking `Undo` request, so the push is `block_on`ed on that thread and is not a job (no cancel, no progress); the confirmation is the existing `NeedsConfirmation` path (`risk.rs`), which every surface (TUI dialog, CLI exit 3 / `--yes`, MCP) already speaks. Trap: the tag mirror (`refs/gitpoulpi/remote-tags/...`) is written by `remote_ref_update` itself, the branch's tracking ref by git. The combined "delete here and on the remote" is one request (`and_local`: `true` or the local name) so it is one entry (the local half is captured like a plain `BranchDelete`/`TagDelete`, label `... and on <remote>`); a local failure after the remote delete leaves that remote deletion unrecorded (the callers check the checked-out branch first). A workflow finish reports its remote deletions in `FlowOutcome::remote_deleted` and `finish_record` copies them into the run's entry (so `FlowStep::DeleteRemoteBranch` is no longer "irreversible"); olds are pinned after the fact there (the delete already dropped the tracking ref that kept them alive).
- **Stuck states**: a blocked top entry can be forgotten (`forget`, confirmed, releases pins, never touches the repository); `settle` also runs on reads, so a merge finished with plain git closes its entry at the next refresh.
- **Maintainer rule 12 ("Undo story")**: every feature states whether it is journaled. Not journaled and not undoable by design: LFS checkout and installs, `am` continue/skip/abort, index-lock removal, operation skip and abort, creating PRs or issues on a host, link and profile edits (config), the image view (reads only; "Edit in" of a non-working-tree version goes to a read-only temp copy).

## Merge conflicts and stuck states

Plan: [merge-conflict-editor](../developer/the merge-conflict-editor design); user page: [merge-conflicts.md](../user/merge-conflicts.md).

- **Git2 forwards conflict resolution to the CLI backend**: one implementation, identical behavior. `TakeSide` is kind-aware (a deleting side removes the path, a submodule takes the gitlink without checkout, the stage's own mode is kept so symlinks and the exec bit survive). Refusals are typed (`ConflictRefused`: `Stale`, `NotConflicted`, ...).
- **The editor's save carries the stage oids it opened on**; the daemon refuses with `Stale` and writes nothing. After each refresh the open file is compared with the list ("resolved outside" and "changed outside" strips).
- **Unresolve** puts the three stages back and rewrites the file the way git leaves it, byte for byte. `<git-dir>/gitpoulpi/resolved.json` records the first stages of each resolved path, so unresolve works after a restart.
- **Undo during an operation**: Save & Mark Resolved is journaled and undoable while the merge runs (the one exception to "nothing in progress"); resolution entries are dropped when the operation ends, and have their own cap so a big merge cannot push the merge entry off the history.
- **Whitespace-only conflicts resolve themselves** through ordinary journaled `ResolveConflict` requests, only when unambiguous (a side equals the base, or trailing whitespace only). Indentation and blank-line differences stay conflicts. The operation stays open for the person to conclude.
- **The flow** (maintainer): after a resolve the editor closes and the list is back; nothing opens the next file by itself. `m` in the Diff view was already the layout toggle, hence `Shift+E` for the merge editor.
- **Non-text conflicts are cards**, each choice one whole-file resolution through the same stale guard and Undo. Save never writes from a card.
- **Banners**: one painter (`operation_banner::paint_strip`) for the operation, rebase, auto-stash and stuck strips; it clears the row, picks the longest text that fits and drops buttons from the left. A second banner goes on the Log's bottom border, never over the column titles.
- **`git am`** is its own state: only an `am` has `rebase-apply/applying`; rebase commands refuse while it exists.
- **Stale `index.lock`**: older than 10 s (`STALE_INDEX_LOCK_SECS`) and confirmed by the user; no process detection, which is not reliable on every OS.
- **A merge whose result equals HEAD** can be committed: with no WIP row, the banner's Commit opens a prefilled message prompt and the staged-files rule is dropped while merging. An empty cherry-pick or revert is skipped, not continued.

## Agents and MCP view control

Plan: [mcp-show-ui](../developer/the mcp-show-ui design); user page: [mcp-and-agents.md](../user/mcp-and-agents.md).

- **All agent view state is process-level** (the window restarts on every tab switch): the carried intent, handled ids, the Back stack, the marked tab, the active proposal.
- **An intent lands in the TUI with focus** (focus events `\x1b[I` / `\x1b[O`); newest wins; it is answered `stale` after 8 s (the daemon's own timeout), older ones of a batch `superseded`. A highlight clears when the user moves the cursor or presses Esc.
- **Proposals** (reword, commit message, branch, tag, conflict resolution) open the real dialog pre-filled and are gated by `[mcp] allow_ui_propose` (default off) on top of the UI gates. The user's own confirm is the acceptance (`accepted` / `accepted_edited`); closing otherwise is `rejected`. A proposal is refused while the user holds unsaved work in that dialog. `Enter` is swallowed for 600 ms after a proposal opens, so a keystroke in flight cannot confirm it. Agent text is sanitized (controls and bidi stripped) before it is shown.

## Images, avatars, themes

- **Self-update** (`gitpoulpi-update`, `tui/updates/auto.rs`, plan slice B of the updates-and-install design). The engine has no UI and takes every file operation through `FileOps`, so the Windows swap (`Replace::MoveAside`) is tested on Linux against an in-memory file system that follows Windows' rules (a running file can be renamed, never deleted or overwritten; the flag travels with the file). Traps: (1) "updatable" = the marker `.gitpoulpi-dist` next to the exe AND its target equals the host target; a cargo build has none. `xtask dist` writes the marker into the archive, so the swap replaces it with the other files and `.gitpoulpi-dist.old` doubles as the record of the previous version (no journal file); (2) downloading never touches the install: files land as `<name>.new`, only files the install already has are staged (a binary the user removed is not re-added); a staged marker that is not newer than the running version is discarded at start; (3) the swap is all or nothing with a walk-back log (Unix: `link_or_copy` to `.old`, then atomic rename over; Windows: rename aside, then rename in); Windows pre-checks that no other binary is held open (`FileOps::is_locked`, opening for write) and names it; (4) revert is a swap (live to `.swap`, `.old` to live, `.swap` to `.old`), so it is its own inverse; cleanup deletes `.old` (and stray `.swap`) 60 s after the new version started (`CLEANUP_AFTER`); a running Windows `.old` cannot be deleted, so it is retried at the next start; (5) the quit after a swap is requested through `take_quit_request` in `after_input` (NOT in `tick`: `drain_input` resets `should_quit` first), and the re-exec happens in `run_with` after the UI thread joined, i.e. with the terminal restored and the embedded daemon about to die with the process (Unix `exec`, Windows spawn and exit with the child's status). The exe path is read at start: Linux reports a replaced exe as `... (deleted)`; (6) allowed download hosts live in `gitpoulpi_hosting::updates::DOWNLOAD_HOSTS`, checked on the first URL and on every redirect (`ReqwestTransport::download` uses a custom redirect policy); loopback is allowed only while `GITPOULPI_UPDATE_API` is set (test builds also refuse non-loopback hosts); the token is never sent to asset URLs; (7) `HttpTransport::download` streams bytes with a progress callback that can cancel (`RecordedTransport::on_file` for tests); (8) the PTY test installs a hard link of the built binary next to a marker in a temp folder and serves a fake archive whose "binaries" are shell scripts printing a sentinel: seeing it proves the restart ran the swapped-in file. Not journaled in git undo (it changes GitPoulpi's files, not a repository).
- **The updater owns the swap** (`crates/gitpoulpi-updater`, `gitpoulpi-update::{channel, plan, job}`, `tui/updates/handoff.rs`, the install-channels design). The TUI no longer replaces files: it plans (`plan()`: swap, run, show or announce per install channel), writes `<data>/updates/job.json`, saves the session, restores the terminal and hands over to `gitpoulpi-updater`, which writes `status.json` and the next start shows one status line. Traps: (1) the TUI **copies the updater to `<data>/updates/bin/`** (the staged `.new` one when there is one) and runs the copy, so the install folder's own `gitpoulpi-updater.exe` is never locked and a package manager can overwrite it; (2) the **coordinator is never elevated**: only the internal `elevated` step is, through UAC on the **installed** updater in Program Files (which only administrators can write), never the user-writable copy, and the relaunch afterwards is done by the unelevated coordinator, so GitPoulpi never runs as administrator; (3) the elevated **worker re-resolves the channel from disk and re-reads the policy** (machine policy only, never the user's config) and downloads and re-verifies the `.sha256` itself: nothing in the job is trusted for a decision about rights, and a job schema it does not know exits 6 (the panel falls back to Download); (4) the **Windows lock chain**: a waiting parent keeps the old exe locked and blocks Chocolatey and winget, so on Windows the TUI starts the updater detached (`CREATE_NEW_CONSOLE`) and exits completely; on Unix it `exec`s the updater in the same terminal; (5) the updater stops every session's daemon (`Shutdown{reason:update}` through `gitpoulpi_ipc::discovery`, bounded connect), waits for the port files to go, then terminates what is left of this user after a grace period; the Restart Manager lists other users' processes only once elevated; (6) **`updating.lock`** in the data folder and `.gitpoulpi-updating` in the install folder keep the CLI and daemon from starting mid-swap and are ignored when ten minutes old, so a crash never locks the install; (7) the **marker's `channel=` and `feed=` survive a swap**: the archive's own marker says `channel=archive`, so `install::apply` keeps the installed marker's channel and feed, otherwise the first update of a package-manager install would rewrite it as an archive install and the next update would fight the package manager; a marker without `channel=` is Legacy and the first successful update writes the resolved channel; (8) `Run` commands are the package manager's own and the app never uses a hidden `sudo` (apt, AUR and cargo are only shown); (9) the Inno uninstall key's `DisplayVersion` follows the swap, or winget and Apps & features show the old version.
- **Command alias and PATH** (`gitpoulpi_config::desktop::{alias, path}`, `preferences/launcher_alias.rs`, `commands/alias_cmd.rs`; plan slice C of the updates-and-install design): same rules as the shortcuts (injected `Env`, jobs on a thread, a press during a job queues, Remove only touches what is ours). The alias is a symlink on Unix because a shim script would add a process hop to every start and `current_exe` would name the shim; since a symlink cannot hold a marker, ours = "a link whose target file is called `gitpoulpi-tui`" (anything else at that path is `Foreign`). Windows gets a `.cmd` shim with a `REM X-GitPoulpi=1` line, in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\GitPoulpi\bin` (a bin dir of our own, so no admin and nothing in the exe folder). The alias targets `gitpoulpi-tui` (the CLI and the MCP server look for it next to themselves). PATH: Unix writes a marked block into the shell rc files (never a file it did not find, except one `.bashrc`/`.zshrc` when none exists) and a whole-file-ours fish `conf.d` file; Windows has no place for a marker inside the user PATH, so the registry value `GitPoulpiPathAdded` records what we added and Remove takes out only those entries (the `Path` value is rewritten as `REG_EXPAND_SZ` and read without expanding, so `%VARS%` survive; PowerShell does the registry and the `WM_SETTINGCHANGE` broadcast, and `path::UserPath` is the seam tests fake). Only directories not on PATH are added; "on PATH already, not by us" is its own state (`on_path`) with nothing to add or remove. A shell alias or function (oh-my-zsh's `gp`) cannot be seen from here: the Settings help says so, and the PATH lookup only warns about files. `ui.command_alias` is only the name Create uses: the real state is on disk (`alias status` lists every alias of ours, Settings shows the ones that are not the configured name). **Not journaled** (no git state, no Undo): Remove is the way back. The palette commands open the Launcher group and `focus_action` selects the acted-on button so the page scrolls to it (this also fixed the existing shortcut commands leaving their buttons below the fold).

- **Add a folder to the open window** (`gitpoulpi --add`, `gp -a`; `gitpoulpi_daemon::join`): no new IPC request. It is the existing `UiShow(ShowTab)` sent to one window of a running session (`ViewerInfo` of `UiQuery(Viewers)` is how sessions with a window are found: a bare `.port` file or a script-started daemon has nobody to show the tab), tagged with `UiOrigin::add_command()` (`client = "gitpoulpi-add"`, `control = auto`). The window treats that origin like its own `LOCAL_CLIENT` (`ui_viewer::is_local`): never the `ask` chip, no "shown by" toast or agent mark, not held back by a key pressed a moment ago; only a form being typed in defers it (the chip, exit code 3). The window is brought forward first with the launcher's `UiQuery(Raise)` (`allow_foreground` on Windows), best effort. The code lives in the daemon crate only because the `gp` alias starts `gitpoulpi-tui` directly (no `gitpoulpi` in between) and both binaries need it. A window with `ui.allow_agent_view_control` off never attaches as a viewer, so `--add` does not see it (same as `launch`). The `gpa` alias cannot be a symlink (it needs an argument): a marked `/bin/sh` script (`exec '<tui>' --add "$@"`, one process) on Unix, `"<tui>" --add %*` in the `.cmd` on Windows; `alias::Kind` tells the two apart and a name made as the wrong kind reads `Outdated`. The Settings **Create** button makes whichever of `ui.command_alias` / `ui.command_alias_add` are set. Not journaled, nothing to undo (a tab is not git state); `gitpoulpi --add` is a root flag, not a subcommand, so it is not an MCP tool (`ui show tab` is the agent's way).
- **Launcher: one window per click and taskbar/dock grouping** (maintainer, 2026-10-21; `commands/launch.rs`, `tui/window_ctl.rs`, `desktop::terminals::{wm_class, launch_argv, launch_helper}`, `windows::aumid_script`). Shortcuts no longer run the terminal command directly: they run `gitpoulpi launch -- <terminal command>` (Windows: the console-less `gitpoulpi-launch.exe` beside the exe, same arguments without the word `launch`; a GUI-subsystem build, because a console program started from Explorer flashes a console and `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` cannot be applied to a shortcut target). `launch` lists the windows of every live session from the port files (`UiQuery(Viewers)`, 300 ms per daemon, never starts a daemon), and `ui.reuse_window` (default on, live, read per click) decides (`launch::decide`, pure). It asks the most recently focused window to raise its terminal (`UiQuery(Raise)` -> `Event::RaiseWindow` pushed to that viewer's connection only -> `UiReport` outcome: `Applied` when a method worked, `Refused(reason)` otherwise) and exits 0; any other answer (no window, no daemon within 300 ms, the window could not raise itself, the answer took over 1.5 s) falls back to starting the command, so a click is never swallowed on a desktop where raising is impossible. Raise is **not** an agent intent (no `UiIntent` variant, ignores `agent_control`, no MCP tool: `launch` is in `registry::EXCLUDED`). Methods (`window_ctl::raise_plan`): Windows `SetForegroundWindow` on the found top-level window (`ShowWindow(SW_RESTORE)` first when minimized, `FlashWindowEx` when Windows refuses; the launcher calls `AllowSetForegroundWindow(viewer pid)` first), `wezterm cli activate-pane --pane-id $WEZTERM_PANE`, Hyprland `hyprctl dispatch focuswindow address:..` picked from `hyprctl clients -j` by the nearest ancestor pid. Nothing for other desktops and macOS (Dock). Test hook `GITPOULPI_RAISE_HOOK` (a program run instead of the methods) is what the PTY test uses. Grouping: Windows shortcuts and our taskbar pins carry AppUserModelID `GitPoulpi.GitPoulpi` (set by a PowerShell/C# `IPropertyStore` helper in the same script as the `.lnk`; wrapped in try/catch so a failure leaves a working, ungrouped shortcut); `Status` reads the helper path and the ID from the `.lnk` bytes, so an old shortcut reads `Outdated` and the sync rewrites it, `sync_pinned` too. At start a TUI that came from a launcher gives its terminal's top-level window the same ID and our `.ico`: it sets a unique console title (`window_ctl::title_token`), `EnumWindows` for it (up to 3 s, the terminal applies titles asynchronously), restores the old title, then `SHGetPropertyStoreForWindow` + `SetValue(PKEY_AppUserModel_ID)` and `WM_SETICON`; the HWND is kept for Raise. Hand-declared FFI (no `windows` crate in the TUI). **Limit:** a terminal window with several tabs moves as a whole, all its tabs group under GitPoulpi while it runs there; Windows Terminal also keeps its own taskbar identity if the window belongs to an already-running instance. Linux: kitty/foot/Alacritty/WezTerm/Ghostty start with class `gitpoulpi` (Ghostty needs a dotted GTK id) and the `.desktop` has the matching `StartupWMClass`; `xdg-terminal-exec` keeps Omarchy's `TUI.tile` (its window rules match it); the others have no reliable flag. macOS: unchanged (the `.app` keeps the old command; the Dock already groups and raises). `BINARIES` (`release.rs`) gained `gitpoulpi-launch`, so archives, installers, the updater and uninstall all handle it. None of the Windows code was compiled or run when written (Linux development machine): the pure parts are unit-tested, the FFI and the PowerShell are not.
- **Launcher shortcuts** (`gitpoulpi_config::desktop`, `tui/launcher.rs`, `preferences/launcher_page.rs`): every directory is injected (`Dirs`), every file we write carries `X-GitPoulpi=1` and Remove/sync touch only such files (a `.lnk` is checked by searching its bytes for the marker, no PowerShell to read). Jobs run on a thread (Windows spawns PowerShell for the `.lnk`; a press during a job queues). The icon is rendered at install time from `logo_colors` in the user's `ui.logo_color`; `launcher_sync` (polled by the event loop, debounced like the logo color, run on a thread) watches the logo color AND `ui.launch_terminal` and, at start-up and on any change, runs `launcher::sync_shortcuts`: every shortcut with our marker whose state is `Outdated` is rewritten in place and stale icons are refreshed; `Foreign` and `NotInstalled` are never touched, nothing is ever created. Settings runs the same sync before every read, so a row never rests on "outdated", and there is no Update button (Create appears only while nothing is created). The CLI `desktop shortcut install` rewrites ours too (scripts); the CLI has no sync. On Linux the icon name carries a hash of the picture (`Icon=gitpoulpi-<hash>`) and a new color rewrites the `.desktop` too: omarchy-shell (Quickshell) caches an image by its file path, so rewriting `gitpoulpi.png` in place kept the old color in Super+Space. Windows double-click relaunch (`relaunch.rs`) is guarded by `GITPOULPI_RELAUNCHED` and `GetConsoleProcessList == 1`. Taskbar pinning has no API and Settings has no row for it. A pin the user made is a COPY of the Start menu `.lnk` in `%APPDATA%\Microsoft\Internet Explorer\Quick Launch\User Pinned\TaskBar` that keeps the `IconLocation` it had, so the `.ico` file NAME carries a hash of the picture (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\GitPoulpi\gitpoulpi-<12 hex>.ico`): the taskbar caches an icon by its path, so overwriting one path never refreshed a pinned button. A color change (`windows::refresh_icon`) writes the new file, re-points every shortcut of ours (Start menu, Desktop, pinned copies; marker, else our exe path in the bytes) through PowerShell, re-applies the AppUserModelID (a `WScript.Shell` save drops it), sends `SHChangeNotify(SHCNE_UPDATEITEM, SHCNF_PATHW)` per `.lnk` plus `SHCNE_ASSOCCHANGED`, then deletes older `gitpoulpi-*.ico` that no shortcut in Start menu, Desktop or ANY `.lnk` of the pinned folder still names (`cleanup_icons`; only our naming pattern). The running window gets the new icon too (`window_ctl::refresh_window_icon`). Window grouping (`window_ctl.rs`): the terminal window is found through the ancestor processes (Toolhelp snapshot, `choose_window`: nearest ancestor with a visible unowned window, the foreground one when it has several), the unique-title trick is only the fallback and restores `TUI_TITLE`, never the console's original title; the foreground window is never used as a last resort (grouping a browser would be worse than not grouping). Every step is logged at info (`window grouping: ...`) and applied twice, 2 s apart. `gitpoulpi launch` reads Shift (`GetAsyncKeyState`) first thing and `--new-window` (Linux `.desktop` action `new-window`) forces a new window. Unverifiable off Windows; if Explorer strips both marker and exe path from the copy, the fix is unpin + pin again (documented in docs/user/*/settings.md). The CLI twin (`gitpoulpi desktop shortcut`) is not an MCP tool. Tests/scripted runs set `GITPOULPI_DESKTOP_NO_HOOKS=1` (no `gtk-update-icon-cache`).
- **Image protocol** (`ui.image_protocol`, `auto` by default): read once at start-up; `auto` also reads herdr's `kitty_graphics` switch when running under herdr. Kitty image ids are tracked thread-locally; retired ones are deleted after each frame and all of them on leaving a window, so terminal memory does not grow.
- **Avatar placement**: in the Author column, one picture per row (per-row changes cost only their rows; scrolling re-sends cached encodes). With avatars in the graph, the ink shifts right but text cells, hit targets and row arithmetic do not move. Pictures are fetched at about 3x the drawn box and area-averaged down (nearest-neighbor at every stage was the "heavily pixelated" bug); the cache directory was renamed (`avatars-v2`) so old sizes are not served forever.
- **Image view**: reads only. Bytes are read off the input thread (requested by the draw, taken by `offer`); per-view state is thread-local and forgotten by the first frame that does not draw it, which frees bitmaps. An untracked picture has no git diff, so one is synthesised to preview it.
- **Open in for binary files** (`tui/open_with/`, pure parsing in `gitpoulpi_config::os_apps`): the OS's "Open with" list for an extension (Linux: `mime/globs2` + `mimeapps.list` + `.desktop`; Windows: registry `UserChoice` / `OpenWithProgids` / `OpenWithList` read through `reg.exe query`, one spawn per key, so seconds, never on the input thread; macOS: default only). One lookup thread per extension, cached for the process; the view shows nothing for the first 150 ms, then "looking up apps...". The binary view reuses the picture keys (`o`, `Shift+O`). Traps: the lookup dedupes by full command (`libreoffice --writer` and `--calc` stay two entries) but the image "Edit in" merge dedupes by executable against the user's own list; the image popup merges the OS apps by file name, and `image_view::pane` holds its `RefCell` while painting, so `edit_in::render_button` must not call a pane accessor (it panicked once with "already borrowed"; the lookup is warmed from the pane's `sync` instead). Tests never read the real desktop: `XDG_DATA_DIRS`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` point at a temp tree and the `.desktop` `Exec` lines run recording scripts, one script per app (two entries on one executable are one program).
- **Themes are data** (`gitpoulpi-config/src/theme/`); the TUI reads them through `th()`, one atomic load. Caches that bake colors compare `theme_generation()`. CLI validation lists names from the registry, never a hard-coded list.

## UI chrome, menus and text input

- **Hit registration order is load-bearing**: `find_hit_target` returns the first registered rect, so a row button registers before its row band (the band once swallowed every Stage All and Stage Hunk click), a tab's close button before the tab, every boundary strip before the header cells. Where it matters, registration is a named function so a test pins the real order.
- **Every mouse affordance has a palette command**, enforced at compile time by exhaustive matches in `palette.rs` (adding a toolbar button, menu action or diff-bar button fails to compile until it is reachable). `chrome::hover_coverage` does the same for hover feedback. Key labels come from the keymap (`keymap::current().label`), never a literal.
- **Hover**: one `hovered_target`, set from the `Moved` arm; corner glyph buttons have no background at rest. **When hover changes an element's width, gate the element on its narrowest form** (the REMOTE `+` vanished on hover because the wider hover label failed the room check).
- **Responsive bars** (`button_bar::fit_bar`): drop by priority into a `⋯` menu; the bar paints and registers from the same `BarFit`. **A chosen overflow row is a synthetic click** on the hidden button (`forced_hit`), not a call: the buttons' actions are inline code over many loop locals, and a second copy is the drift this codebase keeps paying for.
- **Context menu key**: `.`, `Shift+F10` and the Menu key (`context_menu::is_menu_key`). The Menu key produces no event under classic Windows `conhost` (crossterm has no `VK_APPS` arm); `Shift+F10` works everywhere.
- **One menu at a time.** `OpenMenu` is one slot, but the remote/push/pull menu (`RemoteUi::menu`) and the hosting menu (`HostingUi::menu`) live in their own states, so a right-click on a branch while a remote's menu was open painted both. `input::handle_key` / `handle_mouse` now snapshot the three slots (`open_menu::MenuSlots`) and close the older ones when an event opened a new one, whatever opened it. A right-click on a row of an open menu is swallowed (popups own the pointer).
- **A click outside a text field releases it**, keeping its content (the Refs filter stays applied).
- **Text fields share one core** (`text_edit`): a caret of byte offsets beside the field's `String` (never wrapping it, so every reader stays unchanged), clamped and floored to a char boundary on every use. Keys are decoded before they are applied, so a field's arm can claim editing keys ahead of pane shortcuts while Enter, Esc and Tab keep their field meaning. Field rects come from the render; a click is applied to the caret only after the ordinary click handling decided who has the keyboard, and never through a modal. `Ctrl+Alt` is AltGr and types, except for ASCII alphanumerics.
- **A text input's width is chrome, not content**: fields have a reserved slot and scroll their text, measured in terminal columns (`display_width`), never bytes or chars (the find bar's buttons moved as the user typed).
- **Count prefix**: computed as a plain `let` before the big key match, not by wrapping the match in a block (`cargo fmt` reindented a thousand-line match and produced a duplicate-arms merge bug).
- `Ctrl+N h` / `Ctrl+N l` once failed to collapse panes while the Editor had focus; now pinned by `pty_editor.rs` `ctrl_n_l_collapses_the_status_pane_while_the_editor_has_focus`.
- **OS pointer shape** uses OSC 22 (kitty, foot, xterm; not WezTerm or Windows Terminal), so every affordance is also drawn in the buffer; the pointer is a bonus, never the mechanism.

## Comparing the Log's multi-selection

- **A pure function of the selection** (`compare::resolve`), recomputed every frame: nothing is stored but the user's swap (`compare_swapped`, forgotten when the set of rows changes). `LogSelection` holds `RowKey`s (the WIP row, or an `Oid`; a stash row is its stash commit's `Oid`), kept next to the Log cursor as before. "Older to newer" is the Log's display order read from the bottom: the WIP row is the newest end and a stash sits above its base.
- **Backend targets**: `DiffTarget::CommitRange` (two commits or a stash, already there), `CommitVsWorkingTree(oid)` (staged and unstaged edits of tracked files; untracked files are not in any diff target) and `CommitSpan(oldest, newest)` (from the oldest's first parent, the empty tree for a root commit). The system backend passes `--no-renames` on all three so a rename is a delete plus an add, like libgit2's tree diff. A range with the WIP row on top and a root commit as its oldest end starts at that commit itself (no parent to name).
- **The file list is the commit-files list under a synthetic key** (`CompareSpec::id`, a hash of the target): the comparison rides `CommitFilesRequest::Compare` and comes back as a `CommitFilesOutcome`, so the tree/path view, filter, sort, scrollbar and click-to-open are the existing code, not a copy. `DiffSource::Compare` carries the target in `DiffViewState::compare`; the open file's hunks are asked by that target, the whole-file layout reads the newer end. A comparison reading the working tree is re-asked on every refresh. Not wired for a comparison: the file context menu (blame/history would be asked of the synthetic key) and the image diff.
- **Swap** is only offered for two rows without WIP: reversing the WIP end would need a "working tree to commit" target, which no backend has.

## File History and Blame

- **The History is a split of the central column, not a modal.** `FileInsightKind::History` is only an *intent*
  (ten places create it); `HistoryView::promote` turns it into the split at the next tick and it is never sent to
  the daemon. The list pages with `GitBackend::file_history` (100 per page, next page when 15 rows from the end).
- **The diff on the right is the Diff view**, kept as a `DiffSource::Compare` state under a synthetic key
  (`HISTORY_KEY`), so modes, scrollbars and keys are not reimplemented. A debounce (40 ms) and a 32-entry per-row
  cache keep a held arrow key to one request; the Diff view's "latest request wins" drops stale answers.
- **A rename commit's diff is two files** (the backends run `--no-renames`): the rows ask for both paths and
  `history_view/rename.rs` merges them into one `Renamed` file, so a row at or before a rename is shown as git's
  `-M` would. A range compare uses each end's own path.
- **Diff / File / Blame is per-view state**, kept while moving between rows; the File and Blame halves read the
  file at the selected commit (`FileInsightKind::Content` / `Blame` with a revision), so they follow the selection.
  There is no control of its own (it duplicated the view bar): while `commands::history::is_open`, the bar's File
  View / Diff View / Blame buttons and the palette's `Blame` / `File View` call `history::set_mode`, and the
  selector's File segment stays lit. The keys (`Alt+1..3`, `d` / `o` / `b`) and `HistoryAction` rows are the same function.
- **One column, three states, two lists** (`commit_column::ColumnState`: Off / File / Graph; `ColumnMode`: File / All).
  The File History's list and the commit column are the one column left of a file's view:
  `commands::commit_column::{current_state, set_state, toggle, select}` are the only writers. File = a `HistoryView`
  is open and its list is not `hidden`; Graph = the saved `Pref` wants the column and a commit's view is open; Off =
  anything else. **Shown / hidden and which list are two settings.** `Pref` (`commit_column`: 0 auto, 1 on, 2 off) is
  shown / hidden; `CommitColumn::mode` (`commit_column_mode`: 0 all, 1 file) is the list it comes back with. A
  session written before the mode existed has no `commit_column_mode` and reads as `All` (what the column always
  showed first). `draw_central` sets `mode` from what is painted (a History list shown = File, the graph = All), so
  the mode follows a History opened from a menu; only `set_state` saves it. The saved mode also decides what a freshly opened commit file shows: with `File` remembered and the column wanted, `WindowState::apply_remembered_list` (tick, before the draw, once per file) turns the view into that file's History, so the pick survives the view closing, another file and a restart (it was only used by `Alt+C` before; `pty_commit_column`). Off is one choice for both lists: it
  writes `Pref::Off` and hides the open History's list (`HistoryView::hidden`, `strip`) and leaves the mode alone.
  `Alt+C`, the strip and a Ctrl+click on the selector are `toggle`: off -> `mode.state()` (File inside an open
  History, whatever the mode says), on -> Off.
- **The bar never moves.** `draw_central` cuts the column (and the History's list and the reopen strip) out of the
  area *under* the bar row (`bar_rows`: 1 for the Diff and File views, 0 for the editor, which has its own header):
  the bar spans the whole central column and `bar_extend` is how far it reaches left of the body (the Diff view and
  `render_file_panel_in` take it). The splitter therefore starts at the column's top (`HistoryView::HEADER_ROWS` is
  0). The bar's items keep the same priorities in every state (`diff_bar_of` no longer lifts the view tabs in a
  History): which buttons fit, and where, never depends on the column. **The selector** (`commit_column/selector.rs`)
  is the bar item `DiffBarButton::History` (measured as `" File │ All "`, fixed width, priority 0, so it folds last;
  hidden behind the `...` it re-dispatches as a click = toggle) drawn as two hits, `ColumnFile` / `ColumnAll`. A click
  is `select(mode)` (opens the column if hidden); Ctrl+click (any segment) is `toggle`. The lit segment is
  `painted()`; closed, the remembered mode is bold + underlined (a coloured mark failed the per-theme contrast test).
  A click on a bar button other than the selector gives the History's right side the keys (the bar is no longer inside
  `HistoryView::column`).
- **Rows: tooltip and menu.** A column row sets `hovered_commit` like a Log row (`mouse_moved`): the part under the
  pointer (`CommitColumn::parts`, filled by the paint) maps to the Log's `LogColumn` (title = message, author, date;
  lanes none), so the Log's own tooltip code in `overlay_menus.rs` paints it. A History row does the same (first line =
  message, second = author). The right-click menu is the Log's `commit_menu` with `log_menu::column_menu_entries()`
  (four rows: Show in the Log, the diff, the file, the blame at that commit, `LogContextAction::Column(ColumnOp)`, the
  History view's own labels) put before it and a rule between; with a multi-selection only the Log's rows. The menu
  key (`GlobalContextMenu`) in `on_key` and the palette's `Menu of the Selected Row` open it anchored on the selected
  row; each `ColumnOp` also has a palette row (`ColumnCmd::Show`, required by the catalogue test that every Log menu
  action has a palette command). The three view rows work through `CommitColumn::force`: the follow step
  (`commit_follow.rs`) opens that kind of view at the picked commit (`ViewKind::FileAsIs` is the file as it was even
  when the commit changed it) and runs even when the view is already on that commit. The File list keeps the History's
  own menu (`HistoryAction`: show in Log, copy, diff / file / blame, compare with the working tree, restore).
- **Moving between the states**: File -> Graph closes the History onto the
  selected row's commit
  (`history::leave_for_graph`: a `DiffSource::Commit` state, the File View / Blame request kept, the Log
  selection moved with `RevealState::request_commit_follow`, a reveal that selects *without* leaving the view,
  unlike "Show in Log"). Graph/Off -> File opens the History intent for the file shown (`history::show_list`).
  A hidden list gets the same `›` strip as the graph (`commit_column::paint_strip`, hit `HistoryHit::Reopen`; the
  cross is `HistoryHit::Close`) and cannot take the keyboard (`pane` is forced to the diff).
- **Trap: the keymap is lenient about extra modifiers**: `km.is(Bind::HistoryCompareWorkingTree)` (plain `c`)
  matches `Alt+C`, so the list's key loop skips chorded (Ctrl / Alt) keys; otherwise `Alt+C` compared with the
  working tree and never reached the column toggle.
- **Trap: a held drag re-lights the splitter by hand.** A held button reports `Drag`, never `Moved`, so
  `hovered_resize_strip` (set on the hover that preceded the press) stays where the press started unless the drag
  handler writes it again. Every splitter whose position the drag changes must do so (`mouse_drag.rs`: pane
  dividers, Log columns, sidebar sections, commit message did; the History list and the commit column did not, which
  left a stale accent bar inside the view's text). `HistoryView::divider_rect` / `CommitColumn::divider_rect` are the
  one source of the cells, also used on release. `pty_history_view` / `pty_commit_column`
  `the_drag_highlight_follows_*` check no cell at the old x keeps the lit background mid-drag.
- **The splitter width is global session state** (`history_split_pct`, 0 = default 40, clamped 15..75), not
  per repository: it is a preference about the screen, like the layout. (The graph state keeps its own width,
  `commit_column_cols`, in cells: the two lists are not the same width on purpose, one is a table of one-line
  commits with lanes, the other a two-line row.)
- **Restore reuses the file menu's `RestoreFiles`** (confirmed, journaled, so Undo brings the old bytes back) at the row's commit, under the path the file had there.
- **Blame "show this commit's diff"** needs the Log to select the commit and load its files before the diff can
  open by path, so the wish waits in `blame_view/pending.rs` and the tick settles it (15 s, then dropped).
- **Blame layout is one pure function of the pane width** (`blame_view/layout.rs`): painter, hit rects and text
  selection read the same answer; the annotation hides below `MIN_ANN` rather than squeezing the code under
  `MIN_TEXT`.

## The commit column

- **A second view of the Log's state, never a second state** (`commit_column/`). Its rows are `graph::LogLayout` (same
  commits, same order). Its selection *is* `log_state`: a pick selects the commit in the
  Log, so the right panel (commit details, file list) and the lazy loader follow with no code of their own. WIP and
  stash rows are skipped (the keys step over them through `LogRows::entry`); rows are hit by commit id, never by index.
- **The Graph part is the Log's rendering, not a copy** (`commit_column/picture.rs`). With an image protocol it is a
  picture: a second `graph::GraphImage` owned by the window (`commit_graph_image`, next to the Log's `graph_image`, with
  its own `finish_frame` / `write_pending` / `keep_under_overlay` in `window/frame.rs`; both can be idle, never both
  drawn, and sharing one would make each re-send its picture whenever the other view was shown). Rows come from
  `graph::visible_raster_rows` over a commits-only `LogRows::new(false, [])` fed the column's own `offset` / selection,
  avatars from `graph::attach_node_avatars` (shared with the Log: memory lookups only), same lane background / tint /
  `ui.avatars` settings. Lanes stay connected without the WIP and stash rows because `grid[i]` is the lane row of
  `nodes[i]` whatever the Log put between commits; a unit test compares every row's cells with the Log's for the same
  commits, WIP and stash included. Without a protocol the lanes are `graph::text_line` (the grid as characters, which
  is also what the Log shows in text mode).
- **Room for the parts** (`picture::plan_widths` / `plan_widths_with`). A header row (the Log's column names) sits over
  the rows; `state.rows_area` is under it. **Automatic widths** (nothing dragged: `commit_column_widths` empty): the
  lanes take every cell the visible rows need (the old 12-cell cap cut links), the title keeps `MIN_TITLE` cells, and
  the author, then the date, give way before the lanes are cut. The width follows the window being shown, so it can
  change as you scroll through a repository with wide merges. **Widths the user sized** (lanes, author, date; `0` =
  automatic, the title is never stored and takes what the others leave, as `Message` does in the Log): they are kept
  exactly, **never given up for room**. When they leave the title under `MIN_TITLE` the row is wider than the column
  (`Widths::content` > inner width) and scrolls sideways, `h_offset`, clamped to `content - inner`; a row that fits has
  no scroll. The first drag or key pins every part at the width it has (`pin_widths`), so sizing one part moves no
  other (otherwise the first drag would switch from the shrink order to explicit widths and re-show hidden parts).
  The boundaries of the header are hit targets (`ColumnHit::Boundary`, `ResizeGrab::CommitColumnPart`) in the cell
  before each part; a drag re-lights the cell by hand from `CommitColumn::boundary_rect` (a plan of the widths just
  written), the same held-drag rule as the splitters. Scrolled sideways, the lanes are characters, not the picture
  (an image is not cut by a cell). Sideways scroll: Shift+wheel and `ScrollLeft` / `ScrollRight` (the diff view's
  gestures; the Log has none), `Shift+Left` / `Shift+Right` while the column has the keys, the scrollbar on the
  bottom border (paint only, like the vertical one). Keys while the column has the keys: `Tab` picks the part,
  `]` / `[` size it, `0` resets (registry context `commit_column`; the palette rows are the same commands). Saved in
  `config.toml`, `ui.commit_column_columns_widths` (part name -> cells, parts left out are automatic; clamped 2..300), global.
  **Seven parts, seven widths** (`Part::slot`, `PARTS`): the Log's seven columns. Refs, Changes and SHA are off by default and, being explicit options, are reserved first by `plan_shown` (never given up for room, the row scrolls instead); author and date give way as before. The parts' order is `ui.commit_column_columns_order` (`order_from_names`: unknown names ignored, missing ones appended in default order, empty = default), its own key apart from `ui.log_columns_order`; `Widths::spans` walks it, so the lanes' picture sits at the Graph span wherever it is. A header drag (`drag_header`, hit `ColumnHit::Header`, registered after the boundaries) reads the *fresh* plan, not the last painted spans, and a move to the right waits until the pointer is where the dragged part would begin, otherwise a wide target flips straight back under a still pointer. The last part's boundary is the cell right after it (`boundary_rect`, drag measures from the part's start); when the row fills the room that cell is the column's right border, and the light stays there while the part is dragged wider. Changes numbers come from the Log's `commit_changes` cache (the column adds its `visible_ids` to the Log's request). The
  message has a stored width like the others; `pin_widths` fixes every shown part (message included) at the first drag
  or key, because with the message auto a boundary right of it cannot move (the message gives the room and the edge
  stays put: the Log's pair-conserving trap). A boundary (`Boundary = Part`) is the **right edge of that part**, hit on
  the **first letter of the next shown part's header**, as the Log's (`log_boundary_grab_rect`): `hovered_resize_strip`
  lights it through the shared `paint_resize_strip`, no rule is drawn, and a drag sizes only the part on its left.
  **Shown parts** are the column's own list, `ui.commit_column_columns_visible` in `config.toml` (the Log's stable column
  names, empty = default lanes + message + author + date), beside the widths and apart from the Log's `ui.log_columns_*`,
  so neither changes the other. **Why config and not the session:** like the Log's columns they are a taste, not a layout
  of this window; `commit_column` (shown/hidden), `commit_column_cols` (width) and `commit_column_mode` (which list)
  stay in the session layout with the other sizes and the last choice. **Migration** (`event_loop/init`, `ColumnPrefs`):
  when the config holds neither key and the session layout still has `commit_column_visible` / `commit_column_widths`,
  they are used, written to the config and cleared from the session (`forget_legacy_commit_column`); the session fields
  are read-only legacy afterwards and not serialized when empty. The writes share the Log's lock
  (`set_commit_column_in_file`, `set_log_columns_in_file`) so two load-modify-saves of `config.toml` never interleave. The header's right-click menu is `OpenMenu::CommitColumns` (a
  `ContextMenu<Part>`, rows `ColumnHit::ColumnsMenuItem`, closes after a pick); its keyboard twins are the palette's
  `ColumnCmd::ColumnsMenu` and `ColumnCmd::ToggleShown(Part)`. The Refs cell is the Log's `ref_pill_spans` over
  `LogLayout::pills_by_oid`. **Max width**: a share of the central column (`MAX_SHARE_PCT` = 70, `max_cols`), not a flat
  100 cells; `MIN_VIEW_COLS` is still the floor the view keeps.
- **Follow is one pre-frame step** (`window/commit_follow.rs`, end of `apply_detail_reads`): the Log's selected commit
  differs from the commit the open view is of (`commit_column::viewed_commit`) and that commit's file list has landed
  (`commit_files.commit == selected`, the read the right panel already makes, so nothing is asked twice). The rule is
  `commit_column::plan`: changed there -> its diff; else the file at that commit with `FollowNote::Unchanged` (File
  View title suffix); a read that fails then says `FollowNote::Missing`, whatever the backend said. Blame stays Blame
  at the commit. The editor has no column (`viewed_commit` is `None` for `CentralView::Editor`: it edits the working
  copy, which no commit serves), so nothing retargets under it; the column is back when the editor closes onto the
  diff. A Diff that was a File View button press becomes a diff again when the
  commit changed the file: the rule is by file, not by the mode last clicked.
- **Where it applies**: a Diff of `DiffSource::Commit`, a File View or Blame at a commit (not the working tree's); not the
  editor. In the File state the same column shows the File History's list instead (the graph hides while `HistoryView::owns`); not a comparison.
- **Shown** by `Pref`: auto (terminal width >= 140), on, off, in the session layout (`commit_column` 0/1/2,
  `commit_column_cols`, `commit_column_mode`, global like the other sizes, not per repository; the part widths and shown parts are `config.toml`'s). It never leaves the view under 30 columns, so
  a narrow window drops it even when it is on. Alt+C flips what the width and the last choice make of it.
- **The way back after the cross** is a strip (`ColumnHit::Reopen`, `render_strip`): `STRIP_COLS` (2) cells at the
  left of the view (under the bar row), the first the pane seam kept as a line, the second a button-colored tab with a `›` (the pattern of
  the collapsed pane strips), painted only while the column is hidden *and* `split` says it would fit, so a click is
  never a no-op. Whole strip height is the hit. Alt+C and `View: Toggle Commit Column` are the keyboard twins; the
  tooltip names the key through the keymap. **Traps that decided the placement:** the view's left border is the
  *pane divider* (`pane_divider_grab_rect` owns that cell, a press arms a pane resize before any hit target below it
  is asked), and the view's bar row is full of buttons that fold behind `...` when narrow, so neither a glyph on the
  border nor a right-aligned bar button is reliable; the title row of the body block holds the path. The cost is two
  cells of the view while the column is hidden.
- **The keyboard** is a flag, `CommitColumn::keys`: a click on a row, the nav badge (`FocusTarget::CommitColumn`) or
  `View: Focus the Commit Column` set it; `commands::commit_column::on_key` runs ahead of the editor and the File View
  arms of `modal.rs` (which otherwise own every key) and any press elsewhere or the badge of another pane clears it.
- **Paging** is the Log's (`lazy_log.next_page` from `frame.rs`, measured against the column's own window): the column
  only paints a "loading more" row.
- **The splitter** is the view's left border (`ColumnHit::Divider`, `ResizeGrab::CommitColumn`), not the column's own
  right border, which is the scrollbar track (the File History's layout, for the same reason).

## The in-app code review

- **A modal owned by `RepoDialogs`** (`RepoDialog::Review`), not a central-column mode: it needs its own keys, popups
  (comment box, Finish review, the metadata picker nested inside) and polls, and a dialog gets all of that for free (keys,
  mouse, paste, poll, render, the palette hand-off). It fills the window over the repository; the palette reaches it
  through `PaletteCommand::Review(ReviewCmd)` (`review_cmd.rs`, compiled without a provider too, so the catalog can name
  the commands). `ReviewView::avail` is the one source of the "why this does nothing" reasons.
- **The diff is the repository's, the threads are the host's.** `review_view/load.rs` fetches `refs/gitpoulpi/pr/<n>`
  (+ `-base`) with `FetchRefspec`, resolves both, takes `GitBackend::merge_base` and diffs `merge_base..head` per file,
  lazily, with `DiffTarget::CommitRange` and `paths` (never the whole request at once). The commits are the head's log cut
  at the merge base. All daemon calls go through the window's `Requester` on a worker thread that polls the `Reply` (a
  `cancel` flag set when the view drops ends it), so the render loop never waits.
- **Rows are lines.** `diffrows::build_rows` makes one `DRow` per screen line (hunks, code lines, thread and pending
  blocks wrapped at the pane width) and the cursor, selection, scroll and hit test are plain row arithmetic. They are
  rebuilt when `revision`, the width or the file changes; a test that edits state must paint once before sending the
  next key, as the real loop does.
