- **Positions.** A comment is `(side, line)` in the diff's own numbering; a context line carries its other number
  (`other_line`, GitLab needs both). GitLab takes no ranges: `Shift+arrows` and drag are refused with a message, and
  `start_line` is never set.
- **Pending review state** lives in `RepoState::reviews` (key `integration|project#number`): comments and the viewed
  files, written on every change (a small TOML file, the same load-edit-save `pin_profile` does). Replies, resolve and
  conversation comments are *not* pending: they post at once, say so in the box, and a refused reply reopens the box
  with the text. Submit sends `ReviewSubmission` once; the dialog stays open and shows the host's error if it fails.
- **Review without a clone** (`ReviewView::open_api`, slice S4a; the Home window hosts it in S4b): `review_view/source.rs`
  `CodeSource::{Git(Requester), Api}` is the one seam for the head/base, commits, file diff, commit diff, a file's new
  side and the ✦ draft's files; `ReviewTarget.repo_root` is `None`. The view keeps working on `Oid`s: the host's 40-hex ids
  as they are, anything else folded into 20 bytes with a table back to the real id. A file the host sent no patch for
  (`ApiFileDiff.omitted`) says "not shown by the host". No encoding choice (needs both blobs from a clone) and no "run the
  agent in the repository". Drafts go to `<config>/remote-reviews.toml` (`DraftStore::Remote`, keyed `host/project`, then
  the usual draft key; an `ai_review` slot per project is stored but unread until S5a). Comments still land on the real
  lines of the host's diff and a submit carries the detail's head sha.
- **Entry points** converge on `HostingEffect::Review(ReviewTarget)`: the PR row menu and Enter (`hosting_ui/review.rs`),
  the palette list, the Radar (`Act::Review` -> `Pending::Review`, run by the repository window once its request list
  is in: `review_ready`), the workspace search (`Alt+R`, `Pending::Review { repo: Some(root) }`: only that repository's
  window takes it). Enter on a PR row used to open the browser; it now opens the review where the host takes reviews.
- **Hook for R3** (AI suggestions): `ReviewView::add_pending` is the one place a pending comment is created.

## File lists of the right pane: selection, menus, bulk conversion

- **Commit details layout** (`commit_detail.rs`): the details box is the `commit:` header, the author (2 rows), the counts, then the message block (its bottom border row is the splitter). The files pane's first two inner rows are the options (sort / Path|Tree / View all files, then Filter): `FILES_HEADER_ROWS`. The list starts at `files_inner`, so row hit tests use `row_to_index(files_area, offset, 1 + FILES_HEADER_ROWS, ..)` and the scrollbar track takes `files_list_body(files_area)`, never `files_area`. `MIN_FILES_HEIGHT` (6) is what the files pane is always left: on a short column the message block gives way first (down to one row). The WIP view keeps its single options row (Path/Tree, View all files) on top of the sections, composer at the bottom; it has no filter.
- **One selection, one list** (`file_selection.rs`): a path set tagged with the list it belongs to (Unstaged, Staged, commit files, View all files); a gesture in another list starts over. A folder row stores its files taken from the whole list, not the rows on screen, so a folded folder selects everything below it. The cursor row joins the selection with the first Ctrl+click, as in the Log.
- **Keys**: `Space` is the stage toggle in Unstaged/Staged, so the selection toggle is `Insert` / `Ctrl+Space` everywhere (plus `Space` in the commit's files). The hook runs first in `keys_normal`, before the diff keys: with a diff open, `Down` scrolls the diff, but `Shift+Down` still extends the selection.
- **Menus** (`pane_menu.rs`, run by `commands/pane.rs`): one `OpenMenu::Pane` for commit files, View all files rows without a change, folders there, empty areas and any multi-selection. A View all files row that has a change opens the working-changes file menu; Unstaged/Staged keep `FileContextAction`/`FolderContextAction`, extended with `Convert(ConvertOp)`. The target's paths are resolved when the menu opens.
- **Convert** (`GitBackend::convert_files`, `OperationKind::ConvertFiles`): the editor's pure functions moved to `gitpoulpi_core::textconv` so the daemon uses the same code. The source encoding is a BOM, else UTF-8, else Windows-1252 (the editor's rule). Binary, missing, undecodable and unencodable files are skipped and reported, never damaged. One journal entry for the batch (path snapshot before and after), so one Ctrl+Z. A file open in the editor blocks the conversion (its unsaved edits would be overwritten).
- **Restore from a commit** is `CheckoutTarget::Files` (one entry for several files). Like `checkout <commit> -- <file>` it also stages the file; its undo restores the index and the working tree.
- **Ignore on a selection** writes one `AddToGitignore` per distinct pattern (each its own journal entry); the attribute rules take every path in one `TreatAs`.

## Failure details, the status line error and the Activity Log

- **The facts travel with the error.** `gitpoulpi_core::ErrorDetail` (operation, redacted git command line, exit code, stderr tail, or libgit2 class/code/message) rides in `Error::Git` (same `Display` as `Backend`, so scripts keep their text) and in `PushRejection::detail`; `ResponseError::detail` carries it over IPC (optional on the wire), `Error::from_wire` rebuilds it, the daemon names the operation (`dispatch::failed`). The CLI's `--json` error and the MCP error result add it as `detail` through a small side registry in `gitpoulpi-commands::client` (calls flatten errors to `String`).
- **Redaction is the one rule that must not slip**: `gitpoulpi_core::redact` runs on every command line, stderr tail, message and log line (URL userinfo, `Authorization`, `Bearer`, `key=value` secrets, token prefixes). git itself already hides some passwords in its stderr, so the tests feed the secret in through the arguments and the fake stderr, not only through a real remote.
- **`FailureKind::classify` reads git's English words** and is only a hint: a localised git stays `Other` and the raw text is still shown. Typed errors (`NotFastForward`, `PushRejected`) never depend on it.
- **One entry point for failures in the TUI**: `run_action`'s error arm calls `activity_log::report_failure`; refusals that already have a sentence (undo, a dialog that asks first) are `Warning` entries that only keep the link's status line. The feed is process-wide (worker threads write, the render loop reads); under `cfg(test)` it is per thread, the window and status-error statics are serialized by `activity_log::TEST_LOCK`.
- **Failures that are not a git action** (clone, repository creation, hosting calls, external programs, AI jobs, files that could not be written) go through one function, `activity_log::report_problem(Source, message)` (`activity_log/problem.rs`): same `Entry`, same status-line error, same palette twins. The `Source` picks the title and the default reason and step (`failure_card` i18n section); the call site passes the string it used to put on the status line, so the Details are that text (redacted), and `FailureKind::classify` still reads clone and hosting messages (so a refused credential offers Open Integrations). Hosting errors reach the TUI as localised strings, which `classify` cannot read: `hosting_task::error_text` remembers the kind of the typed `HostingError` against the text it produced (`remember_kind`, a 32-entry map) and `report_problem` looks it up. Where a function must hand the status line a string, `report_problem_clear` reports and returns an empty line, so the card is the only message (the Activity Log would otherwise also list the status text as an Info entry). Dialogs that stay open (Clone, Init, pull request, issue, token form) keep their inline message next to the card on purpose; the card does not take their keys, so its keyboard twins only work after the dialog is closed. Not converted on purpose: refusals that are a sentence (nothing selected, command unavailable, a name the prompt rejects), the periodic hosting lists (their problem row) and clipboard failures (headless sessions would raise a card for every copy).
- **No card, anywhere (2026-10-29).** A failure is an Activity entry plus a red status line, never a framed pop-up. `activity_log::status_error` is the one place: the reporter (`report_failure` on the link's worker thread, `problem::push_report` on whichever thread) *arms* `(entry id, "Title: reason", raw channel line)`; the window *takes* it on its tick (`apply_notices`: `take_pending`, whichever tab is in front, even when the link's error channel did not carry it), and `claim` recognizes the same failure on the channel by its raw text, in either order. Traps: a tab switch rebuilds the window, so `carry()` seeds the new window's `last_message` with the unread error; an operation still running when the window is rebuilt is dropped with the old link (as before, nothing reports it). Where the footer is covered (Home, Radar, Workspaces: `overlays.rs`; Settings: `overlay_menus.rs`) the draw calls `status_error::note_covered(bar)` and `activity_log::render`'s last pass (`paint_fallback`) paints the unread error on that bar, left of the Activity button and the jobs indicator; the rect it registers is what `route_mouse` hit-tests, so the click works there too (`begin_frame` resets both rects first, so a stale rect never takes a click). A clone failure sets no status message of its own (the dialog effect used to, and would have replaced the red line).
- **The entry carries the remedies.** `Hit::Remedy(i)` / key `r` on the Git operations tab: `panel::remedy_of(entry)` is `friendly::remedy(kind, retry)`, dropped for an action remedy when the entry belongs to another repository than the tab in front (`belongs_elsewhere`: a Retry would run on the wrong link). The window hands the command to the window loop (`take_command`, applied by `apply_card_command` like the old card buttons) and closes. Palette twins: `Activity Log: Copy the Last Error` / `Run the Suggested Action` act on `status_error::shown_entry()`.
- **Which operation produced it (2026-11-12).** A bare `backend error: no such remote: cm6` said nothing about the action. `gitpoulpi_core::OpContext` (operation key, a person's label, main arguments, repository, frontend, request id, job id) is built by `dispatch::failed` from the request (`gitpoulpi_ipc::op_context::context_of`: the label table lives in core, the argument summary reads the request's JSON, so it is in the ipc crate; only a short list of keys is shown, `HIDDEN` keys never, all of it through `redact`) and rides in `ResponseError::context` (boxed, optional on the wire). The frontend is not in the request: each frontend process names itself once (`ipc::auth::set_client_name`, the TUI in `run_with`, last call wins because `gitpoulpi` with no subcommand starts as the CLI), the `Hello` carries it, the daemon keeps it per connection, and the connection's writer task stamps it into every failure it sends (`dispatch::stamp_failure`) and writes the application log line there. That is also why the log line is written in the writer and not in `failed()`: only the connection knows the frontend, and `failure_log` (a poll failing the same way is one line per five minutes) is keyed by operation and arguments now, so removing `cm6` and `cm7` are two lines. The log line is `Label failed (args) [op #12, CLI, repo name]: message`: the tag comes first so a narrow window shows it, and `op_context::parse_failure_line` reads it back. In the TUI `call_raw` leaves the context in a task-local slot of the running action (`daemon_link::failed_op::capture`), `report_failure` takes it (the action's own name when the daemon gave none) and the entry keeps it (`Entry::context`); failures that are not a request (`problem::push_report`) get a named context (`report_problem_as("Hosting request: create pull request", args, message)`, else the `Source`'s label) with the entry's own id as the operation id (`feed::reserve_id`). Traps: the id of a local failure and the request ids of the window's link are different counters, so `feed::find_operation` also compares the label and the stamp (10 s); the Application tab's Enter/click makes an entry from the line for a failure the window did not run (CLI, MCP), keyed the same way, so a second Enter finds it. `ResponseError` is now over clippy's default 128-byte error limit, hence `clippy.toml`'s `large-error-threshold`.
- **The log is files, not IPC**: the TUI, an embedded daemon and a standalone daemon all append to the same `gitpoulpi-YYYY-MM-DD.log`, tagged with their process, so the Application tab reads the day's files on a thread of its own once a second (never the render thread). Pruning runs on a thread at start-up; rotation keeps one `.old` per day. Choosing a level more verbose than the recorded one raises the recorded level (and saves `log.level`); it never lowers it.
- **Timings (Git operations tab)**: measured by the window, not the daemon: `daemon_link::run_action` wraps `dispatch` in an `Instant` (so the figure includes the IPC round trip, and a CLI/MCP-triggered operation is not timed), `link_loop` times the first load ("Open repository"; `collect_refresh` times the graph read inside it as "Load commit graph"), `Refresher::kick` times each refresh as background work, `clone_task` times a clone. `activity_log::timing` owns `log.timings` / `log.extended` (process-wide atomics, installed at start-up and by `write_global` on a `log.*` key). Auto-fetch and fetch-on-open travel as a plain `Action::FetchAll`; they call `expect_background_fetch()` first so the finished action is classed as background. A background line replaces the previous one of the same label and repository, so a refresh every two seconds cannot push the user's operations out of the 200-entry feed. Entries carry their `repo`; the tab lists the scope's entries, "This repository" being the window in front (`set_active_repo`, called by the tick).
- **Application tab horizontal scroll**: lines are not cut to the width any more; `panel_paint::window_cells` slices by display columns from `hoffset`. The bar is `Track::horizontal(hbody, longest - 1, hoffset)` (the `- 1` because the track is one cell shorter than the text width, so the thumb's last position is exactly the end of the longest line); `hbody` is the list's outer rect and the bar row is the row the Application tab keeps above the notice row.

## Background jobs, Cancel and the global Activity Log

- **Every network request is a daemon job** from the moment it is received (`gitpoulpi-daemon/src/jobs.rs`, `JobList` / `JobCancel` / `JobChanged`): queued behind its repository's lock it is visible and cancellable. Duplicates join the running job (same kind, repository and arguments) or are refused (a second clone into one destination); a cancel past the point of no return (a pull's integrate phase, LFS checkout) answers `NotCancellable` with a reason the window words. Network git runs in its own process group / Windows Job Object so a cancel kills the whole tree; the cleanup removes only lock files younger than the job. Canceling is never a journal entry: the repository is left as it was.
- **The jobs cell is process-wide** (`jobs_ui`), like the Activity Log: every window, Home included, is fed by the link's reader tasks, so the indicator, the panel and the palette (`Jobs: ...`) survive tab switches. Every Cancel is a clickable rect painted by the walk that registers it, with a key and a palette twin.
- **One name per operation**: the breadcrumb's in-flight spinner (`Activity::in_flight_text`) leaves out an operation that runs as a daemon job (`Action::runs_as_job`: every `is_network` action but the submodule update) while a daemon job is live in the cell, because the indicator already names it and carries its Cancel. The label stays for everything else (local mutations, the submodule update, a compound action's local stage before its job exists). Traps: client-side jobs do not count (`daemon_job_live` ignores `is_local` ids), and in a terminal too narrow for the indicator (`show_jobs` false) the transfer then has no text at all.
- **Client-side jobs (B11)** are the TUI's own work with a clickable Cancel: an AI request, a workspace walk (Fetch all / Pull all / Clone missing), a create-PR / create-issue send. They are entries of the same cell with ids from `1 << 62` (`jobs_ui::local_start` / `local_finish`, `is_local`), with a cancel closure kept in the cell, so a Cancel works while the window that started the work is gone. They are **not** registered with the daemon: `gitpoulpi job list` does not show them (the plan's `JobRegister` was dropped, nothing but the TUI can cancel them anyway). Traps: a local job's label is already worded (`JobInfo::label` carries it, the daemon's jobs are worded from `kind`); the indicator truncates the label before the verdict, never the other way (a long AI label hid "(Canceled)"); `seed()` on every link start must keep the local entries.
- **Surviving a tab switch**: the window is rebuilt on every switch, so `AiUi` and `RepoDialogs` park what is running in process statics on `Drop` (`ai_ui::PARKED`, keyed by repository root; `repo_dialogs::PARKED_WS`) and the next window takes it back (`AiUi::adopt(root)`, `RepoDialogs::new`). A dropped oneshot sender would cancel the AI request (the select treats a closed channel as a cancel), which is why the sender lives in a shared slot that the jobs list also holds. The answer of a request that ended while parked waits in its channel and is applied by the next `poll`; an Explain popup does not come back, its text is lost. A hosting send dies with its dialog, so its entry (disabled Cancel, reason `Sent`) ends when the dialog is dropped.
- **The Activity Log's scope (B13, then the 2-tab rework)**: two tabs (Git operations, Application) and one Scope box (`Scope::{This, All, Workspace(name), Repo(path)}`) that both tabs obey. `Scoping::judge(repo, workspace)` answers `Yes`, `No` or `AppWide`; **no repo and no workspace is app-wide and is shown under every scope** (marked `[app-wide]` under a narrow one): hiding it would hide a failed save or a daemon error behind a repository filter, and the owner would not know to look. A failure outside any tab (a clone) is reported with `report_problem_in(source, message, dest)`: the entry's `repo` is the destination, and `belongs_elsewhere` makes the card's Details open "All repositories" on it (`open_global_on`) instead of expanding in place. A workspace walk leaves an Info entry with `workspace = Some(name)`; the workspace scope matches an entry's repository against the workspace's repository list (read from `workspaces.toml` when the window opens or the box opens, never in a frame) or the entry's own workspace name. Application lines are scoped by `gitpoulpi_log::Record::repo`: a `repo = <root path>` field on the `tracing` event (the subscriber lifts it out of the message; the file line carries it as `[process:pid|repo]`, percent-escaped), written by the daemon's request logging (`failed`, `completed`, auto-resolve: `request.repo`, the canonical `RepoId`) and by the window's `activity_log` lines and timings (`repo_text`; Home's placeholder has none). Comparison is `same_repo_path`. Default scope: This repository from a repository tab, All repositories from Home (`State::home`, set by the painter), the last explicit pick remembered per entry point (`remembered[home]`); `open_global` always means All. The box is a combobox whose rows are built when it opens (a click on the closed field calls `open_scope_box`, so it never cycles); two comboboxes share one handler (`Box3`: Level, Scope). Keys: `Tab`/`g`/`p` tabs, `s` the box; palette `LevelCmd::{Scope, GitTab, AppTab}`. The feed is the session's: it is **not** persisted (the daemon-side and TUI log lines are, in the day files the Application tab reads), so the Git operations list starts empty after a restart.
- Reachable by `Ctrl+N Shift+G` (`NavOpenGlobalLog`) and `View: Global Activity Log`. Home has no button of its own for it: Home paints the repository tabs' status line (`draw/overlays.rs`: Activity button bottom right, jobs indicator left of it, `paint_statusbar`), and the status button's click, `Ctrl+N Shift+A` and `View: Activity Log` all land on the **Global** tab there (`activity_log::set_home`, set by every frame, makes `open()` pick it since Home has no repository to show). Trap: the Home view (and the Radar) cover the footer row, so they paint that row's button themselves; do not give them a second `clear_status_button`.

## Home, the Radar tab, the `+` menu and the Search button (title bar)

- **The Radar is a pinned tab after Home**, not a page: `repo_switcher::tab_bar_with` draws it after the house and the workspace chip (`NewTabSlot::radar`, hits `HitTarget::RadarTab` and `RadarClose`: an `x` on the open or hovered tab, `Ctrl+W`, middle click, `Radar: Close tab`; `session.radar_closed` remembers it), selected while `RepoManager::radar.is_open()`; then the house is *not* selected. It is still the management window with the Radar view on top, so a click from a repository tab restarts the window as Home with the Radar open (`ManagementEntry::Radar`, as `Alt+R` and `Radar: Open` always did). The house, the `+` and the Workspaces entry close it (`radar.close()` in `go_to_management`). Gated on `build_features::Feature::Radar` (`radar` cargo feature AND a hosting provider; `radar_stub.rs`): without it there is no tab, and the Home header carries no Radar button either.
- **The Workspaces tab (2026-10-27)** is the Radar's twin, not a page of Home: `NewTabSlot::workspaces` (the same `RadarSlot` look, drawn by one closure `pinned` in `tab_bar_with` and as `Fixed::Workspaces` in `tab_column`), hits `HitTarget::WorkspacesTab` / `WorkspacesClose`, selected while `RepoManager::page == HomePage::Workspaces`. Unlike the Radar it is **closed by default**: `session.workspaces_open` (`session_store::set_workspaces_open`, published for the paint walk by `build_features::install_workspaces_open`) says it stays on the bar across restarts. Opened by `Alt+J` (`global.workspaces`), the `+` menu row (`RepoMenuAction::OpenWorkspaces`, offered while the tab is closed), `Workspace: Manage...` and the New/Edit paths of `workspace_ui` (all `go_to_management(ManagementEntry::Workspaces)`, which also adds the tab); closed by its `x`, middle click, `Ctrl+W`, `Workspace: Close Tab` (`tabs::close_workspaces_tab`, back to Home). The page keys `1`/`2`/`3` and the switcher are gone. Home is ONE list: `Section::ORDER` is Favorites, Open, Recent, All, and the one filter (`KnownRepo::matches`) covers name, path and branch for every section.
- **The `+` opens a menu**, not a tab: `tabs::open_new_menu` puts a `RepoMenuState` with `repo_menu::new_menu_entries` (New tab, New workspace) in the tab menu's slot, so keys, mouse, hover and the popups-first hit rule are the tab menu's. Twin: `Tab: New...` (`PaletteCommand::OpenNewMenu`), which hangs it under the `+` when drawn. Trap: running a row from either menu path must also quit the window when `show_management` is set (`modal.rs`, `mouse_prelude.rs`), or a New tab / New workspace from a repository tab does nothing visible.
- **The Search button** (`HitTarget::GlobalSearchButton`) is a bare magnifier in the title bar, flush left of the gear (`render_search_button`, from the rect `render_gear` returns), in every tab position: the tab row and column carry tabs only. It gives way first when the title bar is narrow (drawn only when it fits after the path / "Open in"; the title's `tail_w` reserve counts the gear and quit button, not Search, so the chip and path never shrink for it, and the gear and quit button never lose room to it); it runs `PaletteCommand::WorkspaceSearch`, the shortcut's command.

- **The horizontal tab bar scrolls (2026-11-13)** instead of dropping the tabs that do not fit: `tab_bar_with` draws a window of the repository tabs between `‹` / `›` buttons (`ScrollArrow`, rects from the same walk), the `...` (tab picker) after the right arrow only when room is left. The state is process-wide in `tab_scroll` (like `tab_column`): the offset, and the follow key `(active, tab count, bar width)`. The active tab is brought into view (`window_start`, minimal move) only on the frame that key changes, so an arrow or wheel scroll is never undone by the next paint; Home has no active repository tab and resets the key. Held presses reuse the scrollbar auto-repeat (`scrollbar::note_repeat_button`); a tab dragged onto an arrow scrolls on its own deadline (`tab_scroll::drag_due`) and the drop is recomputed from the last painted hits at release. `Ctrl+N` badges the live arrows (`FocusTarget::TabScroll`). The vertical column keeps its own scroll.

## Palette commands with a key, and `Settings:` rows

- **A key for every palette command.** `keymap::ACTIONS` is no longer a `const` slice but a `Deref` to a list built once: the registered actions (`STATIC_ACTIONS`) plus one `Action::Palette(i)` per palette command that has no registered action (`i` indexes `PALETTE_COMMANDS`; config id `palette.<command id>`, context `Ctx::Palette`, unbound). `command_id` is the command's `Debug` in snake case, so every value of `DiffMode(..)` / `ToggleColumn(..)` has its own; **renaming a variant renames the config key**, hence `command_ids_are_pinned`. `Ctx::Palette` is scoped as `global` (`Ctx::scope`) for conflicts and fixed keys. The dispatch is one lookup at the head of `keys_normal_nav` and runs the palette's `unavailable_reason` + `run_command`.
- **Trap: the list builds itself.** Building the palette actions calls `default_shortcut_actions` and `Keymap::prefix_chord`, which must read `STATIC_ACTIONS` / `Keymap::defaults_of`, never `ACTIONS` (a `OnceLock` re-entered from its own initializer hangs, silently, in whichever test runs first). A `const` slice of `PaletteEntry` has no stable address either: find an entry by its command, not `ptr::eq`.
- **`Settings:` rows** are generated from the Preferences field pool (`preferences/palette_settings.rs`, `palette/settings.rs`), never listed by hand. They are `&'static PaletteEntry` with a leaked `text` (the catalogue's `label` is a compile-time `M`), rebuilt per language epoch, so their values (the shortcut column) are read from `config.toml` again whenever its mtime changes. Edits run on a *detached* `PreferencesState` through the page's own `commit_bool` / `commit_choice` / `commit_text` (`show_page(None, Some(key))` positions it), so persistence, validation and the live `install_*` calls cannot drift from the page. A choice's values are rows too, listed only once the query starts with `Settings: <label>:` (its `...` row sets that query: the sub-list is the palette itself). A number or text uses the footer prompt (`RefNameOp::Setting`), a refused value keeps it open. Never call `override_lang` at run time to get the English text: it bumps `epoch()` and invalidates every cache keyed on it; a row's English side is its config key in words.
- Not covered: the repo-scoped (`Repo-Specific`) fields, which go through the daemon (`core.autocrlf`) or the per-repo state, and the settings rows have no key button of their own.

## Local Markdown issues (slice L1)

- A folder of notes is an `IntegrationKind::LocalMarkdown` profile (`url` = the folder, `Capabilities::local_folder`: no token, no host). The per-repo `IssuesSource.project` is the folder as typed (relative to the repository, or absolute); everything downstream gets it absolute through `resolve_local_folder` before a client is asked (see `hosting_task::resolve_local_source`). A new place that builds a `HostingRequest` or a CLI target from the saved link must do the same.
- Writes are `frontmatter.rs` segment edits, never a re-serialization: a key GitPoulpi does not own, a comment, the body and the line endings must survive an edit. A change to the writer needs a round-trip test with a CRLF note and an unknown key.
- Never launch Obsidian in a test: test `obsidian::open_url`, not the launch. The folder watch fingerprints on a thread; keep file-system calls off the render thread.

## The Kanban board (slice L2)

- Column logic is `gitpoulpi_hosting::board` (pure); a provider declares `board_source()` + `issue_statuses()` and, to be movable, a `move_issue`. The view (`board_view`) never knows a provider: a read-only source is a `BoardSource::ProviderStatus`. Details and the T1 recipe: the integrations-wave-2 design "Slice L2".
- The board has its own load (every state, the declared columns) and re-reads when `hosting_ui::issues_generation()` moves, i.e. whenever the sidebar's lists refreshed (the L1 folder watch, the host cadence). It does not read while a move of its own is in flight: the optimistic card would flicker back.
- A move is applied on the board at once and sent on a worker; the card is shown in its target column from `PendingMove::to`, so a reload during the call keeps it there, and a refusal just removes the pending move (the card is then back where the data says). A second move of the same card is queued behind the first.
- Host columns are labels chosen per repository (`RepoState::board_labels`); the move rewrites only those labels.
## Terminal run mode of local agents

- **Seam.** `AiProfile.run_mode` picks the client in `gitpoulpi_ai::local_agent_client`: `LocalAgentClient` (headless) or `agent_terminal::TerminalAgentClient`. Each mode has its own argument vector (`args` / `terminal_args`, `active_args()`); every Settings row that edits arguments, options or the model goes through `active_args_mut()` and `harness_of(profile)` (it used to look the harness up by command name, so toggles did nothing on an alias).
- **No first-run answers, ever.** The client only reads the screen for `TerminalSpec::blockers` before it types anything and returns `Error::AgentSetup`; "Open the agent" (`ai_page/open_agent.rs`) is the user's way out. Do not add auto-answering "to be helpful".
- **Answer extraction** (`agent_terminal/extract.rs`): a marker only counts alone on its line, because the echoed prompt mentions the markers inside a sentence; the last pair wins. Claude is read from its transcript by session id (`--session-id`), found under any `projects/*` folder; the file is deleted after the run.
- **The screen is tall (1000 rows) on purpose:** `vt100` 0.15 can only look back one screenful (a larger scrollback offset overflows in its grid), so a long answer must fit the live screen.
- **Working folder** (`AgentWorkdir`) is shared by both modes; `default` keeps headless exactly as before (the process folder). The repository's path travels in `PromptContext::repo_root` (set by `from_repo`) and `Job::repo_path()`; a new caller that builds a `Job` without `with_context(from_repo(..))` gets the dedicated folder for `repository`.
- **Cancel and teardown:** dropping the `complete` future sets a flag the blocking thread polls every 50 ms; `PtySession::stop` kills the child and closes the pty before joining the reader (ConPTY keeps its pipe open until the console closes). Two sessions at most (a static semaphore).
- **One pty implementation:** `gitpoulpi-pty` owns `answer_queries` (ConPTY DSR/DA) and `RepExpander`; `xtask::pty` imports them. The two drivers still have separate `Terminal` / `PtySession` types (the test driver carries waits, keys and mouse that production does not need).

## AI code review and instruction levels (slice R3)

- **Layering lives in one place.** `gitpoulpi_ai::prompts::general_layers` (global, workspace, repository: a level in *replace* mode with text drops the more general ones, a blank level never does) and `task_layers` (the action's own text: global, then the workspace's per-action text, but a repository's override of the action wins and is kept as is). `Job::system_prompt` and `Job::prompt_layers` (what `gitpoulpi ai prompt` prints) both call them, so what is shown is what is sent. `AiResolved::action_from_repo` is what lets the workspace sit between the global and the repository action texts; a new caller of `Job::prepare` must also call `with_workspace` (TUI: `ai_ui::prepare`; CLI: `ai_cmd::job_for`) or the workspace level silently does not apply.
- **Which workspace.** `Workspaces::for_repo(repo, active)`: the active one if it holds the repository, else the first that does. The TUI passes the session's active workspace (`workspace_ui::store::ai_for`), the CLI/MCP read `GITPOULPI_WORKSPACE` (there is no window), so the same repository can be layered differently in the two when it belongs to several workspaces and none is active in the CLI.
- **Storage.** Workspace instructions are fields of the `[[workspace]]` entry (`ai_instructions` + `ai_overrides`, same text/mode type as the repository's); both are skipped when empty so an old file round-trips untouched. The workspace form only writes them when AI is on (`Draft::ai` is `None` otherwise): saving a rename with AI off must not wipe them.
- **The review's suggestions are not comments.** `review_view/ai.rs` keeps `Item`s in memory only; accepting one calls `add_pending_batch`, the single place pending comments are born (`add_pending` delegates), so the draft file, the counter and the Finish review path are the ordinary ones. The worker (`ai_worker.rs`, a stub without the `ai` feature) is the only code that talks to `gitpoulpi-ai`; the view compiles with `hosting` alone. A run asked before the request's code is fetched is queued and starts by itself when it lands (still the reviewer's own action); Esc/button cancels, and cancelling kills a local agent (kill on drop).
- **Placing suggestions.** The parser (`gitpoulpi_ai::review::parse_suggestions`) indexes the whole diff by `(path, side, line)`; a line the diff does not show is kept with `anchored = false` and a fallback anchor (the file's first changed line), never dropped. A side that is wrong but exists on the other column is corrected. The view's `Item::place` is `None` only when the file is not in the diff: Accept then says so and the item stays listed.
- **Palette**: the review's ✦ rows carry their sparkle inside the label ("Review: ✦ Draft Review...", as the maintainer wrote it), so they are not `PaletteCommand::is_ai` (that test pins "AI: " labels and the leading sparkle); `needs_ai` hides them while AI is off.
- **Review options (S5b).** `s` opens `review_view/options.rs` instead of asking; the dialog is built from `options::load_defaults` (global `[ai.review]`, the repository's own values from `repo-state.toml` or the project's entry of `remote-reviews.toml`, and the profile's kind, which disables "run in the repository" for HTTP profiles and without a clone). It does not depend on the `ai` feature (no `gitpoulpi_ai` types), so the view still compiles without it. The choices travel as `AskInput::per_run: RepoAiReview` (every field `Some`, the run choice `None` when disabled) and the worker resolves global, repository, per-run in that order. "Save as defaults" pins a field only when it differs from the global value or was already pinned. Context problems and cut notes come from `gather_report` and are listed with the cut/left-out notes on the draft card. There is no file picker in the codebase (the folder picker only chooses folders), so the extra file is typed or pasted. Settings rows are `AiTarget::Review(scope, row)` in `preferences/ai_page/review_rows.rs`; the repository rows are tri-state comboboxes (`Use global (value)` / On / Off), and an empty repository extra file inherits (it cannot switch off a global one).
- **Markdown**: `markdown_lite` is a display helper (lenient, keeps every word); the Overview uses it for the description, conversation comments and review bodies. Thread text in the diff pane is still plain (one style per row).

## Tab order

- The tab list is the session file's own order (`GlobalSession::tabs`), written by the TUI through `session_store`, not daemon state (ADR-0006: a tab list belongs to a window). `GlobalSession::move_tab` keeps each tab's `active` flag, so the focused repository stays focused wherever it lands.
- Every reorder (bar drag, picker row drag, picker `Alt+Up/Down`, palette `Tab: Move Left/Right`) ends in `tabs::reorder_tab`; none arms a repository switch.
- Pinned chips are not `RepoTab`s, so they cannot move or be passed; a drop left of the first tab slot is slot 0.
- A press on a repository tab now only arms a `TabDrag`; the *focus* happens on release when the pointer never passed `DRAG_THRESHOLD`. Do not focus on the press again: the focus restarts the window and kills the drag.
- Ctrl+Up/Down are the picker's navigation (`popup_list::nav`), hence Alt for reordering there.

## Testing in a PTY

The TUI is verified in a pseudo-terminal from Rust (`xtask::pty`, used by `crates/gitpoulpi-tui/tests/pty_*.rs` and by `cargo run -p xtask -- tui <script>`); see [visual-verification.md](../developer/visual-verification.md) and [testing.md](../developer/testing.md).

- **Why not the desktop**: one pointer, one focus and one screen are global state shared with other agents and the user. A real window is still needed for pixels: font metrics, emoji presentation, the OS pointer, image protocols. Use `cargo run -p xtask -- desktop-lock -- <command>` for those.
- **Crates**: `portable-pty` (a pty on Unix, ConPTY on Windows, so the driver has no `cfg`) and `vt100` 0.15, pinned because 0.16 needs `unicode-width ^0.2.1` while ratatui 0.29 pins `=0.2.0` (revisit when ratatui relaxes it).
- **Driver rules now in code**: one write per escape sequence (crossterm tells a lone `ESC` from a sequence by timing); zero-based `(col, row)`, converted to SGR once; `wait_until(predicate)`, never `sleep`; quiescence is the wrong primitive (the TUI redraws on a tick), so `wait_for_stable` watches the grid.
- **A terminal has no frame boundaries.** A snapshot can be half old frame, half new. **Put the whole post-condition in one predicate**, assert nothing transient after a wait, and run any new pty test under `GITPOULPI_PTY_TEAR_MS=10` (small, slow reads) before trusting it.
- **Windows ConPTY gap, closed (2026-09-22).** Three bugs, one of which only the GitHub runner showed. (1) ConPTY writes a 27-byte preamble before the child runs; counting it as the child's output let waits return on a blank grid, which read as "the TUI is silent under ConPTY". The driver charges bytes to the preamble until the grid paints (`bytes_drawn`). (2) Shutdown must drop the writer and the master before joining the reader (ConPTY holds the pipe until the master drops: a three-way deadlock that cost 3h58m of CI); the join is bounded anyway (`READER_GRACE`), because **a test that can hang is worse than one that fails**. (3) The preamble asks DSR (`ESC[6n`) and Device Attributes (`ESC[c`), and on the runner's console host **the child does not start until they are answered** (microsoft/terminal#1810); `answer_queries` replies as a terminal would. Not a `cfg(windows)` workaround: terminals answer these, while the common canned-reply hack injects bytes no terminal sends. Lessons: the earlier skip gate encoded a wrong hypothesis as a fix and switched four real tests off on the main platform; "passes on my Windows desktop" is not evidence about a different console host; fix the error reporting first, and the runner names the cause.
- **`cfg(unix)` on a test is a platform losing coverage**: the driver's self-tests use `xtask emit <hex>` as a predictable child instead of `/bin/sh`, and there is no `cfg` in `xtask/` or the test files. Assert that an attribute reached the grid, not a palette index (ConPTY re-encodes palette colors); truecolor has its own test.
- **Test isolation**: each pty test has its own fixture repository, `GITPOULPI_CONFIG_DIR` and `-s` session, finds clicks by searching the screen for labels, and runs with `GITPOULPI_TERMINAL_IMAGE=off` (pixel protocols are checked by hand). The system backend's `log` turns any `git log` failure into an empty graph (an empty repository legitimately fails), so a test that must notice a missing `git` uses `status`, which propagates.
- **Contract tests must be falsified**: a blame test that only appended a line passed while git2 ignored the revision entirely; a cherry-pick onto an unmoved branch produces the same oid as its source and asserts nothing. A config-dependent bug (`diff.mnemonicPrefix = true` on a developer machine) was invisible to CI, which has no user config: `SystemGitBackend` pins only output *shape* (`-c` overrides for prefixes, color, quoting, signatures, relative paths and encoding; `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` on diffs; the `GIT_DIR` family cleared), never signing, hooks or credentials, which stay the user's. The fixture does the opposite and cuts itself off from global config.

## Left pane section visibility

Per repository since 2026-10-21: `RepoState::sidebar_sections_hidden` (`Option<Vec<String>>`: `None` is "no choice of its own", `Some(vec![])` is "show everything here"), restored when the tab's window is built. `ui.sidebar_sections_hidden` is only the default for repositories with no choice; it is written solely by "Use for all repositories". `sections_scope::SectionsScope` remembers which of the two a window follows and what was last saved, so the tick writes a change to the right file (a toggle marks the set as the repo's own; "Reset to default" drops it and re-reads the default). The window keeps one effective hidden set; each window saves from the tick, so a tab switch (which rebuilds the window) shows each repo's own sections at once. `ui.sidebar_sections_scope = global` (live setting, `live_settings::sidebar_sections_global`) makes the window follow `default` only: a toggle writes `ui.sidebar_sections_hidden`, the repo's stored choice is kept in memory and in its state file untouched, and the tick swaps the effective set when the setting flips (`SectionsScope::follow_scope`). Trap: a default changed in one tab reaches another window only when that window is rebuilt. The single choke point is `visible_sections_with(.., hidden)`: painting, keyboard cycling (`visible_sidebar` is read back from the painted areas) and hit rects all follow it, so a hidden section needs no other guard. Traps: (1) the pane never goes blank - if the hidden set covers every section that exists *here* (a hand-edited config, or sections like Stashes that vanished), all available ones show; (2) the "last one stays" rule counts available sections (`can_hide`), not all eight; (3) the checklist is a `RefMenu` keyed by `sections_menu::MENU_KEY`, so hits, hover and keys are the ref menu's own, and its two actions are intercepted before `take_ref_menu` to keep the menu open (checklist convention); (4) every `RefContextAction` needs a palette twin (`every_mouse_affordance_maps_to_a_palette_command`), hence `ToggleSidebarSection` / `ResetSidebarSections` palette commands.

A combobox with a search line (more than 7 options) used to need a second click to pick; lists up to 12 rows now pick on the first click (`CLICK_PICKS_UP_TO`), because an 8-option list (the old log-retention presets) read as "the click does nothing".

## Commit, Merge options and Message template settings (repo-settings slice 1)

- **Storage.** Five `Option<bool>` in `RepoPreferences` (`push_after_commit`, `skip_hooks_by_default`, `squash_merge`, `template_apply`, `template_strip_comments`; absent = off) in `repo-state.toml`, plus `commit-template.txt` beside it. `gitpoulpi_session::commit_template` owns the file helpers (`template_path`, `split_template` / `join_template`: first line = Summary, the rest after one blank line = Description). `commit.template` and `core.commentChar` joined `REPO_CONFIG_KEYS` (free text, journaled `SetRepoConfig` like `user.name`), so the Save button's request is an ordinary undoable action and the daemon reports both keys in `RepoConfig`.
- **Hooks (trap).** libgit2 runs no hooks, so before this a `Git2Backend` commit ignored a repository's `pre-commit`. Now `Git2Backend::commit` hands any repository with an executable `pre-commit` / `prepare-commit-msg` / `commit-msg` / `post-commit` (in `core.hooksPath` or the common git dir's `hooks`) to the system git, with `CommitOptions::no_verify` -> `--no-verify`. `--no-verify` skips only `pre-commit` and `commit-msg`; the other two still run, which is why the lookup ignores `no_verify`. Every `GitBackend::commit` caller now runs hooks (the flow's squash commit too, as `git commit` would); reword re-creates commits itself and runs none. Contract test `check_commit_runs_hooks_unless_no_verify` (Unix: needs an `sh` hook).
- **Squash is already in the backends.** `MergeOptions { squash, no_commit }` stages without committing (`merge --squash`, no `MERGE_HEAD`, git leaves `SQUASH_MSG`); the contract test `check_merge_squash_without_commit_stages_the_changes` pins it on both backends. **Undo decision: not undoable.** Nothing moves (no ref, no HEAD), so the journal records no entry and `Undo` right after refuses with `NothingToUndo` instead of rewinding an earlier entry (`undo_history.rs::a_squash_left_staged_is_not_undoable_...`). Backing out is discarding the staged changes. The TUI reads `squash_merge` from the repo data in the action task (`run_action`, never the render thread) and the CLI has an explicit `merge --squash`; the setting is the TUI's default only.
- **Push after commit** is one `Action::Commit { push: true }`: the commit request, then (only if it succeeded) a `Refs` read to find the checked-out branch's upstream remote and the ordinary `Push` request (`push_now`), so the job tracking, rejection card and in-flight text ("committing, then pushing...") are the push's own. No upstream: nothing is pushed and `committed_not_pushed` is the notice (the first push of a branch stays the Push button's, which sets the upstream). It counts as a network action (`is_network`) so it runs spawned.
- **Composer.** `commit_settings::CommitSettings` is reloaded when git's config is reported (the template is `commit.template`, known only after the daemon read) and when Settings closes (`window/commit_prefill.rs`); never per frame. An empty composer is prefilled once per commit cycle (`commit_template_applied`; reset by a commit, an emptied composer is respected), and a squash's `SQUASH_MSG` once per modification time (comment lines dropped). Comment stripping happens in `composer::submit_commit` and the commit prompt, because commits use `-m` and git does not clean those up. Skip hooks is `commit_skip_hooks` (`HitTarget::SkipHooksCheckbox`, `Shift+H`, palette `Commit: Toggle Skip Hooks`); it returns to the setting's default after each commit and is drawn only when the Amend row has room beside the (short) AI Compose button, which keeps its room first.
- **Settings pages** (`preferences/commit_pages.rs`): toggles are `Target::Repo(RepoField::..)` rows saved at once; the template page edits a `TemplateDraft` (summary row = `Control::Text`, whose label carries the live 72-column counter; description = the `repo_pages` multi-line editor with a target). If `commit.template` names another file (relative = from the work tree, `~/` expanded) the page shows and edits that file and never rewrites the setting; otherwise Save writes the own file and queues `SetRepoConfig`. A config refresh reloads the draft only when it has no unsaved edits.

## Conflict prevention (repo-settings slice 3)

- **Backend op.** `GitBackend::predict_merge_conflicts(ours, theirs) -> sorted paths`, a read that touches no work tree, index or ref. `Git2Backend`: `merge_commits` with rename detection on, then the index's conflicts (our / their / ancestor path). `SystemGitBackend`: `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only -z` (exit 1 = conflicts; git older than 2.38 is `Error::Unsupported`, which the daemon turns into status `disabled` and one `info` log line). Contract tests (`contract/predict.rs`) pin the same set on both: content conflict, add/add, modify/delete, and a rename on one side with an edit on the other (clean on both: libgit2 needs `find_renames(true)`, falsified). Both can write unreferenced blobs and trees into the object database (`gc` collects them). `merge-tree` gets two commits, so a work-tree-dirty state never matters.
- **Pure parts in core** (`domain/conflict_prevention.rs`): the Base branches globs (`**` crosses `/`, `*` and `?` stay in a segment, `!` excludes and wins, only exclusions = everything else, blank and `#` lines dropped) and `pick_base` (fewest `ahead`, then fewest `behind`, then remote over local, then name: a total order, so refs order never matters). `ahead` is commits of HEAD not in the candidate, from the existing `ahead_behind`.
- **Daemon** (`gitpoulpi-daemon/src/conflict_prevention.rs`, `OperationKind::PredictConflicts`, a read). Settings are read per request (`general.conflict_detection` AND the repo switch; the globs from `repo-state.toml`). Base = target of the open or draft pull request whose source is the branch, from `hosting-cache.json` (the TUI's snapshot, read with a lenient mirror; no hosting dependency, no network), else `pick_base`. The branch itself, its upstream and same-named remote copies are never candidates; 300 candidates at most. Cache by `(head, base)` holds only the files: which branch was the base, and where it came from, is the current request's answer (a cached answer once showed the previous glob's source). Distances `(tip, base)` are memoised (4096 per repo) because finding the base is the cost, not the merge (`perf-budgets.md`). One merge at a time per repo (`busy`). `args.debounce_ms` > 0 sends the request to its own task before any repo lock (`server.rs`): it waits, a newer debounced request supersedes it (`Superseded`, nothing computed), and a merge finished for a superseded request is still cached but not answered. A request with no debounce (CLI, MCP) never supersedes anyone. **Trap**: a debounced request must never sit under the shared repo lock, or a mutation would wait 2 s behind it.
- **TUI.** `conflict_badge.rs` asks once per key (every branch tip + a settings generation bumped when Settings closes) through the raw requester, like the commit total, and shows the strip only when the answer's `head` is the HEAD checked out now, so a stale badge cannot linger for a frame. The strip is on the Log's next free banner row (`operation_banner::strip_rect`; it yields to the operation, rebase, am, auto-stash and stale-lock banners, and is not drawn when the Log has no room). `conflict_popup.rs` is a modal like `revert_mainline` (static state, rects from the paint walk, keys for everything). A detached HEAD asks nothing. The palette command `Conflicts: Show predicted conflicts` is the twin of the click. The popup's two actions submit the same `Action::Merge` / `Action::Rebase` as the base branch's own ref-menu rows (journaled, undoable), so no new destructive path exists.

## Log retention

`log.retention_days` is 1 to 3650 (ten years; default 7); `LogConfig::retention_days_clamped` is the only reader, so a hand-edited 0 or 100000 still loads. The stored value is always days. Settings shows it as a number and a unit on one line (`Control::Quantity`, `preferences/quantity.rs`): `log.retention_unit` (`auto`, `days`, `weeks`, `months`, `years`) is display only, and `LogConfig::retention_quantity` reads the pair back (the stored unit when it divides the days, else the largest unit that does, so an old config with only `retention_days = 90` shows "3 months"). A week is 7 days, a month 30 and a year 365: fixed, so "2 months" is the same 60 days whenever it is applied. Settings writes the days first, then the unit, so an older GitPoulpi that ignores the unit still reads the right days; picking a unit that would overflow the maximum cuts the number instead of refusing. Disk use stays bounded: a day's file is capped by `log.max_file_mb` and rotates to one backup, so the worst case is about `2 x max_file_mb x retention_days` (about 36 GiB at the extremes, 5 MiB and ten years, only when every day hits the cap); pruning runs at startup on its own thread.

## Optional features carved out of `hosting` (custom builds)

`radar`, `mermaid`, `board` and `review` are cargo features of the tui, commands, cli and mcp crates (all in `full`; `xtask build-custom --no-<name>`, one `full minus <name>` run in `xtask features`). Decisions and traps:

- `radar`, `board` and `review` do **not** imply `hosting` in Cargo (that flag means "at least one provider" and must never be enabled alone). The effective flag is `build_features::RADAR` / `BOARD` / `REVIEW` = feature AND `hosting`, so a build with one of them and no integration is simply without it.
- Gating is by tags, not by `#[cfg]` at call sites: `Feature::*` on `@Feature` keymap actions, `PaletteCommand` -> `palette::command_requires` (the palette filters on both the catalogue's `.requires(..)` and `command_requires`, so an AI review command needs Ai *and* Review), `build_features::MERMAID`/`REVIEW`/`BOARD` at the few menu, header and key sites. Help rows with a `{ctx.action}` key of an unbuilt action are dropped by `help_text::built_parts`; a row whose description names the feature in prose is not (translations).
- The view modules swap for stubs (`board_view_stub.rs`, `review_view_stub.rs`, `mermaid_popup_stub.rs`, `radar_stub.rs`): `BoardView` / `ReviewView` and their targets are empty enums, so the compiler proves nothing can open them. Code that only they use carries `cfg_attr(not(feature = ..), allow(dead_code))`.
- `hosting review` (CLI/MCP) is **not** gated by `review`: Gerrit's whole surface and the agents' review loop are that command. `hosting board` and `log --format mermaid` are gated (the clap fields and arms are `cfg`'d out, so the subcommand does not exist rather than failing at run time).
- Tests: the "off" assertions live in `build_features::tests` (palette, keymap, help, popup) and run in the matrix's `tui lib tests, all-langs only` run; the PTY files of a carved-out feature (`pty_radar`, `pty_log_mermaid`) are `#![cfg(feature = ..)]`.

## `history-rewrite`, `global-search`, `workspaces` as custom-build choices

Same recipe as the carved-out features above (tags, stubs, `cfg`'d clap items). Decisions and traps:

- **History workbench** (`history-rewrite`, the label in every build menu): the engine (`GitBackend` rewrite operations, the daemon runs, IPC) stays compiled in every build, it is the shared core; what goes is every way in. `rewrite_view` is not swapped for a stub (its types sit in `InputMode`, `HitTarget`, `PaletteCommand` and the AI request enums, which never lose a variant); the entry points are guarded (`open_rewrite*` return at once, `deeper_available`, the Log menu rows, an agent's `Proposal::Rewrite` is refused as not built) and everything listed is filtered: `Ctx::Rewrite` actions are all `built() == false` (also the `@Ai` one), `command_requires(Rewrite(_))`, help section (`HelpEntry::Gated`; a section whose rows are all hidden loses its heading), the Sync page's script limits (`SYNC_REWRITE`), the two AI blocks of the AI page (`ai_action_built`). CLI/MCP: `rewrite`, `ai rewrite-script`, `ai rewrite-rules` and `ui propose rewrite` are `cfg`'d out of the clap tree; `ai prompt` and `workspace set-ai-instructions` refuse the two actions (`build_features::ai_action_offered`). `.mailmap` (`mailmap`, `log.use_mailmap`) is a Log feature and is **not** part of it.
- **Global search** (`global-search`): `workspace_search` swaps for `workspace_search_stub.rs` (an uninhabited `WorkspaceSearch`, so `InputMode::WorkspaceSearch` can never be built); the title bar draws no button (nothing reserves room for it, so the bar's layout is unchanged), `GlobalWorkspaceSearch` and `PaletteCommand::WorkspaceSearch` are tagged, `search_cmd` and the `search` subcommand are `cfg`'d out. `ui show search` is the Log find bar and stays.
- **Workspaces** (`workspaces`): off = the single default workspace, i.e. "No workspace". The readers in `workspace_ui::store` (`load`, `active`, `ai_for`, `switch`) answer "none", so the chip, the profile bar, the AI layers, the Radar and search scopes and the Activity Log's scope box need no gate of their own; `workspaces_tab_shown()` is false, `go_to_management(Workspaces)` returns, the `+` menu has no Workspaces row, `Alt+W`, `Alt+J`, the Home page keys and every `Workspace:` palette command are tagged, the file watcher does not poll. `workspaces.toml`, the session's `active_workspace` and the parked per-workspace tab lists are neither read nor changed: the window keeps whichever tab list was live when the feature was switched off. CLI/MCP: `workspace`, `commit --workspace`, `search --workspace` (and `GITPOULPI_WORKSPACE`) do not exist; `workspace_cmd` is not compiled.
- Tests: `build_features::tests::*_surface_follows_the_feature` (run in both directions, so the default build checks the "on" half), `preferences::tests::pages`, `crates/gitpoulpi-commands/tests/feature_surfaces.rs`; the PTY files of the two (`pty_workspaces`, `pty_workspace_search`) and the CLI files that drive them are `#![cfg(feature = ..)]`. `xtask features` has a `full minus <name>` run for each, and the quick run's minimal build has all three off.

## Worktree tab grouping, ghost branch label, hover-only tab cross (2026-10-20)

- `general.group_worktree_tabs` (default on): `GlobalSession::group_worktrees` puts each linked worktree's tab right after its repository's tab (the repository tab heads the group; a worktree whose repository is not open stays put). It runs on `session_store::open_tab`, after every `move_tab` (so a worktree dragged away snaps back, and a repository tab brings its worktrees along), and once per window open / when the setting is turned on (`live_settings::take_regroup_request`). Trap: the setting is installed in `restore_launch_tabs`, before any window, because the launch opens a tab. Worktree detection reads `.git` from the file system (`linked_worktree_main`), no daemon round trip. `gitpoulpi repo open` writes the session file itself and is regrouped at the next window open.
- `ui.graph_ghost_ref` (default on; reworked 2026-10-21): the hovered commit row with no pill of its own paints a faded pill (`log_pills::ghost_pill_spans`, same shape and truncation as a real pill) naming the ref it belongs to. The first version drew a clickable `⎇ +` that created a branch; the maintainer found it read as a display glitch and wanted a name, not a button, so the click action and `HitTarget::LogGhostRef` are gone (a click is a plain row click; create-branch stays a menu / palette entry). Hover only, not selection: the pointer is what the pill answers to, so nothing lingers after the pointer leaves or the log scrolls. The name is deduced once per layout build (`graph::ghost_ref::deduce`, stored in `LogLayout`), no git call on hover: local branches beat remote branches (tags are never ghosts since 2026-10-31: they are drawn where they are), and within a kind the tip that is newer in the log walks its first-parent chain first and claims every commit it reaches; a claimed commit ends later walks (so a fork point shared with a merged side branch stays on the trunk, and the pass is O(commits)). Hidden/solo refs are excluded (it reads `refs_for_pills`). The tooltip (`GhostRef::tooltip`, `tooltip.ghost_*`) rides the existing Ref-column commit tooltip, so it shows over the whole Ref cell of the hovered row, not only over the pill. Deleted branches (2026-10-31): after the live-ref pass, `ghost_ref::infer_deleted` walks the merge commits newest first; if the subject parses (`parse_merge_branch`: `Merge branch 'x'` [into y / 'y'], `Merge remote-tracking branch 'origin/x'`, `Merge pull request #N from o/x`, `Merged in x (pull request #N)`), it walks the first-parent chains of both parents newest-first until they meet (the merge base) and gives the still-unclaimed second-parent-side commits the ghost `x`, marked `deleted_in = merge` (struck-through pill, tooltip `tooltip.ghost_deleted`). A commit a live branch or an earlier merge owns ends the walk; chains that never meet (fork off a merged-in branch, history cut by the load limit) claim nothing. Squash merges, rebases, fast-forwards and custom merge subjects leave no trace; the reflog is not used (not in the log data).
- Tab close cross: painted only on the active and the hovered tab; the cell stays blank on the others (stable widths) and their close rect is empty, so hit rects follow the paint. The Radar tab has no cross in this build.

## Third-party notices, trademark wording, renamed themes (2026-10-20)

- `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt` (repository root) is **generated** offline: `cargo run -p xtask -- licenses` reads `cargo metadata --offline` and the license files in the cargo registry; hand-written parts live in `packaging/notices/*.txt` (header, theme credits, bundled code, MPL note). libgit2's `COPYING` is read from the `libgit2-sys` source, so it always matches the linked version. Its first lines hold a SHA-256 of `Cargo.lock` (CRLF-normalized); `xtask/tests/licenses.rs` fails when the lock changes without a regeneration. A crate without a license file in its package gets the standard text of its license (found on another crate), never a gap.
- The file ships in `xtask dist` archives (the task fails without it), `packaging/install.sh` and the Inno script, and is `include_str!`-ed into every binary (`gitpoulpi_core::licenses::THIRD_PARTY`): About > Licenses (`t`, palette `Help: Licenses and Credits`), `gitpoulpi licenses` (CLI, MCP read-only tool).
- `deny.toml` holds the license allow-list; `scripts/check.ps1` runs `cargo deny check licenses` only when cargo-deny is installed. Never patch `option-ext` (MPL-2.0) or libgit2 in-tree.
- Traps found while verifying credits: the "Material" lane colors are 16 of the Material Design 400 shades (Google, Apache-2.0), values unchanged; verify a color source's license before adding a palette.

## Split commit (2026-10-21)

- Plumbing like reword (`commit-tree` per part, descendants re-created, one `update-ref` that checks the old HEAD), not a rebase: no conflict, no auto-stash, index and worktree untouched. Trap: the last part must be the original tree verbatim, and line selections are cumulative on the **parent** preimage, never applied on part k-1. Plan and rejected alternatives: the split-commit design.
- Hunk ids come from `split_source` only (`--no-renames`); the editor, CLI and AI all number from it, so a plan made against one read can drift if the commit is never re-read: the window asks the daemon each time the editor opens.
- Signing is a request flag, default off (reword's rule); the editor's checkbox starts from `SplitSource.sign_default`, read by the backend as the effective `commit.gpgSign`.
- The split is **one** journal entry (labelled like Reword); undo is safe with a dirty worktree because no tree changes.
- `Proposal::SplitCommit` opens the editor asynchronously: `apply_propose` stores `pending_split_proposal` and asks for the source; the answer on `split_source_rx` opens `SplitForm::with_parts` and sets the proposal's "proposed" text (the plan as JSON, so moving a change counts as an edit). `Dialog::Split` is open while the source is pending or the form is up. A failed read settles it as Failed. The split editor's submit path is the only confirm (`proposal::split_confirmed`); a daemon refusal after it keeps the editor open but the proposal is already settled.

## History rewrite: reading order

User page: [history-rewrite.md](../user/history-rewrite.md); plan: [history-rewrite](../developer/the history-rewrite design). The sections below are per slice and each is self-contained; read them in this order. Engine and daemon: S4 (scopes, refs, impact), S5 (daemon jobs, journal). Rules: S9 (content), S10-S12 (scripts, external scripts and trust, AI), S16-S17 (`.mailmap`, trailers), S19 (signatures), S20-S21 (paths, LFS), S24 (message encoding). Around a run: S14 (secrets), S18 (size analysis), S13 (push), S15 (purge), S25 (agent proposals). Rules that hold for all of them: commit headers are edited as **bytes** (unknown headers, continuation lines and their order survive; `encoding` stays unless S24 drops it); a message that is not UTF-8 is never touched by a message rule; a rewrite never pushes; one journal entry per apply; secret values never reach a saved recipe, a log or a proposal.

## History rewrite engine: scopes, refs, impact (S4)

- The preview reads the repository from files (git dir, `commondir`, `shallow`, `HEAD`, other worktrees' `HEAD`) and spends at most 8 `git` processes for preview + apply, multi-ref included (`spawn_budget_rewrite.rs`): one `for-each-ref`, one `rev-list` for what is pushed (skipped without remote-tracking refs), the walk, one `cat-file --batch` that also reads tags, notes trees and stash commits, one `for-each-ref --contains` for the branches that keep the old history, then apply's three. A reftable repository costs one more (HEAD is a stub).
- A pass is the rules plus the re-creation over a set of walk members. Protected exclusion runs the pass a second time without the excluded branches (the commits are already read), so the plan never mixes the two.
- A pushed protected base is excluded only when a rewritten commit of its history is on a remote; excluded refs are listed in `ProtectedExcluded` and, when they share rewritten commits, in `OtherBranchesKeepOldHistory`. When nothing is left the preview is refused with `ProtectedRef`. Protected names come from `<git common dir>/gitpoulpi/workflow.toml`, the committed `.gitpoulpi/workflow.toml` and `ScopeOptions.protected_branches` (for a caller whose `per_repo_state_dir` is elsewhere: the daemon resolves it and passes the names).
- `selection` and `range` walk everything reachable from HEAD (minus `base`) and run the rules on the active commits only; the others are re-parented. Tags follow rewritten commits (`include_tags`), annotated ones are re-created (same headers, a trailing signature dropped), a tag of a tag is followed. Notes are rebuilt with their fan-out and the notes ref moves to a new commit on top of the old one.
- Apply sends `update HEAD` only for the ref HEAD names; every other branch (linked worktrees included) goes by name. `PushState` of a tag is read from `refs/gitpoulpi/remote-tags/<remote>/<tag>`.

## History rewrite in the daemon (S5)

- **IPC**: `RewritePreview {recipe}` (a job, answers the plan *without rows*), `RewriteRows {run, filter, offset, limit<=200}`, `RewriteApply {run, digest, confirmed}` (a job, one journal entry), `RewriteDiscard {run}`, `RewriteTools {fresh}`; types in `gitpoulpi_ipc::rewrite`, progress as `Event::RewriteProgress` plus the job's `phase`/`progress` (Walking 0-50 %, Rewriting 50-90 %, Indexing 90-99 %).
- **One live plan per repository**, in memory (`rewrite_runs.rs`), full with its rows; a new preview replaces it and discards the old run's directory, a failed or canceled one leaves the previous alone. A run the daemon does not hold (restart, another process) is reloaded for Apply from `plan.json` (`GitBackend::rewrite_load`: the file stores the recipe, counts and warnings besides the refs, and the digest is recomputed from recipe and refs, so a hand-edited file is `Stale`); the reload has no rows, so `rewrite rows` of such a run is `Stale` and the pushed confirmation still works from the stored warnings. Refs are CAS-checked at Apply as before. `include_taggers` (off by default) makes identity and anonymise rules also rewrite the tagger of annotated tags that are re-created (only those whose target moved; taggers are numbered after all commit identities). Old run directories (and the maps an apply keeps) are swept after 24 h at the next preview of that repository (not at daemon start: the daemon does not know the repositories yet).
- **Jobs**: preview is `Policy::Free` and runs under the *shared* repository lock (a read), cancelled cooperatively: the progress sink the engine gets is a thread-local `Control` that breaks when the job's cancel flag is set (`GITPOULPI_TEST_REWRITE_TICK_DELAY_MS` slows each step in tests). Apply is `UntilPoint`: cancelable while queued behind the lock, refused from the moment it starts (`enter_point`), with the reason `RewritingFiles` (no new reason, to avoid a catalog round). `jobs::is_job` is the one place that says which requests become jobs (network + these two).
- **Apply asks `confirmed: true` when the plan has `PushedRefs`** (`NeedsConfirmation`, nothing changes). The digest and the run are checked against the live plan before the backend's own compare-and-swap.
- **Journal**: write-ahead capture lists every ref of the plan (branches, tags, notes) with its old value and pins each distinct old tip (existing refs, never a missing object); the outcome gives the final list, a detached `HEAD` entry is the head change. `Effect::Files` only when the checked-out tip's row changes its tree. The label is fixed (`Rewrite history: 412 commits, 2 branches, 4 tags`, notes not counted) and the TUI peels it into words (`undo_label.rewrite_*`). Undo of a rewrite asks a confirmation when a rewritten branch is already on its upstream (`risk.rs` looks at every ref of this kind, not the first).
- **The filter-repo lesson needed no special code**: every mutation goes through `dispatch`, which ends with `outside_adopt`, so the refs snapshot is refreshed under the same lock and the next outside sync records nothing; `rewrite_undo.rs` falsifies it (skip the adopt for `RewriteApply` and four entries appear).
- Protected branch names are read from the effective workflow (shared file plus the user override in the per-repository state dir) and passed in `ScopeOptions.protected_branches`; the backend adds the two files it can see itself.

## History rewrite content engine (S9)

- **Rules**: `ContentReplace` (literal or regex on text, or secret literals), `RemovePaths` (globs, `GlobSet`), `RemoveBlobsOver` (bytes). They compose per file in recipe order (`trees.rs`, `blobs.rs`); `ContentFind::Secrets { scan: true }` works since S14 (see "History rewrite secrets (S14)"). Paths, LFS and scripts are the sections below (S20, S21, S10).
- **Caches**: a blob is read and transformed once per `(blob id, regular file, path only when a rule has path globs)`; a tree once per `(tree id, path)`. Trees and blobs go to the temporary pack as they are made (`pack::PackWriter`, count patched and trailer hashed at the end, an offset index `objects.idx` beside it), so memory does not grow with the history. Anchored globs give a literal directory prefix, and a subtree no rule can reach is never read; a glob without a slash (`secrets/`, `*.log`) matches at any depth, so it walks every unique tree (still no blob read).
- **Text rules**: a blob with a NUL in its first 8 KiB is binary and skipped (counted, `Counts::blobs_skipped_binary`); so is text that is not UTF-8 for a pattern rule; a secret literal is replaced byte for byte, so a Latin-1 file is cleaned. Blobs over the rule's `max_blob_bytes` (default 50 MiB) are skipped and counted (`blobs_skipped_large`), and are read past, not held. `.gitattributes` is deliberately not read per commit.
- **Rows**: `CommitChange.paths` lists what the rules did to files (a directory that matches a glob is one `Removed` entry for the directory), capped at 64 per commit; `fields` gets a `Tree` change. `per_rule` counts the commits each content rule acted on (a bit per rule threaded through the tree results).
- **Pruning** (`prune_empty: auto`, the default; `never` keeps them): a non-merge commit whose tree a content rule changed, that equals its new parent's tree and was not equal to its old parent's, is dropped; a merge whose parents collapsed to one commit with its tree goes the same way. Roots are never dropped. A dropped commit maps to its new parent in `new_id` (so refs and tags that pointed at it move to the parent), is listed with `new: None`, has zeros in the `commit-map`, and its notes are not carried over.
- **Checked-out files**: `Counts::head_tree_changed` says the commit HEAD names gets another tree (also in `plan.json`, so a reloaded plan knows; the journal reads it for `Effect::Files`, which makes undo/redo move the files). The preview refuses `DirtyPathsTouched` when `status --porcelain -z -uno` lists a file the rules would change or remove in HEAD's old tree (decided with the same blob cache, so no second implementation), and `CheckedOutElsewhere` when a rewritten branch whose tree changes is checked out in another worktree. Apply: the `update-ref` transaction, then `read-tree -m -u <old> <new>` (keeps local edits to files the rewrite leaves alone); if it refuses, a second transaction puts every ref back and the error is `DirtyPathsTouched` again.
- **Spawns**: a content run costs the metadata run's budget plus one `status` and one `read-tree`: 8 for a repository without a remote, at most 10 with one (`spawn_budget_rewrite.rs`). An empty pack (every change was a drop) skips `index-pack`.
- **Secrets**: `plan.json` stores `recipe.saved()` (no secret literal), and the plan digest is computed over the saved recipe too, so a reloaded plan hashes the same. The compiled secret regex is never echoed in an error; the blob diff of a recipe that has a secret rule hides every removed line (`redacted: true`, the text is `REDACTED_LINE`) because the preview cannot know which lines held the literal after a reload. The undo pins keep the old history (secrets included) alive until Purge (S15): `log --all` still shows it.
- **Blob diff**: `rewrite_blob_diff(run, commit, path)` reads the old file from the repository and the new one from the preview pack (the new commit's tree, then new trees and the blob by offset); `commit` is the id before the rewrite. IPC `RewriteBlobDiff`, CLI `rewrite diff`, MCP `rewrite_diff` (a read). It works from the run directory alone, so it also works after a daemon restart.
- **TUI forms (S9)**: the content kinds' fields come from `rules_form::fields`, which also sets `Field.error` from the core's own checks (`CompiledPattern`, `validate_glob`, size above zero) so the reason sits under the field; `prune_empty` is a run option shown at the end of the three content kinds' panes (`DetailForm::run_prune`, written back to `recipe.options`). `scan` is never offered in the rule form (the Secrets tab writes scanning rules, see "History rewrite secrets (S14)"). Sizes parse `500k`/`5M` and `5 MiB`. The Commits row drill-in (`rewrite_view/drill.rs`) is a master-detail overlay (files, diff) on top of the workbench; its answer is dropped when the cursor moved on (`pending` carries the file index). `DirtyPathsTouched` now carries every dirty path, one per line (capped at 50), and the three listing refusals (`DirtyPathsTouched`, `CheckedOutElsewhere`, `PartialClone`) fill `RewriteView::refusal`, drawn in the Preview pane until a tab change, edit or new preview.

## History rewrite scripts (S10)

- **Language and feature**: the embedded language is **Lua 5.4** (maintainer decision 2026-10-21; the plan's Rhai text reads Lua), through `mlua` with the `vendored` feature, so the C sources are compiled by `cc` (MSVC `cl` on Windows, which is there for the Rust toolchain anyway; no Lua install needed). Cargo feature `rewrite-scripts` (default on) on `cli-backend`, `git2-backend`, `daemon`, `testkit` and the binary crates; custom builds tie it to `history-rewrite`. Off: a `Script` rule, `rewrite_script_check` and `rewrite_script_test` answer `Error::Unsupported`; `xtask features --quick` has a "history-rewrite, scripts off" clippy run. External and shell languages arrived with S11 (next section).
- **Where**: pure parts in core (`rewrite/script.rs`: the `--!` header parser, `resolve_params`, `ScriptCheck`, `ScriptTestRow`, the `ScriptFailed` detail text); `cli-backend/src/history/script/{contract,lua_rt,stage}.rs`. The rule is a metadata-stage rule (not a content rule): `engine.rs` loads it per pass and `stage::run_stage` calls it commit by commit in `rev-list --topo-order --reverse` order, over the rules before it (it sees their result).
- **Contract**: one JSON request (`contract::Request::json`, the shape the plan lists for external commands, `version: 1`, `kind: "commit"`); Lua gets `c = request.commit` and `ctx = {index, total, window, params, state}`. `window.before` is the first-parent ancestors **nearest first, as already rewritten**, `window.after` the first-parent descendants nearest first **as originally** (summaries: id, author, committer, subject); the header's `window: N` (0..50) sets how many. `params` are typed by the header (`text`, `number`, `bool`; defaults apply; an undeclared name is refused). The answer: `author` / `committer` partial objects, `message`, `state` (replaces the carried state), `log`; any other field is `ScriptFailed` naming it (`tree` and `parents` belong to the engine). `ctx.state` starts `{}` per pass and is JSON between calls. Not in S10: `needs` (changes, path, binary), `rewrite_blob`, `rewrite_tag` and `kinds` other than `commit` are reported as check problems.
- **Failure**: `RewriteRefused(ScriptFailed, "<commit hex>: <reason>")` (`script_failure` / `parse_script_failure` in core; no commit prefix for a script that does not compile). The CLI also prints `refused.script = {commit, reason}`. A script is loaded (compiled, top level run, header and params checked) before the history is walked, so a bad script fails fast and changes nothing.
- **Sandbox** (`lua_rt.rs` header lists it; `lua_rt` tests and the testkit's `has_no_file_clock_or_module_access` pin it): only `string`, `table`, `math`, `utf8` are opened; `dofile`, `loadfile`, `load`, `print`, `collectgarbage`, `pcall`, `xpcall`, `string.dump`, `math.random`, `math.randomseed` are removed; chunks load in text mode only. **Trap: no `pcall`** on purpose: a script could otherwise catch the limit error and loop on. Falsified: with `dofile` left in, a script read `/etc/passwd`.
- **Determinism trap**: Lua orders table keys by a hash seeded from the clock and addresses, so `pairs` is replaced by a sorted-key version, `next(t)` only answers "first key" (to test for empty), `tostring` of a table or function is the type name, and responses are read back with sorted keys. `rand(seed)` / `stable_hash(text, salt)` are the pure replacements. Still address-dependent: `string.format('%p')` and `%s` of a table (do not use them). Script globals persist across the calls of a pass (one Lua state per pass); use `ctx.state` for anything that must survive a different processing order (`rewrite_script_test` runs a selection, a preview runs everything).
- **Limits** per call (`Limits::default()`): 10 million VM instructions (a hook every 1000), 64 MiB growth on the Lua allocator, 5 s of wall time (the safety net; the instruction count is the reproducible limit). Recursion ends in Lua's own "stack overflow". Trap: the hook cannot interrupt one long C call, so a pathological `string.find` pattern is only stopped when it returns.
- **Helpers**: `regex_replace(text, pattern, with)`, `regex_match(text, pattern)` (Rust `regex`, 1 MiB size limit, `$1` in the replacement), `stable_hash`, `rand`, `weekday(time, offset)` (ISO 1 = Monday), `hour(time, offset)`, `log(text)` (64 lines of 1 KiB per call, returned with `rewrite_script_test`, not shown by a preview yet). `trailers(message)` (a list of `{key, value}`), `set_trailer(message, key, value[, "replace"|"add"|"skip"])`, `remove_trailer(message, key)` and `at("YYYY-MM-DD[ HH:MM[:SS]]", offset_minutes)` (Unix time) are S17's, see "History rewrite trailers and message references (S17)".
