- **`rewrite_script_test(repo, script, commits)`** reads the history reachable from the commits and HEAD (so it costs like a preview's walk), runs the script as a selection run would (history order, state carried, window from the real history, earlier tested commits "rewritten") and returns a `ScriptTestRow {commit, fields, log}` per commit, in history order, writing nothing. It returns rows, not `CommitChange`s, because no new commit exists in a test. `rewrite_script_check` returns a `ScriptCheck {header, functions, problems}`: a syntax error is a problem in the answer (so an editor can show it live), only an unsupported language or an unreadable file is an error. A `ScriptSource::File` path is relative to the repository and may not leave it (`InvalidRule`); the CLI's `--script FILE` reads the file itself and embeds it as an inline script, so a saved recipe is self-contained.
- **TUI (S10)**: the Script rule's editor is an overlay (`rewrite_view/script.rs`, like the blob diff drill-in) opened by Enter on the rule's *Script* line, because the detail pane is a few rows high; the pane keeps the language, source and parameter rows and one check line. Typed text goes into the rule at every key (`set_script_text`), so there is no draft to lose. **Check**: `CheckState` is a debounce (300 ms after the last edit) with at most one `RewriteScriptCheck` in flight; every answer carries `key_of(script)` (language + text or path, not the parameters) and is shown only while that key is still the one on screen, so a stale answer is never drawn (falsified). The wake-up for the debounce is `RewriteView::next_deadline` in `idle.rs`; a reply pokes the loop itself. **Parameter rows** are `FieldId::ScriptParam(i)` built from `parse_header` of the inline text (core, pure, no daemon round trip), or from the last check's header for a file source (`DetailForm::file_header`); `Field::label_text` carries the script's own label. An empty value removes the key, so the header's default applies again. `model::script_issue` refuses Preview for a text parameter with neither value nor default (`ScriptHeader::missing_params`). **Test on selection** is a plain `RewriteScriptTest` request, not a daemon job, so Cancel only forgets the answer (the run ends by itself within the script limits); commits are the Log's multi-selection, else the 20 newest nodes (`ScopeContext::test_commits`, built in `keys_prefix.rs`). **Files** are read and written synchronously (a script is a few kilobytes) and only for paths made of normal components, so nothing leaves the repository. A build without `rewrite-scripts` greys the kind (`RuleTemplate::disabled_reason`, `PopupRow::disabled`) and the palette row opens the workbench with the reason. **Trap**: `script:LINE` is not at the start of a problem (`syntax error: script:3: ...`), find it anywhere (`problem_lines`). **Trap**: the text editor's `Caret` does not handle Enter; the caller inserts the newline.




## History rewrite external scripts and trust (S11)

- **Languages**: `ScriptLanguage` is `Lua` (embedded, sandboxed, always on), `Shell`, `Python`, `LuaExternal` (serde `lua_external`; the S10 placeholder `External` is gone, nothing could have saved it). Everything but `Lua` is `is_external()`: a process of its own, **not sandboxed**. Settings (live): `rewrite.external_scripts` = list of `"shell"` (default), `"python"`, `"lua"` (the *installed* Lua; the embedded one needs no entry), `rewrite.script_timeout_ms` (5000) and `rewrite.script_total_timeout_s` (3600), `0` reads as the default. Docs: `config-reference.md`.
- **Policy never comes from a client**: `Recipe.script_policy` (a `ScriptPolicy`: the three switches, the timeouts and `trusted` hashes) is overwritten by the daemon on every `RewritePreview` (`rewrite_runs::preview`), and `rewrite_script_check/test` take a `&ScriptPolicy` argument the daemon builds the same way (`rewrite_runs::script_policy`: settings + the repository's `trusted_scripts` + the request's `vouched`). A bare backend (a test) sees `ScriptPolicy::default()`: shell allowed, nothing trusted. `Recipe::saved()` drops it. Core also carries `script_hash` (SHA-256 hex of the text, `sha2` in core), `ScriptTrust`, `ScriptSwitches` (in `RewriteTools.external_scripts`, filled by the daemon, `None` = not known: nothing greyed on a guess).
- **Trust** (per-repo state, `RepoState::trusted_scripts: [{hash, label, trusted_at}]`, capped at 200): a script runs when its content hash is there, whatever the source (inline or `File`; the hash is of the text the backend read, so a file changed after the question is a different hash). `RewriteTrustScript { hash, label }` (an `OperationKind`, classified like the other script reads) writes it. `vouched: [hash]` on `PreviewArgs`/`ScriptCheckArgs`/`ScriptTestArgs` trusts hashes **for that request only** (never stored): the TUI sends the hashes of scripts typed in its editor (`ScriptState::authored`, last 64), never of a script that came with a recipe or a file (`load_script_file` and `set_script_text` do not mark; the editor's key handlers do). That is "inline scripts typed by the user are trusted" without trusting a shared recipe's inline script. Refusals: `ScriptsDisabled` (language off; checked first), `ToolMissing` (interpreter), `ScriptNotTrusted` (detail = the hash). `rewrite_script_check` answers an untrusted script with `trust: Untrusted` + `hash` (an answer, not an error) and starts nothing.
- **Agents cannot trust**: `Session::may_trust()` is false by default and only the `gitpoulpi` binary sets it (`commands::run`); `rewrite script trust` and every `--trust` need it. MCP: `["rewrite","script","trust"]` is in `registry::EXCLUDED`, and `schema.rs` / `argv.rs` drop the `trust` argument (test `an_agent_cannot_trust_a_script`). **Trap**: the CLI does not vouch for `--script FILE` on its own; a person runs `script trust FILE` or passes `--trust`, so a repository's script never runs just because a command named it. The daemon socket is not a boundary (anything that can write to it can also run `git`); this guards agents, not local malware. **Trap**: `per_repo_state_dir = in_worktree` keeps the trust file inside the working tree (`.gitpoulpi/`, gitignored): a repository must never ship one, and the default (`<git dir>/gitpoulpi`) cannot be cloned.
- **Process** (`history/script/external.rs`): `ExternalProc::start` writes the script's copy (`script.py`, `script.sh`, `script.ps1`, `script.lua`; the copy is what was hashed) into a fresh `gitpoulpi-script-<pid>-<n>-<nanos>` temp dir (0700) which is also the cwd, runs `<interpreter> <lead args> <copy>` with `env_clear()` plus `ENV_ALLOW`, stdin/stdout/stderr piped, and starts the tree guard. A writer thread feeds requests (a script that does not read stdin cannot block the engine), a reader thread parses stdout lines (16 MiB cap per line), a third drains stderr (64 KiB kept, 1 KiB per line, the rest still drained: a script blocked on a full stderr pipe would hang). `call` waits in 50 ms slices for the answer, the per-request deadline, the total deadline and the job's cancel (progress ticks at most every 100 ms). Any failure, cancel or drop kills the tree **at once** (`TreeGuard::kill_now`), reaps the child and removes the dir; a clean end closes stdin, gives the script a second to exit, then kills whatever is left (a background child must not outlive the pass).
- **Interpreters**: `Tool::Lua` joins `Python`, `Bash`, `Pwsh`, `Sh` in the tool probe (`lua5.4`, `lua54`, `lua`, `luajit`; `lua` has no `--version`, so its probe runs `-v`). `ToolProbe::program(tool, root)` resolves the program (`PATH`, on Windows Git for Windows' `usr/bin`). Python runs as `python -I -u FILE` (`py -3 -I -u` for the Windows launcher): isolated mode ignores `PYTHON*` variables and the user site; external Lua as `lua FILE`; a shell script as `bash --noprofile --norc FILE` / `sh FILE` (POSIX) or `pwsh -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File FILE`. The dialect is `.ps1` / `.sh` / `.bash` by the file name, else a shebang naming `pwsh` or `powershell`, else the platform's (PowerShell on Windows, POSIX elsewhere). **Test seam**: `GITPOULPI_TOOL_<NAME>_PATH` points a tool at one exact program (the testkit's fixture stands in for Python and Lua, so CI needs neither); `GITPOULPI_TOOL_<NAME>=missing` still forces the status. Both cross the daemon and PTY boundaries.
- **Header**: `parse_header_for(language, text)`: Lua uses `--!` lines, shell and Python use `#!` lines; `#!/...` and `#! /...` shebangs and other `#` comments are skipped like plain comments. An external script has no function list, so the check only reports header problems, the kinds/`needs` it cannot run yet, and a failed handshake.
- **Handshake** (`rewrite_script_check`, trusted scripts only): one process, one request for a made-up commit (`Dry Run <dry-run@example.invalid>`, `"dry_run": true` added to the request), the answer read like any other (`{"error": ...}` included), then the process is ended. An untrusted script is never started.
- **Windows process tree (trap, not testable here)**: the std child is created with `CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP | CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and joins a Job Object (`KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`) right after the spawn (`TreeGuard::for_pid`); `kill_now` terminates the job. A grandchild the interpreter starts in the few microseconds before the assignment escapes the job, as with the network commands (`tree_kill.rs`). A grandchild that keeps stdout open would make the reader thread wait, which is why no thread is ever joined: the threads end when the pipes close. Unix: `process_group(0)` and `kill(-pgid, SIGKILL)`; a script that calls `setsid` leaves the group (documented, not defended).
- **Windows PowerShell as a script host**: stdout is read as UTF-8 bytes lossily; a script that writes the BOM or a UTF-16 console encoding is a "not JSON" failure that says so.
- **TUI** (`script.rs`, plus the language table in `rules_form.rs`, a refusal arm in `mod.rs`, the greyed rows in `popup.rs`): the language row offers the four languages; a row is dim with its reason (`off in the settings` from `RewriteTools.external_scripts`, `not installed` from the tool check, never on a guess) and picking it puts the whole reason in the notice (`edit_field` refuses, so the Left/Right stepping is guarded too). The rule pane's first note and the editor's title say "not sandboxed". A new script follows the language only while it is the untouched starter or empty (`starter_for`). `Question::TrustScript { hash, then, cursor: Option }`: two numbered answers, **no highlight and no Enter default** until an arrow or `1` / `2`; yes sends `RewriteTrustScript` and repeats what was refused (`AfterTrust::Preview` / `Test`) when the daemon answered; the check line says `Not trusted yet` for an untrusted script. Tests: `rewrite_view/tests/script_external.rs`, PTY `pty_rewrite_external.rs`.
## Workflow hooks trust (security hardening, item 3)

- **What is trusted**: the `[hooks]` of the committed `.gitpoulpi/workflow.toml`. `gitpoulpi_core::workflow::hooks_hash` is SHA-256 over `phase \0 command \n` in fixed phase order (`after_start`, then `before_finish`); the hash is the key, so any change of a command, its phase or the list asks again. Trust lives in `RepoState::trusted_hooks: [{hash, hooks: [{phase, command}], trusted_at}]` (capped at 50, oldest forgotten); the commands are kept so a resume can check them and the TUI can show what changed. Same trap as S11: `per_repo_state_dir = in_worktree` puts that file in the working tree; a repository must never ship one.
- **Personal hooks never ask**: `FlowFacts::hooks_personal` is true when the user override (`RepoState::workflow.hooks`) has a `hooks` value (it replaces the shared list as a whole, `Workflow::merged_with`); the planner copies it to `FlowStep::Hook::personal` and `HookInfo::personal`. A saved flow state without the field reads as `personal: false` (the safe side).
- **The check is one read**: `facts::gather` loads the workflow once and the daemon computes `FlowPlan::hooks_trust` (`Personal`, `Trusted`, `Untrusted { hash, previous }`; `previous` is the newest trusted list) from that read, so the hash that is checked and the commands that run cannot differ. `flow::exec::start_run` calls `trust::check_plan` right after the plan and before the `Exec` exists: nothing has run when it refuses (`TypedError::HooksNotTrusted { hash, hooks }`, test `untrusted_committed_hooks_refuse_a_run_before_any_step`). **Trap**: `resume_continue` must check too (`trust::check_resume`, before `exec_for`), because `<git-dir>/gitpoulpi/flow-state.json` is a file whose commands nobody reviewed: each hook still to run must be in a trusted record, or be `personal` and still one of the user's own hooks. A step in the file claiming `personal: true` is not believed (`a_resume_checks_every_saved_command_and_refuses_an_injected_one`; break the call to see it red). `resume: abort` runs no hook and is never refused.
- **`FlowTrustHooks { hash? }`** (classified like `RewriteTrustScript`: `R`, `ALL`) re-reads the shared file and trusts only when `hooks_hash` of what is on disk now equals `hash` (a stale hash from before an edit is refused, no time-of-check gap); without a hash it only reports `{hash, hooks, trusted}`. Agents cannot trust: `Session::may_trust()` guards `--hash` (and the terminal question's trust), `["flow","trust-hooks"]` is in `registry::EXCLUDED`, `schema.rs`/`argv.rs` drop `hash` (`an_agent_cannot_trust_workflow_hooks`). `--run-hooks` on untrusted hooks is answered by the CLI with `{"refused": {"reason": "hooks_not_trusted", hash, hooks, trust_command}}` and exit code 2 before any `FlowRun`; the daemon's own refusal is the backstop for any other client.
- **TUI** (`flow/hook_trust.rs`, `flow/dialog.rs`): a preview whose `FlowPreview::trust` is `Untrusted` has no "Run hooks" checkbox; the action button opens the question over the preview (the dialog's `TrustUi`). The pane shows the commands verbatim (control characters are spelled `\x1b`, never sent to the terminal; a long command wraps, never truncates), scrolls by wheel, draggable `BarDrag` scrollbar and PgUp/PgDn/Home/End; answers `1 Trust and run` / `2 Run without hooks`, no highlight and no Enter default until an arrow or digit (like S11's question); Esc, the Cancel button and the close cross do not answer. "Trust and run" sends `FlowTrustHooks { hash }` off the render thread (spinner after 150 ms) and runs with the hooks when the daemon answered; a refusal (the file changed meanwhile) shows the daemon's text and reads the preview again. Tests: `flow/tests.rs`, `flow/hook_trust.rs`, PTY `pty_flow_hook_trust.rs`.

## History rewrite: AI writes the script (S12)

- **Action**: `AiAction::RewriteScript` (key `rewrite_script`, label "History rewrite script"), profile chosen like the other actions; its Settings > AI strings live in the `[rewrite_ai]` i18n section (like `[review_ai]`), as does everything else of this slice, so the section sits at the end of the catalogs.
- **`gitpoulpi-ai`**: `prompts/rewrite_script.rs` (`BASE_PROMPT` = the contract: header, `rewrite_commit(c, ctx)`, return shape, helpers, sandbox limits; `rewrite_script(instruction, sample)` = the user message; `SampleCommit` = author, email, UTC date, subject line, no body, no files; `MAX_SAMPLE` 50); `parse::rewrite_script` (the fenced block that defines `rewrite_commit`, else an unfenced script from its first `--`/`function`/`local` line; a script with no `--!` line gets `DEFAULT_SCRIPT_HEADER`); `assist/rewrite_script.rs` (`RewriteScriptRequest::new(instruction, commits, send_sample, n)` is where the sample is dropped when the box is off, `Job::write_rewrite_script[_with]`, the `ScriptChecker` seam).
- **Retry**: one second call, when the answer has no script or the check (`ScriptChecker`, the daemon's `rewrite_script_check`) reports problems; the retry carries the reason and the rejected script. The second answer is returned as it is, with its remaining `problems`, so the editor shows what is still wrong instead of dropping the model's work. `checked: false` when the check itself could not run.
- **TUI**: `rewrite_view/script_ai.rs`. The dialog never talks to a model: it sets an `AiWish` (`Describe`, `Send {instruction, sample}`, `Cancel`) that `window/tick.rs` collects each frame and runs through the ordinary `AiUi` (`AiRequest::RewriteScript`, jobs indicator, same profile and key handling), and it gets the answer back through `put_ai_script` / `ai_stopped`. This keeps `RewriteKeyOutcome` untouched. The system prompt shown in the dialog comes from `ai_ui::rewrite_prompt_preview` (the real `Job::system_prompt`, no key read), the user message from `ai_ui::rewrite_user_message`, so the dialog and the request cannot differ. The sample rows are `ScopeContext::sample` (filled in `keys_prefix.rs` from the Log nodes of `test_commits`, at most `SAMPLE_MAX` = 20).
- **Modal while it runs**: the dialog stays open (Esc cancels) so the rule under the cursor, which the answer goes into through `set_script_text`, cannot change. The mark "written by AI" is the `script_key` of the script when it arrived: it shows in the editor's title while the text is still that one and disappears at the first edit (no flag to forget). The palette row is `RewriteEntry::ScriptAi` (`is_ai`, so it is hidden with AI off and carries the sparkle); the label does not contain "AI: ", which `every_ai_palette_command_carries_the_sparkle` now allows for rows ending in "with AI...".
- **Traps**: the fake agent of the PTY tests echoes through `cmd` on Windows, so test scripts avoid `& | < > % ^`; the capturing agent that asserts what was sent is Unix only. The Script editor's left pane is about 38 columns wide, so nothing long goes on its label row (the mark is in the title). The button is offered only for an inline embedded Lua script (not a file source, not an external language).
- **CLI/MCP**: `ai rewrite-script <instruction> [--sample N]` (`ai_cmd.rs`, MCP `ai_rewrite_script`, read + network like `ai_split_plan`), JSON `{script, check, retried, sample, applied}`, exit 2 when the check still has problems.
- **Create Rules with AI (sibling, `AiAction::RewriteRules`, config key `rewrite_rules`)**: the model answers a JSON object (`scope`, `scope_options`, `options`, `rules`, `notes`) in the workbench's own `Rule` serde shape. The vocabulary of the system prompt is the table `prompts::rewrite_rules::VOCABULARY` (kind, meaning, JSON example); a test parses every example into `RuleKind`, validates it, and checks the table covers every `RuleKind` but `script`, so the prompt cannot drift from the types. `rules_answer::rewrite_rules` parses and validates **each rule alone** (`validate_recipe` on a one-rule recipe), keeps the valid ones and returns `RuleProblem { position, what, reason }` for the rest. Refused whatever their shape: `script` (code), the secret-literal form of `content_replace`, a `.mailmap` file path, an extraction `mode`, `signatures` (asked at Apply), scopes other than current branch (no `since`) / all local / existing refs (a date limit cannot be expressed: the model says so in `notes`). **Data only, structurally**: `assist/rewrite_rules.rs` has no checker, daemon or backend; the TUI turns the outcome into `RulesProposal` and `RewriteView::put_ai_rules` copies it into `recipe` (add after, or replace when the box is ticked) and calls `touch()`, never `preview`/`apply`. The context (scope, refs, authors of the newest 20 commits) is sent only with its box (off by default) and is on screen in the sent pane. CLI `ai rewrite-rules <description> [--context]` (`ai_cmd.rs`, MCP `ai_rewrite_rules`) prints `{recipe, problems, notes, retried, context, applied: false}`, exit 2 when something was left out. TUI: `rewrite_view/rules_ai.rs` (own dialog state next to the script's; `AiWish::DescribeRules` / `SendRules`), key `Alt+I` (`rewrite.ai_rules`, `@Ai`), palette `✦ Rewrite: Create Rules with AI...`. Tests: `ai/tests/rewrite_rules.rs`, `tui rewrite_view/tests/rules_ai.rs`, PTY `pty_rewrite_rules_ai.rs` (refs asserted unchanged), CLI `cli/tests/ai_rewrite_rules.rs`.
- **Tests**: `ai/tests/rewrite_script.rs` (parser fixtures `ok_plain`, `ok_fenced`, `prose_around`, `no_header`, `compile_error_then_fixed`; retry with a scripted `AiClient` and checker; sample off sends no commit data, asserted on the recorded prompt), `tui rewrite_view/tests/script_ai.rs`, PTY `pty_rewrite_script_ai.rs` (scripted local agent), CLI `cli/tests/ai_rewrite_script.rs`. Falsified: `RewriteScriptRequest::new` ignoring `send_sample` turned the sample-off test red; `ai_sample` ignoring the box turned the TUI tests and the PTY test red; accepting the first answer without the retry turned both retry tests red.

## History rewrite secrets (S14)

- **Where**: pure scanner in core (`rewrite/secrets.rs`: `scan_text`, `preview`, `SecretValue`, `SecretFinding`, `SecretScan`, the shared `TOKEN_SHAPES` table); the walk in `cli-backend/src/history/scan.rs`; `GitBackend::rewrite_scan_secrets(repo, &Scope, sink) -> SecretScan` (git2 delegates); IPC `RewriteScanSecrets` (`ScanSecretsArgs { scope }`, a **job**: `Policy::Free`, one per repository, progress as `Event::RewriteProgress`: `Walking` while commits are read, `Rewriting` while trees and blobs are scanned - there is no `Scanning` phase because adding one breaks the TUI's exhaustive match).
- **One table for the log and the scanner**: `redact`'s word-prefix masking reads `secrets::token_prefixes()` (the scanner's `TOKEN_SHAPES`), no list of its own. A prefix added for the scanner is masked in the application log from then on; the test `the_scanner_and_redact_share_their_token_prefixes` pins it. `redact` is looser on purpose (any word with a known prefix and 8 more characters); the scanner needs the real length.
- **Values**: `SecretValue` has a redacting `Debug`, no `Display`, no `Serialize`; `reveal()` is called in two places only (the finding id hash and the replacement built in `blobs.rs`). `SecretFinding` (what crosses the wire) holds the rule id, the first path, `more_paths`, first/last commit, commit and blob counts, a stable id and `preview`. `preview` shows at most a known prefix and four characters (`ghp_****abcd`), a value under 16 body characters only its prefix or length; never more than eight characters of a value. The id is `f-` plus twelve hex digits of SHA-1 over `"gitpoulpi-secret\0" + value` (same value, same id, whatever the rule or file); it is as sensitive as the history that holds the value, no more. The pre-existing `SecretText` (recipe literal) is unchanged: it must serialize because the form sends it to the daemon, and `Recipe::saved()` drops it.
- **Patterns** (`rule_ids()`): `github-token`, `github-pat`, `gitlab-token`, `slack-token`, `stripe-key`, `anthropic-key`, `openai-key`, `google-api-key`, `npm-token`, `aws-access-key` (the prefix table), `private-key` (BEGIN..END block, at least 80 bytes), `jwt`, `url-credentials` (`scheme://user:PASSWORD@host`), `generic-secret` (`password|secret|token|api_key|access_key|private_key|credentials` in the key, `=`/`:`/`:=`, value of 8+ characters without whitespace or quotes). Overlaps keep the most specific (the earliest rule in the table, then the generic ones). **Guards** that keep false positives down, each tested: a token glued to a letter, digit, `+`, `/`, `_` or `-` before it is not a token (the middle of base64 or of a longer word; `disk-...` is not `sk-...`); a letter after an AWS key means it is not one; fewer than six distinct characters or a placeholder word (`example`, `changeme`, `your_`, `<`, `${`, `os.environ`, `getenv`, ...) is not a key; generic values need Shannon entropy >= `ENTROPY_MIN` (3.0 bits per character, 3.5 when the value is only letters so `passwordField` stays out), are not UUIDs, URLs, `data:` URIs or `sha512-` style integrity strings, and are not read in lines over 4000 bytes (minified data) or in lock files (`*.lock`, `*-lock.json`, `*-lock.yaml`, `go.sum`; the token prefixes still apply there). Text only: a NUL in the first 8 KiB means binary (counted), a blob over 5 MiB is skipped (counted); both counters are in the answer so "not exhaustive" is visible.
- **Walk**: commits in `rev-list --topo-order --reverse` order from the scope's tips (`refs::resolve`, so every scope kind works and `Selection`/`Range` scan their whole walk), one `cat-file --batch`. Per blob (cached by id, plus the lock-file flag) the matches; per tree (cached by tree id) only the **files that hold a finding**, so a commit costs one lookup. First/last commit, commit count (a commit counts once however many files hold the value) and blob count (distinct ids) come from that. Dev-profile timing on the `xtask perf --rewrite` fixture: 2000 commits and 6001 blobs in 0.39 s (the same fixture's replace-text preview takes 0.78 s). `head_of` (new in `walk.rs`) reads HEAD without `probe`'s refusals: a scan is a read and works mid-merge or in a shallow clone.
- **`scan: true` on a secrets rule**: `ContentFind::Secrets { literals, scan: true }`. A literal shaped like a finding id (`SecretText::finding_id`) **selects that finding**; other literals are plain secret values as before; with no id at all the rule removes **every** finding in the recipe's scope. The wire shape of `ContentFind` did not change (a new field would have broken every constructor in the TUI crate). At preview time `scan::resolve` scans the recipe's scope, maps ids to values **in memory** and hands them to `Content::with_secrets`/`Blobs::add_replace` (the replacement is the same byte-for-byte regex alternation as for literals, with non-UTF-8 values escaped byte by byte); the values live for the duration of the preview call. An id the scan does not find is `InvalidRule` ("scan again"). A scan that finds nothing makes the rule a no-op (no empty regex). `Recipe::saved()` keeps finding ids (they name no value) and drops real literals; `holds_secrets()` ignores ids when `scan` is on. **Trap**: the plan digest and plan.json hash the saved recipe, so they hold ids only; the blob diff hides every removed line of a recipe with a secrets rule as before.
- **Scope of the scan and the rewrite**: the scan inside a preview uses the recipe's scope, so a finding that exists only on a branch outside the scope is not removed (the preview's other branches keep it). The standalone scan defaults to `Scope::default()` (current branch); the TUI should offer the same scope picker as the workbench.
- **Not done**: commit messages are not scanned (a token in a message needs a message rule; the plan mentions it); notes and tags' messages neither; blobs reachable only from refs outside `refs/heads`, `refs/tags`, `refs/notes` are not walked. No allow-list file for false positives yet.
- **CLI/MCP**: `gitpoulpi rewrite scan-secrets [--all-local | --ref R... | --since REV]` (read; MCP `rewrite_scan_secrets`), JSON `SecretScan`; `--remove-finding ID|all` (repeatable) on `preview`/`run` adds the scanning rule; a non-id (a value typed by mistake) is refused without being echoed. `--secrets-file` (S9) still fills every secrets rule, including a scanning one (the lines are literals; an id-shaped line is read as an id). Help keys in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` are English placeholders for the next translation round; the translated `docs/user/*/cli-reference.md` do not mention `scan-secrets` yet.
- **Tests**: core unit tests (`secrets/tests.rs`: every shape and prefix, the entropy threshold both sides, placeholders, lock files, UUIDs, base64 with an embedded `AKIA` shape, glued tokens, minified lines, previews, shared prefixes); contract on both backends (`rewrite_secrets.rs`: planted token with first/last commit and counts, decoys, scope, cancel, empty repo, remove by id with the token gone from every blob of every branch and tag, other secret kept, plan.json and run dir free of values, refs put back = old history back and same ids, `all`, ids mixed with literals, nothing to find); daemon (`rewrite_undo.rs`: the scan is a job with progress, no value in answers, events, job list, the log (an in-memory `gitpoulpi_log` at Trace; the second secret is one `redact` does not mask by itself, so the silence is the scanner's), journal entry and undo, cancel); CLI `assert_cmd` on the JSON; perf line in `rewrite_content_perf`. Falsified: the preview/rule field leaking the value turned the contract, daemon (wire) and CLI tests red; a `tracing::info!` of a file holding the unprefixed password turned the daemon log test red (a `ghp_` token in a log line is masked by `redact` anyway, which is why the second secret exists).
- **What the TUI Secrets tab needs**: call `RewriteScanSecrets` (job, cancel with `JobCancel`, progress from `Event::RewriteProgress`) with the workbench's scope; list `SecretScan.findings` (id, rule, path + `more_paths`, first/last commit, commits, blobs, `preview`) with the skipped counters and the "not exhaustive" and "rotate it first" sentences; "Remove from history" appends `ContentReplace { find: Secrets { literals: [SecretText(id)...], scan: true }, with: "" }` to the recipe (several ticked findings = several ids in one rule; none ticked + button "all" = empty literals); drop `model::without_scan`; the preview then reports the usual counts, and the Commits drill-in shows the redacted blob diff. Findings are stale after any rewrite (rescan); a stale id answers `InvalidRule`. i18n: a `[rewrite_secrets]` section (tab title, column heads, "rotate first" text, "not exhaustive", skipped counters, one label per rule id), added at the end of the sections; `RewritePhase::Rewriting` is shown during a scan, so the progress label should depend on the job kind, not the phase.
- **TUI Secrets tab** (`rewrite_view/secrets.rs`, `PreviewTab::Secrets`, the last tab; it works with no preview). The scan is a job like the preview but `busy()` covers both, so only one runs: progress is attributed to the scan only when it differs from the report the connection already held when the scan was sent (`activity.rs` keeps the last report forever, never clears it), and the label is "Scanning" by job kind. The TUI types hold `SecretFinding`s only (preview, paths, commits, counts), so no value can reach a buffer; the unit test `no_secret_value_can_reach_the_screen_in_any_state` and `pty_rewrite_secrets.rs` pin it. "Remove from history" writes one `ContentReplace { Secrets { literals: ids, scan: true }, with: "" }` and **extends** it on the next click (`is_id_rule`). Stale: `stale` flag (set by any apply and by an `InvalidRule` answer) and `result.scope != recipe.scope`. A successful Preview from the Secrets tab switches to Summary (the counts are there). `model::without_scan` is gone: a scanning rule goes to the daemon as it is.

## History rewrite signatures (S19)

- **Where**: `cli-backend/src/history/sign.rs` (`Signer`, `attach`, `check`), the policy in `engine::run_pass`, the smoke test as `GitBackend::rewrite_check_signing` (`OperationKind::RewriteCheckSigning`, a plain read for the daemon, no job), core types `SigningCheck`, `RewriteRefusal::SigningFailed`, `RuleKind::RemoveSignatures`, `RewritePhase::Signing`, `Counts::{resigned, signing_millis}`.
- **Signed in the preview, not at Apply.** A signed commit's id includes its `gpgsig`, so its children's `parent` lines, the ref moves, the plan digest and the pack all depend on the final ids. The preview therefore signs (parents first, inside `run_pass`), the pack holds the signed objects, and Apply is unchanged. The cost is that the preview of a re-sign run takes the signing time and may prompt, and that every preview of the same recipe signs again (a per-run cache by payload hash makes the second `run_pass` of a protected-branch preview free; ssh ed25519 is deterministic, gpg is not, so ids differ between two previews and the digest with them).
- **How a commit is signed** (the way git's `gpg-interface.c` does, no `commit-tree -S`, which would re-create the commit and lose the verbatim headers): the serialised commit without `gpgsig` is the payload; `gpg.format` picks `gpg --status-fd=2 -bsau <key>` (openpgp), `gpgsm` (x509) or `ssh-keygen -Y sign -n git -f <key> [-U] <payload file>` (ssh; a literal `key::...`/`ssh-...` key goes to a temp file and `-U`, the signature is read from `<payload file>.sig`). Programs: `gpg.<format>.program` (`gpg.program` for openpgp), else the default name, resolved with the S2 probe rules (PATH, PATHEXT, Git for Windows' `usr/bin`). Key: `user.signingkey`, or for openpgp the committer's `Name <email>`. The signature becomes a `gpgsig` header **after the last header** (continuation lines start with one space), whatever its place in the old commit.
- **Policy** (`RunOptions::signatures`, still `None` until asked, never a silent default): `strip` (today's behaviour, the warning counts the lost signatures), `resign-signed` (a re-created commit that was signed is signed), `resign-all` (every re-created commit is signed). Unchanged commits keep id and signature in every case; commits that stay as they are need no choice. `RuleKind::RemoveSignatures` makes a signed commit in the active set a changed one on its own, those commits are never re-signed and are not counted in `signed_changed` (no question for them).
- **Smoke test, early**: a re-sign run opens the `Signer` and signs and verifies a dummy payload (`ssh-keygen -Y check-novalidate`, `gpg --verify`) **before the history is walked**; a missing program is `ToolMissing`, anything else (key missing, agent locked, bad passphrase, timeout) is `SigningFailed` with the program's last stderr lines in the detail. Nothing is written and the run directory is removed. The workbench's "Check signing" (row 4 of the question, or `rewrite check-signing`) is the same code, returns the time of one signature (`SigningCheck::millis`) and never fails as an error: a broken setup is a value with `reason`.
- **Progress and cancel**: `Signing` ticks (`done` = signed so far) before each signature, so a cancel lands between two signatures; the preview's run guard removes the run directory, no ref or object moves. `total` is an upper bound (the signed commits for `resign-signed`, all for `resign-all`), made exact only at the end.
- **Traps**: (1) **gpg-agent prompts**: a locked key makes `gpg` ask for the passphrase through `pinentry`, which has no terminal inside the daemon and opens a GUI (or fails, "Inappropriate ioctl") - unlock the agent first (sign anything once, "Check signing" does exactly that), and a signature that needs a prompt nobody sees is killed after 120 s as `SigningFailed`. (2) **pinentry in a TUI**: `pinentry-curses` would draw over the workbench, so the program is started with stdin closed or piped and `GPG_TTY` untouched; use a GUI pinentry or an agent with a cached key. (3) **ssh**: an encrypted key needs `ssh-agent` (`-U` for literal keys, an agent key file for paths); `ssh-keygen` opens `/dev/tty` for a passphrase, which also hangs until the timeout. (4) **Windows**: `gpg.exe` and `ssh-keygen.exe` are found in Git for Windows' `usr/bin` (not on PATH), `gpg.program` may be a Windows path with backslashes, and `user.signingkey = ~/.ssh/id` is expanded with `USERPROFILE`; a Windows `gpg` may print CRLF, which the armored block tolerates. (5) The payload and key temp files live in a private temp directory removed on drop, never under the repository. (6) `verify` of the smoke test checks the pair, not the identity: `git verify-commit` still needs `gpg.ssh.allowedSignersFile` for ssh.
- **TUI**: the existing signature question gets a fourth row "Check signing" (key `4`, mouse, in the same `Answer(i)` hits; it does not close the question) whose result (`RewriteView::sign_check`, `rewrite_view/signing.rs`) is appended to the prompt: the measured time per signature and the estimate for the question's count, or why it fails with the install hint for a missing tool. The Summary shows "Commits signed again: n, in t". Add menu: group "Signatures" with "Remove signatures" (palette: "Rewrite: Remove Signatures...").
- **CLI/MCP**: `--signatures strip|resign-signed|resign-all` (existing), `--remove-signatures` on `preview`/`run`, `rewrite check-signing` (read; exit 2 `signing_failed`/`tool_missing` with the detail; MCP `rewrite_check_signing`). Help keys in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` (French translated, the others English).
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`rewrite_signatures.rs`, `gpg.format=ssh`, a generated ed25519 key, skipped without `ssh-keygen`): re-sign signed signs only the signed ones and the untouched prefix keeps ids and signatures (`git verify-commit`), re-sign all, strip, smoke test refuses a broken key early (also where nothing would be signed) and leaves nothing, cancel between signatures leaves refs/objects/run dirs as they were and the progress counts the signed commits, the rule strips the scope. CLI `check_signing_then_resign_and_remove_signatures_end_to_end`, PTY `re_signing_the_signed_commits_checks_signing_and_leaves_good_signatures`, TUI unit tests for the question text and the snapshot.
- **Perf**: ssh-keygen ed25519 is about 4 ms per signature here (spawn included); gpg is typically 20-100 ms with a warm agent, much more with a smartcard or a prompt. `signing_millis` in the plan and the Summary is the measured total.

## History rewrite trailers and message references (S17)

- **Where**: `core/src/domain/rewrite/trailers.rs` (pure: `find_block`, `parse`, `add`, `remove`, `map_text`, `map_identities`, `map_values`, `person_of`), `hashes.rs` (`update`, `IdIndex`, `HashLookup`, `HashStats`); the engine calls them from `cli-backend/src/history/rules.rs` (`trailer_stage`, the identity rule) and `engine.rs` (the hash update). `Git2Backend` delegates like every rewrite op.
- **The grammar is git's, measured**: `trailers::find_block` re-implements `find_trailer_block_start` of `trailer.c` (no configuration): the block is the **last paragraph** and never the first (the title), it stops before a `---` divider line, `#` lines count for nothing, a key is letters, digits and `-` with optional blanks before the `:`, a line that starts with a blank continues the line above, `Signed-off-by: ` and `(cherry picked from commit ` lines are the recognised kinds. A block is a trailer block when **all** its lines are trailers, or when it has a recognised line and a third of the lines are trailers. `tests/trailer_grammar.rs` (cli-backend) compares `parse` with `git interpret-trailers --parse` on 21 fixed messages and 1500 generated from a pool of lines (seeded, so a failure reproduces); the table in `trailers/tests.rs` pins the same cases without git. Falsified: with the third turned into "any", both go red. Not modelled: `trailer.separators` other than `:`, `trailer.<token>.*`, `--where`; a custom `core.commentChar`.
- **Edits keep every other byte**: `add` inserts one line at the end of the block (or makes a block after a blank line; before a `---` divider; the line ending follows the block's, a message without a final newline stays without one), `remove` takes the trailer with its continuation lines and, when that empties the block, the blank line before it (so `remove(add(m)) == m`), `map_text` replaces inside the raw value only (never in the body), `map_identities` rewrites values that are exactly `Name <email>`. Keys match without regard to case; an added key is written as given. `TrailerIfExists`: `Add` (default; skip only the identical `key: value`, git's `addIfDifferent`), `Replace` (every trailer of the key goes, the new one is last; unchanged when it is already the only one), `Skip`.
- **Rule**: `RuleKind::Trailer(TrailerRule { action: Add|Remove|Map, key, value, if_exists, find, with })`. `Remove` takes the trailers of `key` whose value matches `find` (all when `None`); `Map` replaces `find` by `with` in their values (`$1` in regex mode). `validate_recipe` refuses a bad key, a multi-line value, a missing or uncompilable `find`. A message that is not UTF-8 is never touched (the other message rules' behaviour). The plan row shows the change through `RewriteField::Message`; per-rule counts count messages changed.
- **Identity rules** get `map_trailers` (default **off**, documented in the user guide): the identity trailers of the message (any key whose value is exactly `Name <email>`) that `when` matches are set through `set`, whatever `apply_to` says. The rule's count is commits whose identity **or** trailers changed. Anonymise rules do not map trailers (a gap to close if the maintainer wants it).
- **`update_message_hashes`** (recipe option, **off by default**; the core default used to be on and unused): in the re-creation loop of `run_pass` (`engine.rs`), parents first, every active commit's message goes through `hashes::update`. A candidate is a whole word of 7 to 40 lowercase hex characters; `IdIndex` finds the commits of the walk whose old id starts with it. **One match** that was already re-created gets the new id cut to the **same length**; a commit that keeps its id is left (not counted); **two or more** matches (ambiguous), **none** (unknown, or a plain word such as `deadbeef`) and a match **re-created later in the walk** are left and counted in `Counts::message_hashes_left` (words that are only digits are replaced when they match but never counted, they are numbers far more often than ids). `Counts::message_hashes_updated` counts the replacements; the Summary says both. Trap: the new id depends on the message, so only references to commits **earlier in the walk** can be followed; a cherry-pick source on another branch that sorts later is left.
- **Lua helpers** (`lua_rt.rs`): see S10's list above; `set_trailer` rejects a key with other characters and a multi-line value as a script error, `at` rejects a date that does not exist (`2023-02-29`). They are pure, so they keep the determinism rule. The AI prompt (`gitpoulpi-ai/src/prompts/rewrite_script.rs`) lists them.
- **TUI** (`rules_form.rs`): the Trailer form has the Action dropdown (*Add*, *Remove*, *Change the value*) and, per action, *Value* + *If it already exists* (a dropdown), *Value contains* (a literal) or *Find in value* + *Replace with*. The run option **Update commit ids** is a checkbox at the end of the panes of the rules that edit messages (trailer, replace text, delete lines), like `prune_empty` on the content rules: `FieldId::UpdateHashes` is intercepted in `RewriteView::edit_field` and written to `recipe.options`. The identity form gets **Also map trailers**. The Commits rows show a message change whose subject is the same as `+Key: value` / `-Key: value` (`preview.rs` `message_delta`), else the first line that differs. i18n keys sit in a `# s17` block at the end of `[rewrite_view]`.
- **CLI/MCP**: `--trailer-add KEY=VALUE`, `--trailer-remove KEY`, `--trailer-map KEY:OLD=NEW` (literal), `--map-trailers` (needs the identity flags), `--update-message-hashes` on `rewrite preview` and `rewrite run`; they are rules after `--replace-message`'s. Help keys in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` are English placeholders except French.
- **Tests**: core units (grammar table, add / remove / map / if-exists / line endings / divider, `remove(add(m)) == m`, hash lookup and ambiguity), `trailer_grammar.rs` against git, contract on both backends (`testkit/contract/rewrite_trailers.rs`: add equals `git interpret-trailers --trailer` on six message shapes, if-exists / remove / map change only their lines, identity option off and on, non-UTF-8 untouched, ids follow, invalid rules refused), Lua helper units, TUI form snapshots and tests, PTY `pty_rewrite_trailers.rs` (last 3 commits get a `Signed-off-by`, preview, apply, `git log`, undo), CLI `trailer_flags_*` JSON tests. Falsified: grammar third (unit + differential red) and `IdIndex` ambiguity returning the first match (unit red). The contract test for ids has no real ambiguity (a 7-hex collision cannot be made cheaply); ambiguity is covered by the unit test only.

## History rewrite LFS import and export (S21)

- **Rules**: `RuleKind::LfsImport(LfsImportRule { patterns, min_size })` and `RuleKind::LfsExport(LfsExportRule { patterns })` (the placeholder `LfsMigrate` is gone; recipes never saved it). Patterns are the `GlobSet` of the other content rules (no slash: any depth), `min_size` is "at least N bytes", absent means every match. Both are content rules (partial clone refused, working tree, dirty paths, branch checked out elsewhere). `required_tools()` names `git-lfs` for both: the rule greys in the Add menu with the install hint, and `preview` refuses `ToolMissing` with `LfsUnavailableReason::message()` before the history is read (`GITPOULPI_TOOL_GIT_LFS=missing` is the test seam).
- **Our own engine, not `git lfs migrate`**: `cli-backend/src/history/lfs.rs` (objects, store, missing list), a `Step::LfsImport` / `Step::LfsExport` in `blobs.rs` (one blob step among the others, same blob cache keyed by blob and path), and the root-tree `.gitattributes` handling in `trees.rs` (`lfs_attributes`). Pure text work (`add_attributes`, `remove_attributes`, `attribute_token`) lives in `core/rewrite/lfs.rs`; the pointer text is `domain::LfsPointer` (spec v1, the same type the LFS card reads), so the format is written once.
- **Import streams**: a matching blob is copied from the long-lived `cat-file --batch` through a small buffer (`Reader::blob_copy`) into `<run>/lfs/.part-N` while a SHA-256 is computed, so a multi-gigabyte file never sits in memory; the file is renamed to `<run>/lfs/<oid>` (once per distinct content), the pointer blob goes into the preview pack. A blob that already is a pointer (first 1 KiB parse as spec v1) is left alone, so a second import changes nothing. When an earlier rule of the same run changed the content (a replace), the bytes in memory are hashed instead. The preview writes only inside its run directory, as every preview does.
- **Apply installs the objects before any ref moves** (`lfs::install`): rename into `<lfs storage>/objects/aa/bb/<oid>` (copy to a temporary name and rename when the run directory is on another device), never over an object that is there, so it is atomic and deduplicated; a failure leaves unreferenced objects, never pointers without content. The storage is `lfs.storage` (relative to the common dir) else `<common dir>/lfs`. If the repository's LFS filters are not configured, apply runs `git lfs install --local` first (the same precedent as `lfs pull` and `lfs checkout`: filters and the pre-push hook, this repository only), because the undo's `reset --keep` and `git status` compare through the clean filter. After the ref move and `read-tree -m -u` a best-effort `git lfs checkout` runs; **measured**: `read-tree` already smudges the pointers (git applies the new `.gitattributes` first), the extra checkout is a safety net and removing it does not fail any test.
- **`.gitattributes` per commit**: in the root tree only. Import adds `<pattern> filter=lfs diff=lfs merge=lfs -text` to the root `.gitattributes` of **every rewritten tree that holds a pointer the rule made** (create the file when missing, append when the line is absent, a missing final newline is added, every other byte stays; the entry is placed in git's tree order with a `/` appended for directories, checked with `fsck --strict`). A commit that has no matching file keeps its tree and its id. Export removes the lines of its patterns from **every** rewritten root tree (a file left empty is dropped, so an import followed by an export gives back the original commit ids, which the round-trip contract test asserts). The result per `(.gitattributes blob, rules that acted)` is cached. Nested `.gitattributes` files are never read or written (documented; the cost is a lookup per directory per commit).
- **Export** reads the pointer (blob of at most 1 KiB), looks the object up in the local store and verifies its size and SHA-256 against the pointer, then adds the real blob to the pack (the file is held in memory, unlike the import). Objects that are missing or fail the check are collected over the whole run and the preview is refused as `LfsObjectsMissing` with one `<oid> <path>` per line (capped at 50 and `...`), changing nothing; the workbench lists them and offers "Download LFS objects" (the existing `LfsFetch` with `all_refs` from `origin`), then the preview is run again.
- **Numbers**: `Counts.lfs_blobs`, `lfs_bytes`, `lfs_objects` (import: distinct blobs converted, their bytes, objects waiting in the run directory), `lfs_restored` (export), `lfs_run` (the recipe has an LFS rule). `PathChange::LfsImported` / `LfsExported` make the Commits rows say "moves X to LFS" / "brings X back from LFS" and the drill-in diff shows the pointer text. `RewriteRefusal::LfsObjectsMissing` (code `lfs_objects_missing`) is new.
- **Quirk, documented**: with `min_size`, a file that matches the pattern but is smaller stays a normal blob while `.gitattributes` names its pattern, so `git status` reports it modified (the clean filter would turn it into a pointer) until it is committed or the line is narrowed. `git lfs migrate import --above` has the same shape. The contract test does not pin it.
- **Undo**: nothing LFS-specific is journaled. The rewrite is one `RewriteApply` entry (refs back, `Effect::Files` so `reset --keep` moves the working tree); the real files on disk are the same bytes before and after, which the daemon test asserts through apply, undo and redo. The objects written to the LFS store stay (`git lfs prune` removes them), like the old history's objects until a purge.
- **After apply**: the result panel has "Send LFS objects" (`l`, `RewriteHit::LfsPush`) when the plan's `lfs_run` is set and the run moved refs a remote has: the existing `LfsPush` operation with `all_refs` to the first moved ref's remote. A plain push of the rewritten refs (S13, `git push` without `--no-verify`) triggers the pre-push hook `lfs install` left, which uploads the objects too, so the button is the explicit earlier route. The collaborator note (`note_text_with`) adds a paragraph and the commands `git lfs install` and `git lfs pull` (commands stay English, outside the catalog, like the other git commands of the note); the core's English `note_text` used by the CLI does not know about LFS yet (known gap).
- **CLI/MCP**: `rewrite preview|run --lfs-import PATTERN... [--lfs-min-size BYTES] [--lfs-export PATTERN...]`; recipes carry `lfs_import` and `lfs_export` rules. Help keys exist in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` (English except French).
- **TUI**: Add menu group LFS: "Migrate files to LFS" (`RuleTemplate::LfsImport`, palette `Rewrite: Migrate Files to LFS...`) with *Files to move to LFS* and *Only files of at least* (empty: every match), and "Bring files back from LFS" (`LfsExport`, `Rewrite: Bring Files Back from LFS...`) with *Files to bring back*. New keys sit in `# s21:begin` blocks at the end of their sections in the 17 catalogs (English text outside English and French). Words of the git vocabulary lint: the English texts avoid the literal words push, pull, fetch (the lint fails on them in other languages); commands live in code.
- **Not done**: a "Move to LFS" row action on the Size tab (the rule is one Add-menu entry and a glob away; the tab's glob builder `glob_for` would be the way), `lfs.fetchexclude` and other LFS config, nested `.gitattributes`, bare repositories, SHA-256 object-format repositories (refused as for every rewrite).
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`rewrite_lfs.rs`: pointers equal `git lfs pointer` output, objects in the store with the right bytes and `git check-attr --source` per commit, `.gitattributes` byte-identical elsewhere and not duplicated, unrelated blob ids unchanged, `git lfs fsck` and `fsck --strict` clean, counts and rows; `min_size` and pointers already there; export round trip to the original commit ids; missing objects refused with the list and nothing written); daemon (`rewrite_undo.rs`: apply, undo, redo keep the real file on disk and `status` clean); CLI `assert_cmd` on the JSON (`lfs_flags_import_then_export...`, including the typed refusal); TUI unit tests (`tests/lfs.rs`: forms, summary and rows, the push button and the note, the refusal list and the fetch), `pty_rewrite_lfs.rs` (palette, preview, apply, the Log diff shows the pointer, the file on disk is real, undo), and the existing `a_rule_that_needs_a_missing_tool_is_greyed_and_refuses_to_preview`. All of them skip when git-lfs is missing, like the other LFS checks (`GITPOULPI_REQUIRE_LFS=1` makes that a failure). **Falsified**: a pointer written as `size  N` (two spaces) turned every import test red on both backends and the PTY test red; skipping the `.gitattributes` removal in the export turned the round-trip test red on both backends; removing the best-effort `git lfs checkout` stayed green (see above).

## History rewrite line endings, exec bit and file encoding (S22, S24bis)

Three content rules in the same stage as the S9/S21 ones: `RuleKind::LineEndings(EolRule)`, `StripExec(StripExecRule)`, `Reencode(ReencodeRule)`. The pure parts live in `gitpoulpi-core::domain::rewrite::textconv` (`convert_eol`, `reencode`, `PathFilter`, `Attributes`); `encoding_rs` (already a core dependency, notices already in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`) only does Windows-1252, everything else is hand written because `encoding_rs` has no UTF-16 encoder and maps ISO-8859-1 onto Windows-1252.

- **Line endings**: the user picks the TARGET (`lf`, `crlf`, `cr`). Every `\r\n`, lone `\r` and lone `\n` of a matching text file becomes the target, so mixed files come out uniform (a lone `\r` inside a text file is read as a classic-Mac line end: the price of a rule that also converts CR). Path filters are `paths` (empty: every file) minus `exclude`, through `PathFilter` (the same `GlobSet` as the other rules). Binary = a NUL in the first 8 KiB, skipped and counted in `blobs_skipped_binary`. A file already in the target ending keeps its blob id (`convert_eol` returns `None`).
- **`.gitattributes` (decided default: OFF)**: `follow_attributes` makes the rule read the ROOT `.gitattributes` of each commit: `-text` / `binary` files are skipped, `eol=lf|crlf` files get their own ending instead of the target. Off by default because the user named the target and the files explicitly; nested attribute files and `text=auto` heuristics are not read (a `text`/`text=auto` line just means "text"). Cost: `Content::load_attributes` reads the root tree and the attribute blob (parsed once per blob id, `attrs_parsed`) at each `rewrite_root` and the per-tree cache key is `(tree, prefix, attributes blob id)`, so subtrees are shared between commits with the same attributes; `Blobs::decide`'s cache key gets the `EolAttr` too. Without a following rule none of this runs.
- **Exec bit**: its own kind (`StripExec`), at least one glob (stripping every executable is never meant; validated, and `--strip-exec` needs one). It works on the tree entry in `Content::rewrite_tree` (`strip_exec_by`): mode `100755` becomes `100644`, the blob id is untouched, the path is listed as `Modified`. It needs no blob read, so it also works with `walks` only. `would_change` knows it (a dirty file is told from one the rewrite leaves alone). Symlinks (`120000`) and gitlinks are never touched; the undo is the generic one (the whole rewrite is one journal entry).
- **Encoding**: `reencode(bytes, target, from, fallback)`. Source: `from` when given, else detected per file: byte order mark (UTF-8 BOM, UTF-16 LE/BE), then valid UTF-8, then `fallback` (default Windows-1252, the user's choice in the form). A given `from` is believed (UTF-8 read as Latin-1 is double-encoded: the user asked). Targets: UTF-8, UTF-8 with BOM, UTF-16 LE, UTF-16 BE (both written with their BOM), Windows-1252, ISO-8859-1. `from == target` is refused by validation. Empty files are untouched. A file that is binary (NUL in the first 8 KiB) is skipped, EXCEPT UTF-16 text (a UTF-16 BOM, or `from` UTF-16), whose NULs are normal. **Undecodable** (`Reencoded::Undecodable`): the source cannot read the bytes (invalid UTF-8 with `from` UTF-8, an odd UTF-16 length, unpaired surrogates) or the target cannot hold a character (a euro sign in ISO-8859-1): the file is left byte for byte and counted in the new `Counts::blobs_skipped_undecodable` (Summary line, `sum_skipped_undecodable`). Never replaced by U+FFFD or `?`.
- **Composition**: one `Blobs::compute` pass applies the steps in recipe order on the same bytes, so `reencode` then `eol` sees the converted text, and a file turned into UTF-16 by an earlier rule is binary to a later line-ending rule (test `check_rewrite_eol_and_encoding_rules_compose_in_recipe_order`). The path stage still runs last on the finished tree (S20/S21 rule), so the filters name the original paths. `reach()` limits the walk to the directories the include globs lead through; an empty `paths` means the walk reaches everywhere.
- **Surfaces**: Add menu group Content (three entries, palette `Rewrite: Convert Line Endings...`, `Remove the Executable Bit...`, `Convert File Encoding...`); the forms use the usual choice fields (dropdowns open on click) for *Convert to*, *Files are in* (first entry: detect) and *Fallback*; *Files* / *Except files* are the same glob fields as the other rules. CLI: `--eol lf|crlf|cr --eol-paths GLOB... [--eol-exclude GLOB...] [--eol-follow-attributes]`, `--strip-exec GLOB... [--strip-exec-exclude GLOB...]`, `--reencode TARGET [--from SOURCE] [--encoding-fallback ENC] --encoding-paths GLOB... [--encoding-exclude GLOB...]`; the `--eol` and `--reencode` flags require their paths flag in the CLI (a recipe may leave `paths` empty: every file). The flags work for MCP through the shared `Cli` tree (no MCP table change). New keys sit in `# s22:begin` blocks at the end of `[palette]` and `[rewrite_view]` in the 17 catalogs (English outside French).
- **Not done**: nested `.gitattributes`, the `core.autocrlf` / `working-tree-encoding` attributes, source encodings beyond the six (Shift_JIS, KOI8-R...), per-file encoding detection by statistics, a diff view for the exec-bit change (the "diff of the diff" shows the file as unchanged).
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`rewrite_textconv.rs`: CRLF to LF with mixed endings, binary and unmatched/excluded blob ids unchanged and the id git gives the expected text; LF to CRLF and CR; follow `.gitattributes`; exec bit stripped only on matching paths with blob ids unchanged; Latin-1 and Windows-1252 to UTF-8 (e acute, euro), UTF-16 with BOM to UTF-8, undecodable and binary skipped and counted; every target; unmappable characters; composition order), unit tests in `textconv.rs`, CLI `assert_cmd` on the JSON (`text_flags_convert_line_endings_the_exec_bit_and_the_encoding`), PTY `pty_rewrite_textconv.rs` (palette, type `*.txt`, preview, apply: history and disk are CRLF, undo restores both). **Falsified**: removing the binary check of the line-ending step turned `..._to_lf_converts_text_only...` (and the composition test) red on both backends, and the PTY test went red when `convert_eol` was forced to change nothing; making the undecodable arm a no-op turned the two re-encoding checks red (the skip count is part of the contract).

## History rewrite size analysis (S18)

- **Where**: pure data in core (`rewrite/analysis.rs`: `SizeAnalysis`, `BlobRow`, `FolderRow`, `DeletedRow`, `BlobState`, `glob_for`, `human_size`); the walk in `cli-backend/src/history/analyze.rs`; `GitBackend::rewrite_analyze(repo, &Scope, top, sink) -> SizeAnalysis` (git2 delegates; `top` is one more argument than the brief's signature, so the cap is the caller's); IPC `RewriteAnalyze` (`AnalyzeArgs { scope, top }`, `top` 0 = 50, clamped to 1..=5000; a **job**: `Policy::Free`, one per repository, progress as `Event::RewriteProgress`: `Walking` while the history is read, `Rewriting` while blob sizes are asked for; the TUI labels it "Analysing" by job kind, as for the scan).
- **How it reads**: `rev-list` for the commit count (progress, `EmptyScope`), `git log --raw -r -z --root --no-renames -m` streamed (one NUL-separated token at a time, the child killed on cancel or error) for every blob added or removed with its path and the commit index, `ls-tree -r HEAD` for what HEAD holds, then one `cat-file --batch-check='%(objectname) %(objectsize) %(objectsize:disk)'` in chunks of 4096 (a writer thread per chunk, like `Reader::commits`, so a big exchange cannot deadlock). No blob content is read. `-m` makes a merge print against each parent so a blob only a conflict resolution introduced is seen (the header repeats per parent and is deduplicated). Submodule entries (mode 160000) are skipped; symlinks count.
- **Definitions** (the traps): a blob is **in HEAD** when HEAD holds that very content, **replaced** when one of its paths is in HEAD with other content (an old version), **deleted** when none of its paths is. `first_commit` is the commit that introduced it, `last_commit` the last one that touched it (the removing commit, else the introducing one), by `log` order, not the last commit that still contained it. A folder's size is the sum of the **unique** blobs ever stored below it (a blob at two paths counts once per folder it is under, once in the totals); `in_head` says HEAD still has files in it. `deleted` lists paths missing from HEAD with their versions' sizes. Totals (`bytes_total`, `disk_total`, `bytes_in_head`, `bytes_history_only`) are over the whole scope and ignore the cap; `*_cut` count the rows the cap left out. `disk_size` is the object database size, so a packed delta counts as the delta: shown per file, never summed into a "frees" figure. A blob seen only in a scope's boundary (a `Range`'s base) is missed: the analysis is of the commits the scope walks. HEAD is the reference for "deleted" whatever the scope.
- **TUI Size tab** (`rewrite_view/size.rs`, `PreviewTab::Size`, last tab; works with no preview). `F7` (`Bind::RewriteAnalyze`, also the palette entry) opens it and runs; `busy()` covers it like the scan, progress is attributed by the same baseline trick. The tab asks for 200 rows per list and sorts them itself (size, path, deleted first; `Row` abstracts blob/folder/deleted). Ticks are keyed by blob id or path and survive a re-sort; a new analysis clears them. "Remove from history" adds a `RemovePaths` rule of `glob_for(path)` (a name without a slash gets a leading `/`, `*?[` are escaped as classes) skipping globs already in a rule; "Remove every file over" writes `RemoveBlobsOver { bytes: smallest ticked - 1 }` (the engine drops files strictly over the limit) and lowers an existing size rule instead of adding a second. The estimate line says "up to about" and "only after a purge and a gc". A successful Preview from the tab switches to Summary. **Trap fixed on the way**: list rows must be `ui.fine` hit targets; the preview pane registers its own coarse target before the rows, so a coarse row never receives the click (the Secrets rows had this bug and were fixed too; unit-tested for the Size rows).
- **CLI/MCP**: `gitpoulpi rewrite analyze [--all-local | --ref R... | --since REV] [--top N]` (read; MCP `rewrite_analyze`), JSON `SizeAnalysis`. Help keys in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` are English placeholders except French.
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`rewrite_analyze.rs`: order, first/last commit, states, folders, deleted list, totals, cap and cut counts, scope, cancel, empty repo, nothing written; merge commit, odd file names, shared content; the rows' rules really remove what they name and the size rule drops the big blobs); daemon (`rewrite_undo.rs`: a job with progress that changes no ref, cancel); CLI `assert_cmd` on the JSON; TUI unit and insta snapshots (`tests/size.rs`: job label and cancel, three lists, sort orders, rules written, keys and clicks alike, hit targets, stale), `pty_rewrite_size.rs` (analyse, tick the deleted file, Remove from history, Preview, Apply, gone from HEAD's history, Undo brings it back; the same by keys). Falsified: sorting ascending turned the contract ordering test red on both backends; a rule that names nothing turned both PTY tests and the unit test red.
- **Perf** (`xtask perf --rewrite`, release): 10000 commits, 30001 blobs in 0.59 s (the secrets scan of the same fixture: 1.78 s).

## History rewrite: pushing it back (S13)

- **What a push needs is stored at apply**: `apply` writes `moved.json` (the plan's `RefChange`s, each with its `PushState::OnRemote { remote, remote_oid }`) beside `ref-map` in `<git-dir>/gitpoulpi/rewrite/<run>/`. The plan file is gone by then, so push, note and maps read `moved.json`, which is why they work from another process, after a daemon restart, and only for 24 h (the preview sweep). The run id `latest` means the newest `moved.json` (`store::resolve_run`); CLI `--run` defaults to it. `commit-map` is written by the preview, `ref-map` and `moved.json` by the apply.
- **`GitBackend::push_rewritten(repo, &PushRewritten) -> PushRewrittenOutcome`** (async, network; both backends, git2 delegates to `SystemGitBackend`, like every network call). Per remote one `git push --porcelain --atomic --force-with-lease=<ref>:<old remote id> ... <remote> <new>:<ref> ...` (`history/push.rs`). **Trap, pinned by a test**: the lease is the id the *preview* recorded, never `--force-with-lease=<ref>` alone (that leases against the tracking ref, which an auto-fetch has already moved to the collaborator's commit, so the push would overwrite them). The contract test moves the tracking ref to the rival commit too; with the bare lease it goes red. There is no `--force` anywhere in the file. The source is the recorded new *oid*, not the ref name, and a ref that no longer holds it (undone since) is `NotPushed`, never pushed.
- **Which refs**: the run's moved refs that are `OnRemote` *and* that the remote has by that name (a same-named `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch>`; a tag is in the tag mirror by construction). The preview's `remote_of_branch` falls back to any remote's tracking ref so a local branch that merely contains pushed commits reads `OnRemote`; pushing that with another ref's lease would be wrong, so such a branch is neither pushed nor in the note (`RewriteRecord::on_remote` carries the filtered names; `note_refs` uses it). A branch whose upstream has another name is skipped too (same-name assumption; failing safe). A deleted ref (`new: None`) pushes `:<ref>` with its lease.
- **Statuses** per ref: `pushed`, `would_push` (dry run), `up_to_date`, `rejected_remote_moved` (porcelain `stale info`, `fetch first`, `non-fast-forward`), `rejected_protected_by_host` (the remote message says protected / GH006 / GH013 / not allowed to force push), `rejected_other` (git's words in `detail`), `skipped_protected`, `not_pushed` (`atomic push failed` on the others, or the local ref no longer holds the rewrite). A rejection is a status in the answer, not an error; an error is only "nothing was even tried" (unreachable remote, credentials refused, a hook). git runs with `LC_ALL=C` here (`run_full_async_english`) because the `--atomic` refusal is recognised by its words (`does not support --atomic`); then each ref is pushed alone and the answer says `atomic_fallback`. `--atomic` is only asked when a remote has more than one ref.
- **Protected branches**: names and `*` patterns; sources are `rewrite.protected_branches` (config, default `main`, `master`, `develop`, `release/*`, read by the daemon at every preview) and the workflow's protected bases; the daemon puts them in `scope_options.protected_branches`, so a request that reaches the backend directly (contract tests) only sees `workflow.toml`. The recipe's `include_protected` (CLI `--include-protected B`; the TUI's per-branch box and the all/none toggle fill it) decides at preview/apply; `RefChange.protected` records it, and **the push honours it again**: a protected ref is sent only when `PushRewritten::include_protected` names it or `include_all_protected` is set. Consequence for the maintainer's default (`main` protected): rewriting a pushed `main` needs the include twice (apply, then push). That is deliberate: the second gate is the one that publishes. Existing tests that rewrite a pushed `main` through the daemon or CLI now pass `include_protected: ["main"]` (`rewrite_undo.rs::recipe()`, `cli/tests/rewrite.rs`).
- **Journal story**: the push is not journaled and not undoable (like any push). It leaves `<git-dir>/gitpoulpi/rewrite/pushed.json` (never swept: it outlives the run directory) listing `{name, remote, new}`. `journal/risk.rs` reads it for a `RewriteApply` entry: undoing a rewrite whose ref was pushed asks a confirmation saying the remote keeps the new history (the older upstream-tracking check stays as the fallback for refs pushed by hand). The undo itself only moves local refs. A dry run writes nothing.
- **Daemon**: `RewritePush` is a network job (`traits().network`, label "Pushing the rewritten history", `Policy::Free`, one per repository via `Dedupe::Reject`), and goes through `lfs_preflight` like a push (it installs the LFS pre-push hook, so LFS objects upload with the refs). `RewriteNote` and `RewriteMaps` are plain reads (maps only writes when `out` is given). The CLI asks for `--yes`: without it `rewrite push` runs a dry run and answers exit 3 with the list; with nothing to send it just answers.
- **Note text**: `core::domain::rewrite::{note_refs, note_text}` build the English text (CLI, MCP, copy) from the record; the structured `NoteRef`s (name, short, kind, remote, old, new; 12-digit ids) are what the TUI renders with the `[rewrite_note]` i18n section. Commands are never translated.
- **Maps**: `commit-map` / `ref-map` are git-filter-repo's format (header line, then `old new`; a dropped commit maps to forty zeros; ref-map has the ref name as third column). `rewrite maps --out DIR` copies them.
- **TUI result panel** (`rewrite_view/push.rs`): `finish_apply` builds the `PushPanel` from `RewriteOutcome.moved` (refs `OnRemote`). **Protected refs start unticked even though the recipe included them**: the Refs tab's box decided the local rewrite, this list's box is the second gate, the one that publishes (the backend enforces the same: a protected ref is sent only when named). The request is `refs: Some(ticked)` + `include_protected: ticked protected` (`PushRewritten::refs` was added for this: an unticked ref is not sent and not in the answer). A rejected row's "Fetch and review" returns `RewriteKeyOutcome::FetchReview`: the caller closes the workbench and runs the palette's Fetch. The note is rendered by `push::note_text` from the structured `NoteRef`s with `rewrite_note.*`; a test pins that every git command of the core's English note is in it. Copy goes through `clipboard::copy_shared`. The push is `busy()` (no close while it runs); it has no cancel because its job id is not known to the view. Local-only moved refs are not listed once there is something to push.


## History rewrite: the host's protection (S23)

- **Client side, and only for a linked repository.** The daemon never talks to
  a host. The frontends read what the host protects (`gitpoulpi-hosting`:
  `HostingClient::protected_branches`, then `protection::lookup`, which turns
  any failure into `Unknown(reason)`: a token without the scope, a 404, a
  client that cannot) and put the answer in the recipe's
  `scope_options.host_protection` (`HostProtection` in `gitpoulpi-core`,
  `domain/rewrite/host_protection.rs`: kind, project, web base, `patterns`,
  `asked`, `unknown`). `protection::locate` decides who may be asked: a saved
  pulls or issues source, or a pinned profile, makes the repository `linked`;
  otherwise the profile that owns a remote (or the remote's own URL, by host
  name) only serves the how-to links. The TUI calls it from `HostingUi::
  rewrite_host` (a thread, answered on a channel the view polls), the CLI/MCP
  from `HostingEnv::rewrite_host_protection` (`hosting_cmd/protection.rs`).
- **The daemon only merges names.** `rewrite_runs::preview` appends the
  patterns to `scope_options.protected_branches`, next to the config's and the
  workflow's, so a host-protected branch gets everything a protected one has:
  excluded when pushed, `include_protected` per branch, the push's second gate.
  Nothing in the backend knows about hosts. The answer is never saved with a
  recipe (`saved_recipe` drops it; `request_recipe` adds it).
- **What each provider exposes** (token in `docs/user/hosting-integration.md`
  "Branch protection, for a history rewrite"): GitHub `branches?protected=true`
  plus active branch rulesets (list, then `rulesets/{id}` for `ref_name`
  includes; excludes are ignored, which only ever protects too much);
  GitLab `protected_branches`; Azure DevOps `policy/configurations` for the
  repository (enabled and blocking, `Exact`/`Prefix`/`DefaultBranch`);
  Bitbucket Cloud `branch-restrictions` kinds `push`, `force`, `delete`;
  Data Center `branch-permissions/2.0/.../restrictions` kinds
  `fast-forward-only`, `pull-request-only`, `read-only`. A source that fails
  while another answers leaves a note (`ProtectedBranches::note`), both failing
  is `Unknown`. Branching-model restrictions are not resolved.
- **The how-to is data, not prose.** `HostProtection::howto(branches)` returns
  three `HowtoWay`s (`Way`, `HowStep` codes, `links`) and the `Loss` list; the
  TUI words them from `[rewrite_host]` keys (one per `HowStep::code`, git words
  as terms), the CLI prints `Howto::text()` (English) and the JSON. The pages:
  GitHub `settings/branches` and `settings/rules`, GitLab
  `-/settings/repository#js-protected-branches-settings`, Azure DevOps
  `{org}/{project}/_settings/repositories`, Bitbucket Cloud
  `admin/branch-restrictions`, Data Center `projects/KEY/repos/slug/settings`;
  the default branch and the deletion have their own. Never done for the user.
- **The push classifier reads stderr too.** A summary `(pre-receive hook
  declined)` plus `protected` / `GH006` / `TF402455` / `not allowed to force
  push` / `branch restriction` on the remote's stderr is
  `RejectedProtectedByHost` (GitHub says it in the summary, the others only on
  stderr); an ordinary hook failure stays `RejectedOther`.
- **Traps.** `howto_offered` needs a host in the view: a repository whose
  remote is no known host has no button. `request_recipe` is the only place
  the host answer joins the recipe; adding it to `self.recipe` would leak it
  into saved recipes. The sv catalog's local `prs` term still holds the English
  "merge requests", so `rewrite_host.loss_pull_requests` is in its baseline.
- **Tests.** Per provider with recorded JSON through `RecordedTransport`
  (parsed list, missing scope is unknown, never a socket); the daemon test
  `rewrite_host_protection.rs` (excluded by default, ticks back in, unknown
  changes nothing); the TUI view tests (`rewrite_view/tests/host.rs`), the PTY
  test `pty_rewrite_host.rs` and the CLI test `cli/tests/rewrite_host.rs` (a
  hook that says what GitHub says, `origin` named a GitHub URL).

## History rewrite: .mailmap (S16)

- **Where**: pure model in core (`rewrite/mailmap.rs`: `Mailmap::parse/resolve/extend`, `line_for`, `merged`, `problem`, `IdentityCount`/`IdentityList`/`MailmapAdded`); `GitBackend::{mailmap_resolve, mailmap_identities, mailmap_add}`; `OperationKind::{MailmapIdentities, MailmapResolve, MailmapAdd}` (IPC args in `gitpoulpi-ipc/src/mailmap.rs`); `cli-backend/src/history/mailmap.rs`; daemon `mailmap.rs` (setting, cache stamp, blame/file-history mapping); TUI `rewrite_view/identities.rs`; CLI `gitpoulpi mailmap list|check|add` (`commands/mailmap.rs`) and `rewrite preview|run --mailmap [--mailmap-file F]`; MCP `mailmap_list`, `mailmap_check` (read), `mailmap_add` (write).
- **Semantics** are git's (`mailmap.c`): entries keyed by the old email (case-insensitive) and optionally the old name (case-insensitive); a name-specific entry beats an email-only one; of equal keys the later line wins; `mailmap.file` is read after `.mailmap`. Not read: `mailmap.blob` (and `HEAD:.mailmap` only in a bare repository). The core parser indexes entries by lower-cased email: a lookup is a hash probe, so a 100 000-commit Log or rule pass does not scan the file per commit.
- **libgit2's `Mailmap` is not used.** Its lookup compares the email case-sensitively, git's does not; `check_mailmap_resolve_matches_git_check_mailmap` and `check_log_shows_mailmapped_authors_like_git` were red on it. `Git2Backend` therefore loads the same files and resolves with core's parser (no process either way), and the contract suite pins both backends against `git check-mailmap` / `git log --format=%aN%x01%aE`.
- **Display** (`log.use_mailmap`, default on): the **daemon** owns it (read per request with `Settings::resolve_ambient`, like `git.default_encoding`; a client's `LogOptions::use_mailmap` is overwritten). The Log asks the backend for `%aN/%aE` (system) or maps in the revwalk (git2): no extra process. Blame and file history are mapped in the daemon after the backend answered (`mailmap::map_blame`/`map_file_history`: one `mailmap_resolve` over the *distinct* identities). The Log is a cached read: its cache key carries `mailmap::stamp` (nothing while the setting is off, else the worktree `.mailmap`'s mtime and size), so toggling the setting or editing the file by hand re-reads; `MailmapAdd` has `ChangeScope::LOG`, which invalidates the cache. Only the **author** is mapped for display (no view shows the committer). The workspace Search index (`commit_messages`) still holds stored author names. Log search (client side) matches the shown (mapped) name and email, so the stored old email no longer finds the commit while the setting is on; the stored identity is one toggle away.
- **Identities** (`mailmap_identities(scope, options)`): one `git log --format=%an%x01%ae%x01%cn%x01%ce` over the scope resolved by `history::refs::resolve` (`Selection` is `--no-walk`, `Range` is the tip minus the base), counted per pair as author and as committer, most commits first, plus `mapped_to` from one `check-mailmap` call over the distinct pairs, plus `has_mailmap`. No refusal for an operation in progress or a shallow clone (a read).
- **Merge into** (`mailmap_add(canonical, from, file)`) appends `Canonical <c@e> Old <o@e>` (the four-part form: it matches that name and email only; an old identity without a name gives `Canonical <c@e> <o@e>`), once each, skipping the canonical identity itself and lines already in the file. `file` is repo-relative and must be called `.mailmap` (default the root one). It is journaled like `gitignore_add`: `is_snapshot_op`, the path snapshot of `.mailmap` before and after (a file that did not exist is removed by undo; byte-exact otherwise; refused with `PathChanged` once edited), label `Merge identities into <name>`. Undo story tested in `daemon/tests/mailmap.rs` (both backends) and the PTY test. Nothing is committed.
- **Rule `Mailmap { apply_to, file }`** runs through the identity engine (`rules::apply_mailmap`, `load_mailmap`: the worktree's `.mailmap`, or `file` relative to the worktree or absolute; unreadable is `InvalidRule`). Its result equals `git log --use-mailmap` of the old history (`check_rewrite_mailmap_rule_matches_git_use_mailmap`: authors, committers, messages, trees and dates compared against plain git). A second run changes nothing. Taggers are not touched.
