- **TUI**: the **Identities** tab is the sixth `PreviewTab` and works without a preview (like Secrets): `poll_identities` reads the list when the tab is on screen and none is held for the recipe's scope (a request through the `Requester`, never on the render thread); `IdentitiesState` holds the list, the cursor, the ticks, the pending request replies, the "Merge into..." dialog (`MergePick`, painted in front like the purge confirmation, its hit rects first) and what the last merge wrote. Keys: `Space`/`Enter` tick, `m` merge, `r` refresh, `u` undo (after a merge), Check all/none by the existing `Alt+A`-family actions; every button has one. **Undo** hands the journal's undo to the caller (`RewriteKeyOutcome::Undo`, the result panel's way): the workbench closes first. The rows put the counts at the right only when they fit beside the longest name; otherwise the cursor row's counts take the last line. The six tabs would collide with the progress bar at 110 columns, so while a job runs the tabs that do not fit are left out (`progress_need`, a stable width).
- **The offer** (`mailmap_offer`): the plan has a ref already on a remote and an enabled `Identity` rule that changed commits; the Summary shows the sentence and **Use .mailmap** (`m`). `use_mailmap_instead` ticks, when the list arrives, the identities the enabled identity rules match (`CompiledMatch`) and offers each rule's `set` identity (when it has a name and an email) as a candidate to keep, even though no commit has it.
- **Perf** (`xtask perf`, rows `log 5000 (cold, mailmap off|on|.mailmap of 300 entries)`): cold reads (three asks with different limits, so none is cached), same budget as `log 5000`; numbers in `perf-budgets.md`.
- **Tests**: core unit tests (the four line forms, precedence, `merged`, `problem`); contract on both backends (`testkit/contract/mailmap.rs`: Log display vs `git log --format=%aN%x01%aE`, `mailmap_resolve` vs `git check-mailmap`, identities counts and `mapped_to`, `mailmap_add` once/refusals, the rule vs `--use-mailmap`, a given file and a missing one); daemon (`tests/mailmap.rs`: merge, Log/blame/file-history follow, undo/redo, existing file byte-exact, `PathChanged` refusal, the setting off); CLI (`cli/tests/mailmap.rs`, JSON); TUI model and insta snapshots (`rewrite_view/tests/identities.rs`); PTY (`pty_rewrite_mailmap.rs`: two emails of one person, the Log shows the canonical name after "Merge into", Undo restores; the pushed-rule offer).

## History rewrite: purge (S15)

- **Where**: `core::domain::rewrite::purge` (request, outcome, blockers, steps, `PurgeCheck`, the host checklist: pure data and English text), `GitBackend::rewrite_purge(repo, &PurgeRequest, progress)` (`cli-backend/src/history/purge.rs`, git2 delegates), `core::journal` (`EntryState::Purged`, `Journal::mark_purged`, `UndoRefusal::Purged`), the daemon's `journal/purge.rs` (`RewritePurgeCheck`, a plain read; `RewritePurge`, a job), IPC `PurgeArgs { entries, all }` (neither: the newest rewrite that is not purged).
- **What "old history" is**: the objects reachable from the entries' old ref values and from none of the new ones (`T`), by two full `rev-list --objects` listings and a set difference. **Trap**: `rev-list --objects A --not B` is not that: it leaves the blobs of B's older commits in. A new value that another selected entry replaces (rewrite 1's result, which rewrite 2 rewrote again) is dropped from "kept", so a purge of both removes the middle generation too.
- **Blockers come first, and a blocked purge changes nothing.** Candidates: every ref except the pins being dropped, each `stash@{n}` (the stash list is the reflog of `refs/stash`; the ref's own tip is only the newest), the detached HEADs of all worktrees. One `rev-list <all candidates> ^<new tips>` says whether anything reaches `T`; only then one `rev-list` per candidate says which (`PurgeBlocker { kind, name, commits }`: `branch`, `tag`, `remote_tracking` (incl. the tag mirror), `stash`, `worktree`, `undo_pin`, `notes`, `replace`, `other`). A blocked run is still an answer, not an error (`PurgeStatus::Blocked`). Dropping pins whose objects stay alive would lose undo for nothing, hence the order. Index contents (a staged old blob) and alternates are not looked at; if they keep an object, the run ends `Incomplete` with `remaining`.
- **Pins of other entries**: almost every earlier journaled operation pinned a commit of the old history, so the daemon dry-runs, reads the `undo_pin` blockers whose name belongs to this journal (`pin_owner`: `refs/gitpoulpi/undo/<pin_ns><id>/<n>`), adds those entries (state `Done`, any kind; `PurgeEntryInfo::rewrite = false`) with their pins and no refs, and repeats until nothing new turns up. They become `Purged` too, which is what the plan's "Undo will no longer be possible for N operations" counts. A pin of another worktree's journal stays a blocker.
- **The run**: delete the pins (`update-ref --stdin`), `reflog expire`, remove the applied runs' folders (the ones whose `moved.json` lists an old tip; `pushed.json` stays), `gc --prune=now` with `gc.reflogExpire(Unreachable)=never` so gc expires no more than we did, then `cat-file --batch-check` over all of `T`: `Purged` when none is left, `Incomplete` otherwise. Steps reported: Checking, Pins, Reflogs, Collecting, Verifying (job phase + percent; no `RewritePhase` variant, the TUI matches that enum exhaustively).
- **Reflogs, conservatively**: `reflog expire --expire=never --expire-unreachable=<future> <ref>` drops exactly the entries whose old **or** new id is unreachable from the ref's tip (the rewrite's own entry has the old tip as its old id, so it goes) and keeps every other entry. It runs on the reflogs of the refs the entries moved, on HEAD when its branch moved, and on any reflog with an entry naming an object of `T` (found with one `reflog show --all --format="%H %gD"`; other worktrees' HEAD logs one by one). `refs/stash` and undo pins are never expired. Side effect, documented: such a ref also loses entries for commits it abandoned long ago. **Trap, red-green tested**: the cutoff must be in the future (`now + 1h`): an entry written in the same second as the purge is not "older than now" and survives, so the rewrite's entry kept the old tip alive (the contract test was red with `now`).
- **Journal order**: the daemon marks the entries `Purged` and saves the journal **before** the backend deletes anything, then runs; if the real run finds a blocker the dry run did not (a ref moved in between, under the exclusive lock only an outside process can), the saved copy goes back. Crash in the middle: entries say `Purged` with pins maybe still there, which is the safe side. `EntryState::Purged` entries stay in `undo`/`redo`/`retired` and the listing; `check_preconditions` answers `UndoRefusal::Purged { entry }` (forgettable: `undo-forget` drops it to reach the entries below); `restore_offer` / `plan_restore` treat it as "not restorable" like any non-`Done` state; the outside-change snapshot ignores it.
- **Job**: `RewritePurge` is exclusive (not a read), `Policy::UntilPoint(NotCancellableReason::Purging)`: cancel works while it is queued, and from `enter_point("Purging")` on it is refused with the reason (new variant, TUI wording line `jobs.why_purging`). One per repository (`Dedupe::Reject`). Classification `""`, scope `REFS|LOG`.
- **CLI/MCP**: `gitpoulpi rewrite purge [--entry ID...|--latest|--all] [--yes]`. Blocked: exit 4 with the `PurgeCheck` (also without `--yes`: there is nothing to confirm). No `--yes`: exit 3 with `needs_confirmation: { prompt, check }`. `--yes`: runs exactly the rewrite entries the check named; `{ outcome, hosts }`, exit 4 for `blocked` / `incomplete`. A non-rewrite or unknown entry id is `RewriteRefused(Stale, ...)` (exit 2). The MCP tool `rewrite_purge` is destructive and **not listed** (nor callable: the refusal names the key) unless `[mcp] allow_history_purge = true`, a `config.toml`-only key (no Settings row; `policy::hidden`). Undo off (`general.undo_enabled = false`): there is no journal to pick from, so the purge is refused.
- **Host checklist** (`HostKind::of_url`, by host name; `HostStep::text()` English for the CLI, `rewrite_purge.host_<code>` i18n for the TUI): rotate the secret, push the rewritten refs, then GitHub (support for cached views and PR refs, forks, recreate), GitLab (repository cleanup with the `commit-map`, housekeeping, recreate), Azure DevOps (support, recreate and empty the recycle bin), Bitbucket (support, forks, recreate), anything else (server-side reflog expiry and gc), and tell the collaborators. The wording of the host steps follows each provider's documented way as of 2026; it is advice, nothing in it is executed.
- **What the TUI checklist screen needs** (built: `rewrite_view/purge.rs`, an overlay of the workbench like the blob diff drill-in; `RewriteEntry::Purge` for the palette; the first check fixes `PurgeArgs.entries` to the rewrite ids it names so every re-check and the run are about the same entries; job progress is read from `jobs_ui::running_progress`, the daemon reports the English `PurgeStep::name()` as the phase): call `RewritePurgeCheck` (re-call after every fix) and render `PurgeCheck`: `entries` (undo lost; `rewrite == false` rows are "also"), `outcome.blockers` with `kind` -> `rewrite_purge.blocker_<kind>` (`{name}`) and the fix buttons (Fetch for `remote_tracking`, Drop for `stash` via `StashDrop` after a confirmation, none for the rest), `outcome.old_commits/old_blobs/reflog_entries/reflogs` -> `objects_lost`, `reflog_lost`, `hosts[].steps` -> `host_title` + `host_<code>` (`host_github` etc. name the provider), `prompt`-equivalent text from `undo_lost`, `no_stop`, `rotate_first`. The `[Purge]` button is enabled when `PurgeCheck::can_run()`; it sends `RewritePurge { entries: <the ids with rewrite == true> }` (a job: progress from the job's `phase`/`percent`, the phase names are English from `PurgeStep::name()`, the localized ones are `step_*`; Cancel is refused with `NotCancellableReason::Purging` once running) and shows `PurgeOutcome`: `purged` -> `done` (`{n}` = `old_objects`), `incomplete` -> `incomplete` (`{n}` = `remaining`), `blocked` -> back to the list. The result panel's `[Purge old objects...]` button after an Apply opens it. The journal row of a purged entry already reads "(purged)" (`safety_panel/label.rs`), and `undo_purged` is the refusal text. i18n keys exist in all 17 catalogs (English placeholders in the 16 others, for the next translation round).
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`rewrite_purge.rs`: old blobs and commits missing + fsck `--full --strict` clean + reflogs free of old ids + a dry run that changes nothing + step order, a stash blocking until dropped, a branch / tag / remote-tracking ref / detached worktree blocking and clearing, another entry's pin, stashes and an unrelated reflog kept, an empty request); core (`mark_purged`, the refusal, `HostKind`, the confirmation text); daemon (`rewrite_undo.rs`: two rewrites purged as a job, entries `Purged`, undo refused with the typed reason, pins gone, a blocked purge marks nothing and undo still works, other operations' pins joining the check); CLI `assert_cmd` (exit 3, 4, 0, undo exit 2); MCP (tool absent by default, present with the key, refused without `allow_mutations`). Falsified: `--prune=now` replaced by a 2-week prune turned the contract file red (`Incomplete`, 8 objects remain); the reflog cutoff `now` turned `check_rewrite_purge_deletes_the_old_objects` red; skipping the `Purged` check in `check_preconditions` turned the daemon test red (`Interrupted` instead of `Purged`).
- **Dev mode and the "newest" launcher** (maintainer, 2026-10-22; `gitpoulpi_config::dev_mode`, `desktop::Request::newest`, `alias::Env::newest`, `commands/launch.rs::command_for_click`). `general.dev_mode` (`Option<bool>`, absent = auto: exe in `<root>/target/<profile>/` with `Cargo.toml` + `crates/gitpoulpi-tui`) is never a Settings row; it gates `ui.launcher_build = current|newest`, a combobox shown in Settings > Launcher only in dev mode (the row is left out of the pool, so the palette and search do not offer it either). Newest = same file name in `target/dist` or `target/release`, whichever has the later mtime (never debug/custom). **Click time:** `gitpoulpi launch` (and `gitpoulpi-launch.exe` on Windows) swaps every `gitpoulpi*` program of `target/dist|release` in the command it starts for the newest build, so Windows `.lnk` and Linux `.desktop` (both run `launch`) follow builds without a rewrite. macOS has no launch helper: the bundle is written with the newest build at sync time only. **No ping-pong:** with `newest`, `status` accepts the dist OR release spelling (Windows: searches the `.lnk` bytes for either path, pinned copies included; Linux/macOS: compares against the file either build would write); `install` and `sync_pinned` write the newest build. Aliases: a `.cmd` shim / symlink cannot choose at run time, so `create` points at the newest at creation time and `status` treats either profile as `Installed`. Outside newest mode nothing changed. **Traps:** the paths in a `.lnk` are matched as text, so variants must be built with `join` (backslashes) like the written ones; `launcher_sync` includes the flag in its change key so toggling it syncs at once. Config only, no git mutation: no undo story.

## History rewrite paths (S20)

- **Where**: pure model in core (`rewrite/paths.rs`: `PathMove { Rename, IntoSubdir, ExtractSubdir { dir, mode } }`, `ExtractMode`, `Recipe::extraction()`, `validate_recipe`'s "one extraction, and a clone or submodule one comes first" rule); the tree work in `cli-backend/src/history/moves.rs` (`Mover`), called by `trees.rs` `Content::rewrite_root` after the removal and blob rules; the new-repository modes in `history/extract.rs`; `GitBackend::rewrite_extract(repo, &plan, target) -> RewriteOutcome` (git2 delegates). `RewriteRefusal::PathCollision` (`path_collision`) is new; `ApplyArgs.target` carries the folder over IPC (`RewriteApply` stays the one operation: with a `target` the daemon calls `rewrite_extract`).
- **Trees, not blobs**: a rule maps a root tree to a root tree. `take` removes the entry at `from` (rebuilding the spine, dropping a directory that empties, memoised per `(tree, rule, depth)`), `put` inserts it at `to` and **refuses** when anything is there (the entry, a file where a directory is needed): the collision is a typed refusal of the preview listing `<short commit>: <path>` for every commit (capped at 50), never a silent overwrite. `IntoSubdir` wraps the root in one tree per segment, `ExtractSubdir` descends. Trees the stage creates are in the run's pack, not the repository, so `Mover` keeps their bodies (`made`) and `Content::rewrite_tree` reports its own into it; **trap**: reading a tree that only the pack has through `cat-file` fails. Order: removals / blob rules first (a removal glob names the original paths), then the path rules in recipe order. `would_change` (dirty-file check) asks `Mover::would_move` with `PathMove::map_path`, so an edited file the move relocates or drops refuses like one a removal would.
- **Combined order with the LFS rules (S20 + S21)**: per commit, `Content::rewrite_root` runs the content stage first (all `ContentReplace`, `RemovePaths`, `RemoveBlobsOver`, `LfsImport`, `LfsExport` rules, composed per file in recipe order, then the root `.gitattributes` the LFS rules own), and the path stage (`MovePaths`) last on the finished tree. So a removal glob, an LFS pattern and a content `paths` filter always name the ORIGINAL paths, whatever the recipe order, and the LFS `.gitattributes` lines are added at the original root and then travel with the move (into a subdirectory they stay valid: patterns without a slash match at any depth; an extraction keeps them only when the folder holds the file). The path stage never sees pointers differently from files (it moves tree entries, ids unchanged). Both kinds set `Content.walks`, so the tree walk runs when either is present.
- **Extraction and history shape** (`engine.rs` `run_pass`): `Done.vanished_root` marks a commit whose extracted root is empty; with no surviving parent it **vanishes** (`Pass::vanished`, also in `dropped` for the commit map): children lose that parent instead of mapping to a stand-in, and a ref on it gets `RefChange { new: None }` (a tag too, annotated ones through `peel`). Commits with the same tree as their parent are dropped by the existing `prune_empty` logic. Parents are **deduplicated** after mapping (git ignores a duplicate parent), `Commit::serialise` now writes all new parents at the first `parent` header (fewer than before is normal), and with an extraction a parent that is an **ancestor of another parent** is dropped (`is_ancestor` over the walk, positions are topological), which is how a merge of a side branch that never touched the folder simplifies away; `collapsed` is `parents.len() == 1`. `apply` turns `new: None` into `delete <ref> <old>` (undo: `create`); the checked-out branch vanishing is refused in the preview as `empty_scope`. Falsified: a rename that overwrites (collision test red on both backends).
- **Clone and submodule** (`extract.rs`): the preview widens the scope to `AllLocal` + tags and treats nothing as pushed or protected (`Pushed::none()`, no other-worktree or dirty checks, no "other branches keep the old history" warning): the plan is the new repository's, and `apply` **refuses** such a plan (`invalid_rule`), or it would rewrite this repository. `rewrite_extract`: check the target (absent or empty, not inside this repository), (submodule: no edits or untracked files under the folder or in `.gitmodules`, a branch checked out, the folder in HEAD), `git clone --no-local`, a local branch for each local branch here (`update-ref --stdin`), `remote remove origin`, then `preview` + `apply` of the same recipe with the extraction **in place** inside the clone (the tracking refs are gone, so nothing is pushed there), then `update-ref -d ORIG_HEAD`, drop `.git/gitpoulpi`, `reflog expire --expire=now --all`, `gc --prune=now`: the old objects are gone from it (tested with `cat-file -e`). Any failure removes the target. This repository's run directory is removed at the end, so clone mode leaves it **byte for byte** as it was (falsified: skipping the removal turns the test red on both backends). The new repository is a full clone, with its HEAD checked out first (the rewrite's `read-tree -m -u` then moves the files); a `--no-checkout` clone was rejected because `git status` would show every file deleted and the dirty check would refuse. A recipe with secret literals cannot run here (the stored recipe has none).
- **Why the submodule mode does not rewrite this repository** (a decision, the maintainer's wording asks for "the original references it as a submodule"): rewriting the folder out of the history would change every commit id, force a push and a recovery for everybody who has the repository, and lose the ability to check out an old commit with its files; what the owner described (the repository now references the new one) is one commit. So: `git rm -r <dir>`, `git -c protocol.file.allow=always submodule add <abs path of the new repository> <dir>`, `git commit --only -- .gitmodules <dir>`; a failure rolls the index, `.gitmodules`, the folder, `submodule.<dir>.*` and `.git/modules/<dir>` back. The user changes the URL later. A rewrite of the original that drops the folder stays possible as a separate in-place run with `prune_empty`, but it is not offered by this mode.
- **Journal**: `RewriteApply` with a `target`: clone mode records nothing (`rewrite_after` returns `None` for an outcome that moved no ref); submodule mode captures the current branch only (`capture_rewrite`, `Effect::Files`, like a commit) and the outcome's one ref change is the entry. **Undo of a submodule commit** needed `move_head_keep` (`journal_ops.rs`) to `deinit` the submodules a move turns back into folders first (`diff-tree` gitlink to non-gitlink; a submodule with local changes refuses the undo): `reset --keep` alone leaves the submodule's files in the way and nests the folder (measured). Redo gives an uninitialised submodule (an empty folder); `git submodule update --init` fills it (the file transport needs `protocol.file.allow`).
- **TUI** (`rewrite_view/paths.rs`): the rule forms existed (S8); the extract-mode question shows what the highlighted way does (three notes, `rewrite_paths` section at the end of the locales), and a clone or submodule answer opens the app's folder picker over the workbench (its own `Lister`, pumped in `poll`, owns keys, mouse and paste while up; Esc or the cross cancels and the question is asked again): the chosen folder gets a folder named after the extracted directory (`-2`... when taken and not empty) and the Summary names it. `ApplyArgs.target` goes with the apply; after it the new repository opens in a new tab (`take_open_repo` in the window tick, through the submodule tab-open seam), the result headline says what was made. `path_collision` shows as a refusal list (first line dropped) with a hint. No path completion in the forms (not cheap: the scope's paths are not in the TUI).
- **CLI/MCP**: `--move-path FROM=TO`, `--into-subdir DIR`, `--extract DIR --extract-mode clone|in-place|submodule`, `--target PATH` on `rewrite preview|run`, `--target` on `rewrite apply`; the extraction rule is added before the other path flags; a missing mode is `needs_choice` with exit 3 (also for a recipe file), a missing target an error. Help keys in the 17 `locales/help/*.toml` are English placeholders except French; the new i18n keys are one `[rewrite_paths]` section (before `[rewrite_push]`), English in the 15 other locales.
- **Tests**: contract on both backends (`testkit/contract/rewrite_paths.rs`: rename across history with tree and blob ids checked against `rev-parse`, into a subdirectory, collisions refused with commit and path and nothing left, extract with prune checked against `git rev-list -- lib` and `git log -- lib`, deleted branches and tags, merge simplification, clone mode leaves the original byte-identical and the clone has only that history and no remote, submodule mode: `.gitmodules`, gitlink, `git submodule status`, dirty refusal creates nothing, move back restores the folder); daemon (`tests/rewrite_paths.rs`: in-place entry undo and redo with the deleted branch, clone records nothing, submodule undo and redo); CLI JSON (`tests/rewrite_paths.rs`); TUI unit tests and `pty_rewrite_paths.rs` (clone through the picker, a new tab with only that history). Falsified: collision overwrite (red), clone leaving the run directory (red).

## History rewrite message encoding (S24)

- **Rule**: `RuleKind::MessageEncoding { names }` (`core/src/domain/rewrite/encoding.rs` is the pure part: `fix(bytes, declared) -> Fix::{Keep, Text{text, changed}, Left}`, `decode_declared`, `repair_mojibake`). The engine stage is `cli-backend/src/history/encoding.rs`, run from the rule loop in `engine.rs` like the mailmap (it also returns a count of commits it could not decide, `Counts::encoding_left`).
- **Declared encodings**: the `encoding` header value is resolved as git reads it: `ISO-8859-1` and its aliases are strict Latin-1 (every byte is the code point; WHATWG would map `0x80-0x9F` to Windows-1252 letters), `UTF-8` is the identity, anything else goes through `encoding_rs` (`Shift_JIS`, `Windows-1252`, `EUC-JP`...) with **no replacement**: a byte that is not valid in the encoding makes the commit undecidable, never a U+FFFD. Encodings that are not ASCII compatible are refused (a header cannot be in them).
- **Mojibake is repaired only when provable**: the text encodes back to bytes by Latin-1 (all chars <= U+00FF) or by Windows-1252, those bytes are valid UTF-8, the result has no U+FFFD and no control character besides newline, tab and CR, **and** mangling the result the same way gives the original text exactly. So `Ã© and é` (a real letter among the mangled ones) and `Ã© 日本` (letters outside the old alphabets) are *left*, never half repaired. Trap: a lone `é` is French, not mojibake; `Ã©` alone is indistinguishable from mangled `é` and is repaired.
- **The header goes only with a clean commit**: when a commit has an `encoding` header and the message decodes, the author and committer lines are decoded with the same encoding too (they were written in it; `Person::parse` needs UTF-8, so a Latin-1 name had no `Person` before). If any of the three cannot be decoded the whole commit is left and counted: a commit is never half converted. `names` only adds the mojibake repair of names that are UTF-8 already. `Commit::serialise` drops the header through `Edit::drop_encoding`; nothing else about the header block changes.
- **Plan**: `RewriteField::Encoding` (`ISO-8859-1` -> `UTF-8`) is a field change of the row; the old text of a line that had no `Person` is its lossy decoding (`author0`), so the preview reads. `Counts::encoding_left` (the Summary warns). `commits_changed` includes the children that only get a new parent.
- **Tests**: unit tests of the pure part (declared, Shift_JIS, mojibake both flavours, the not-provable table) and of `Commit::serialise`; contract on both backends (`testkit/contract/rewrite_encoding.rs`: Latin-1 and Shift_JIS headers become UTF-8 with the same text and no header, the names decoded, other headers kept; mojibake repaired and a plain neighbour keeps its id; `Ã©` next to a real `é`, a headerless Latin-1 message and an unknown charset left byte for byte and counted; the names option); CLI `fix_message_encoding_flags_*`. Falsified: replacing the strict UTF-8 check and the round trip by a lossy decode turns the "leaves what it cannot prove" and "repairs mojibake" contracts red.
- **UI/CLI**: a Messages-group template (`RuleTemplate::MessageEncoding`, toggle `FieldId::FixNames`), palette `Rewrite: Fix Message Encoding...`, flags `--fix-message-encoding [--fix-name-encoding]` (the second alone is an error), i18n keys in `# s24` blocks at the end of `[rewrite_view]` and `[palette]`.

## History rewrite: agents propose a recipe (S25)

- **Proposal**: `Proposal::Rewrite { recipe: Box<Recipe> }` (`ipc/src/ui/proposal.rs`), kind `rewrite`; CLI `ui propose rewrite --recipe FILE|-` (TOML or JSON, read by the same `read_recipe` as `rewrite preview`), MCP `ui_propose_rewrite` (class `propose`, behind `allow_ui_propose` like the others).
- **What it cannot be**: `Proposal::problem()` refuses a recipe with no rule, more than `REWRITE_RULES` (50) rules or any `Script` rule (a script runs when previewed, even a sandboxed Lua one on the whole history, so the user adds scripts themselves). It runs in the commands layer, in the daemon's `show` (a client that skips the commands layer is refused there) and in the window. `Recipe::saved()` drops secret literals before the recipe leaves the CLI; `sanitize()` cleans the name and the rule labels and resets `script_policy` (which the daemon fills in anyway).
- **What the window does**: `apply_propose` refuses unless the input mode is normal and no proposal is pending, then `open_rewrite_proposed` builds the workbench like the palette does (`rewrite_scope_context`) and `RewriteView::with_proposed` swaps the recipe in and sets a notice. **Nothing is previewed, applied, purged or pushed by it**: the only path that tells the proposal it was accepted is `send_apply`, reached from the user's own Apply key or click (`proposal::confirmed(Dialog::Rewrite, recipe json)`); `agent_propose_tick` settles it once the workbench closes (`accepted` when the recipe is as proposed, `accepted_edited` when changed, `rejected` when never applied). The banner (`✦ Proposed by <client>: ...`) is the global one, drawn over the workbench too.
- **Tests**: PTY `pty_rewrite_propose.rs` (opens filled and marked, waiting and a preview rewrite nothing, Esc rejects, then the user's F5 and Ctrl+S rewrite and the proposal is accepted); CLI `propose_rewrite_*` (script refused before routing, secrets dropped, repository untouched); MCP `a_rewrite_proposal_reaches_the_window_and_rewrites_nothing` and the policy list; core unit tests. Falsified: making the view preview and apply by itself when opened turns the PTY test red at "opening rewrites nothing".

## Integration trust

A saved token is only as safe as the address it is sent to, so the address, the
account and the certificate an integration was connected with are recorded
(`gitpoulpi-hosting/src/trust/`) and checked before a client exists.

- **Where.** `<private>/trust.toml` (`TrustStore`), written through
  `secure_fs::write_private`. Not in `config.toml`: anything that can edit the
  config (including an agent with file access) must not be able to widen trust.
  A file that exists but does not parse is an **error** (`Error::TrustStore`),
  never an empty store: an empty one would let the migration re-trust whatever the
  config says now. `TrustGate` re-reads the file on every use, so a `trust` run in
  another process applies at once.
- **The gate.** `client_for(integration, token, transport, gate)` calls
  `TrustGate::admit`: plain http, then origin (normalized `scheme://host[:port]` +
  base path, IDNA, default port dropped), each refusal a `TrustProblem` that is
  also queued (`trust::report`, deduplicated per subject and kind) for whoever asks
  a person. The CLI resolves **no token** before `preflight` has passed.
  Then the transport is wrapped in `GuardedTransport`: a request off
  `api_origins(kind, recorded)` is refused unsent. Providers that need a second
  host must be added to `api_origins`; the provider test suites run under the same
  guard (`TrustGate::for_tests`), which is how a stray host shows up.
- **Migration.** No record: an integration with a cached `user` (or one the caller
  `adopt`s because a token resolved) gets a record from the config as it stands,
  written silently on first use. One that was never connected is held in memory
  until `record_after_check` runs, so a failed login on a typo does not pin the
  typo.
- **Account.** `verify_account` / `check_account` compare the host's account **id**
  (login only when an id is missing); a record with no account yet learns it.
  Always after a token is stored, once per process for `GITPOULPI_TOKEN_*` or helper
  tokens, and after 24 hours otherwise. The CLI wires it into `login`, `test` and
  `projects`; other commands rely on the origin gate.
- **TLS traps.** (1) `ReqwestTransport` has **one** shared client with pooling off
  (a kept-alive connection belongs to the runtime that opened it), built with
  `use_preconfigured_tls(GuardedVerifier)`: the verifier's policy table is
  process-wide, keyed by host, filled by the gate whenever it admits a record, so a
  second integration on the same host with no pin does not clear the first's.
  (2) `GuardedVerifier` delegates `verify_tls12_signature`, `verify_tls13_signature`
  and `supported_verify_schemes` to webpki **always**: that signature is what proves
  the server owns the pinned key; skipping it makes a pin a bearer token anyone
  who has seen the certificate can replay (a handshake test with a server holding
  the wrong key guards this). (3) A pin only excuses issuer, self-signature and
  name failures, never expiry. (4) `rustls` must stay the version reqwest links
  (0.23, ring). (5) `send` follows redirects only within one origin: only
  `Authorization` is stripped by the library on a cross-origin redirect, the other
  token headers are not.
- **Forgetting.** `trust::forget(paths, integration)` (logout and remove) deletes
  the record and what was cached from the host: the Clone dialog's lists, the Radar
  account answer, the remote review drafts keyed by the host. Per-repository Radar
  snapshots are keyed by repository and cannot be enumerated from an integration;
  they hold public lists and age out.
- **AI.** `TrustGate::admit_ai` binds an API-key profile to the origin of its
  `effective_base_url()` (loopback http allowed, no account check); a local agent
  has nothing to bind.
- **Trust is a person's.** `hosting integrations trust` and `ai trust` need
  `Session::may_trust()` and are in the MCP `EXCLUDED` list; an agent only reads the
  refusal text (`TRUST_REFUSAL`).

## AI privacy and the sensitive staging guard

- **One cleaning step for every request.** `gitpoulpi_ai::client_for_in` (and the network twin) wraps every client in `PrivacyClient`, which runs `privacy::prepare` (drop the per-file diff of a path matching `[privacy] sensitive_patterns`, then `core::privacy::redact_in` per section) before anything is sent. A caller cannot forget it: the signature takes a `&Privacy`. The tests that pass their own `AiClient` to `*_with` bypass it on purpose.
- **Sections end at a header or a closing fence.** `diff -- path (status)`, `diff --git` and a split piece's `### H1 path hunk` start a section; the closing fence is plain text, so dropping the last diff keeps the prompt's fence. A path that is dropped leaves no trace in the prompt (not even its name); the report holds it.
- **The gate asks once per run.** `Privacy::approved` is shared by every clone of a job, so the retry of a composer or a split is not asked again. The TUI's `Gate` (`ai_sent_dialog`) is a process-wide modal fed by the worker thread; a request canceled elsewhere drops its sender and the dialog closes itself. The history rewrite actions get no gate: their dialogs already show what is sent.
- **Input caps never cut silently (`[ai.limits]`).** `gitpoulpi_ai::limits` holds the caps (`Limits`), what is over one (`Cut`: part, detail, bytes, limit, setting) and the decision (`LimitPolicy::decide`). The TUI answers it with a second process-wide modal gate, `ai_limit_dialog::Gate` (`Job::with_limit_gate`, set in `ai_ui::with_limits` next to the privacy gate), so the worker simply waits: no outcome plumbing, no restart, and the stash prompt, the pull request form and the Explain popup under it stay as they are. It is asked *before* the prompt is built, so "What is sent" shows the text that really goes. Cancel is `Error::Declined` (a cancel, not a failure); "Open setting" cancels and leaves the key in `take_open_setting`, which the key and mouse handlers turn into Settings. A review asks once for everything (`Job::suggest_review_with_context`: context files, description, comments, per-file and total diff). Nothing starts an AI run by itself (every one is a button, key, palette entry or command), so no run needs a rule for "nobody is watching" beyond a missing gate: without one (`Job` of the CLI and MCP) the caps truncate, `Job::truncated()` records each cut and the answer reports it (`truncated`), and `with_send_all` (`--send-all`) lifts them. Left internal on purpose: `MAX_ANSWER_BYTES` (an answer sanity cap), the 20 lines / 6 KB of text around a conflict (`CONTEXT_LINES`, `CONTEXT_BUDGET`: surrounding context, not what the user chose to send) and the 50-commit sample and 100 refs / authors of the history-rewrite requests (counts the user picks in their own dialog).
- **The preview setting is read fresh.** `ai_ui::with_privacy` reads `Settings::resolve_ambient()` per request, because a window's copy of the config is older than the file "Don't show again" just wrote.
- **The staging guard is the daemon's** (`stage_guard.rs`): `StageFile`, `StageFolder` and `StageAll` call it with the target; the paths come from the target or `backend.status()` (so a system-git stage spends one more spawn, in the perf budget), contents are read from the working tree (regular files, at most 1 MiB each and 32 MiB in all, a NUL in the first 8 KiB means binary: judged by name only). The refusal is `TypedError::SensitiveStage`; `allow_sensitive` is the retry and is a `serde(default)` key of the same request. Hunk and line staging is not guarded.
- **The TUI has one handler.** Every staging entry point ends in `Action::StageFile|StageFolder|StageAll`; `run_action` hands a refused one to `stage_guard_dialog::ask` (process-wide, keyed by the repository root so another window never receives the answer) and answers it with `Action::AllowSensitive(inner)`. No answer is highlighted until an arrow or a digit.
- **MCP withholds the argument.** `registry::tools_for(cfg)` drops `allow_sensitive` from the `stage` schema and `to_argv` refuses it unless `[mcp] allow_sensitive_staging`.

## Per-repository authentication

Decision record: [ADR-0010](decisions/0010-per-repository-authentication.md). The design is staged; this section says what has landed.

**Landed: the git boundary and the connection test (no stored profile yet).**

- **`NetworkAuth` and `set_network_auth`.** The daemon is not wired to it yet: nothing sets an entry in a running session, so behaviour in the app is unchanged. The backends and their contract tests are the whole slice. An entry is held in memory, keyed by canonical repository root, and replaces the global `credentials.https_mode` and `ssh.private_key_path` for that repository's network processes.
- **One function builds the credential and ssh part of a network command** (`apply_network_auth` in `gitpoulpi-cli-backend/src/process.rs`): `git_async` and `run_network` both call it, so submodule update and LFS cannot drift from fetch and push. Its `-c` arguments are returned to be spliced among the global options (they must precede the subcommand); its environment is set on the command.
- **Traps.** `credential.helper=` (empty) is what removes the repository's helpers, URL-scoped and included ones too; without it a repository's own helper runs even though another is listed (the contract check proves both sides, falsified by removing the reset). `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` set on the child replaces an exported one and outranks `core.sshCommand`. An empty `core.askPass` plus removing `GIT_ASKPASS` / `SSH_ASKPASS` is what keeps a prompt from appearing. `Git2Backend` only forwards: it has no networking of its own.
- **Test helpers** (`gitpoulpi-testkit`): `AuthHttpRemote` answers `401` + `WWW-Authenticate: Basic`, records every `Authorization` header and then answers `404`; `StubSsh` (Unix only) records its arguments and runs the trailing `git-upload-pack` / `git-receive-pack` command with `sh`, which makes a working `ssh://` remote out of a directory. The checks that need `GIT_SSH_COMMAND` in the environment live in `gitpoulpi-cli-backend/tests/network_auth_env.rs`, not in the contract suite, which must not touch the process environment.
- **`remote_access_check`** runs `ls-remote --heads` against `git remote get-url [--push] <remote>` through the same path, so an entry applies. The report strips URL userinfo, counts the advertised branches, and with `ssh_verbose` asks ssh for `-v` (appended to the effective ssh command: the entry's, else `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, else `core.sshCommand`, else `GIT_SSH`, else `ssh`) and reads `Server accepts key:` and `Authenticated to` from stderr (the key may be a path with spaces; the last accepted key wins). A failed connection is a report with a classified `ErrorDetail`, not an `Err`; git's messages are forced to English so the classification holds. On the daemon it is `OperationKind::RemoteAccessCheck`, a network read (no `RepoChanged`); there is no UI or CLI command yet.
- **Not undoable, not journaled:** an entry is not a repository mutation, like settings.

**Landed: the stored profile and the daemon's resolution (no UI, no CLI command yet).**

- **Where it lives.** `<private>/repo-auth.toml`, written by `secure_fs::write_private` (0600, a user-only DACL on Windows), never in `RepoState` (which can sit in the working tree with `per_repo_state_dir = in_worktree`), never in `.git/config`. One `[[repo]]` per repository, keyed by `user_data_folder_name(repo_root)` (all worktrees share it) with `git_common_dir` and `updated_at` beside it; `[repo.https]` has `source = system | integration | helper | token_command`, `[repo.ssh]` has `source = system | key_file | integration_key`. The types are `HttpsAuth`, `SshAuth`, `RepoAuthProfile` in `gitpoulpi-core` (`domain/repo_auth.rs`); the file is `gitpoulpi_session::repo_auth` (`load_repo_auth`, `save_repo_auth`, `clear_repo_auth`). An unreadable file is an error, never an empty store.
- **Resolution.** `credentials::apply_for(backend, repo, session)` replaces the global-only `apply_from_config` at every call site (network dispatch, the explicit and the background submodule update, the remote-undo replay; a clone has no repository and keeps the global call). It reads the file live (a change applies to the next transfer), resolves the profile into a `NetworkAuth` and calls `set_network_auth`; no entry is exactly today's behaviour and spawns nothing. What works: System HTTPS with an optional `username` (`credential.username`) and `ignore_repo_helpers`, SSH `key_file` and `integration_key`. System HTTPS still honours the process-wide `credentials.https_mode` (an entry replaces the mode and must not lift it).
- **`ignore_repo_helpers`.** `git config --show-scope --show-origin -z --get-regexp '^credential\..*helper$'`, keeping the entries whose scope is `system` or `global`. git gives a file included from `.git/config` the scope `local`, so included helpers are dropped too. The reset (`credential.helper=`) also clears URL-scoped helpers, so the kept URL-scoped ones are re-added with their scope (`NetworkAuth::credential_url_helpers`, `-c credential.<url>.helper=`): re-adding them unscoped would make a helper meant for one host answer for all. An empty value in the kept entries is git's reset and is replayed. Trap: git consults helpers in config order (system, global, local) and stops at the first one that gives both a user name and a password, so a repository's helper only matters when the machine's helpers could not answer on their own; the discriminating test is a marker helper, not a missing script.
- **SSH key file.** `<program> -i "<key>" -o IdentitiesOnly=yes [-o UserKnownHostsFile="<path>"] [-o StrictHostKeyChecking=yes]`, where the program is the first word of the effective **global** `core.sshCommand` (system or global scope; the repository's own is what the profile replaces), else `ssh`, so a key offered through the Windows OpenSSH client stays on that client. The key file must exist (else the transfer is refused). `integration_key` reads the integration's `IntegrationSsh` key from `config.toml` at each transfer. `ssh = system` keeps the global key behaviour (`global_ssh_key_command`).
- **A profile that cannot be used refuses the transfer.** `Error::RepoAuthUnavailable { reason }` (typed on the wire as `TypedError::RepoAuthUnavailable`): an unreadable `repo-auth.toml`, a missing integration or key, a git config that cannot be read, and the sources of the next slices (helper, token command), which are parsed and stored but answer "not available yet". Nothing falls back to the machine's credentials: the backend's entry is cleared first, the transfer stops, no process is spawned (test: the loopback server sees no request). The TUI shows the English text of this error until its own slice lands.
- **IPC** (all through the daemon, none are backend calls): `RepoAuthGet` returns a `RepoAuthView` (the profile with a command-running source's command blanked, `summary`, the identity per remote, the repository's own `core.sshCommand` and credential helpers with scope and origin file and whether the profile ignores them, warnings, `unavailable`), `RepoAuthSet { profile, confirm }` (helper and token command are refused with the typed "not available yet" whatever `confirm` says; the trust and hash pinning is a later slice), `RepoAuthClear` (broadcasts only when there was something to clear), `RepoAuthTest { remote?, push }` (a network read on the network dispatch path: `remote_access_check` per remote with the profile applied and `ssh -v`, a refused profile refuses the test too), `Event::RepoAuthChanged { repo }` after a set or clear, and `WorkingTree::repo_auth`, a typed `ActiveIdentity` (`RepoAuthProfile::identity`: how the HTTPS and SSH identities are reached, an account id or a key file name, never a secret; `None` when everything is default), put in the `Status` payload by the daemon from the private file with no spawn. The wording is the TUI's (`activity_log/identity.rs`): the window notes the identity when a refresh lands, and the push menu, the Activity Log entries of fetch, pull and push and the error readings only read that map, so nothing runs on the render thread.
- **Not journaled, not undoable**, like settings: the undo list is unchanged by a set or clear (daemon test).
- **Failure reading.** bash as `sh` words a missing helper script as `<script> get: line 1: <script>: No such file or directory`; `credential_helper_line` now reads that shape next to the existing `sh: ...` ones.
- **Windows hand-checks still owed:** a key path with spaces and the `known_hosts` quoting under Git for Windows' `sh`; the program word when the global `core.sshCommand` names `C:/Windows/System32/OpenSSH/ssh.exe`; the file's DACL; the System source with the credential manager (`credential.helper=manager` is in the system gitconfig and is cleared only when `ignore_repo_helpers` is on, in which case the machine-wide helpers are re-added).

**Landed: the credential broker and the Integration HTTPS source.** Decisions 12 to 17 of the ADR.

- **Pieces.** `gitpoulpi_ipc::git_credential` (the helper entry, its wire types), `gitpoulpi-daemon/src/grants.rs` (the grant registry and its guard), `account_source.rs` (resolving an account into a grant and a `NetworkAuth`), `gitpoulpi_hosting::git_username`, `gitpoulpi_core::redact_register_secret`, `Error::GrantRefused`, `OperationKind::CredentialGrant`. `credentials::apply_for(backend, repo, session)` returns the grant guard; the network dispatch, the explicit and background submodule update and the remote-undo replay each hold it until their operation ends.
- **What git's child gets**: `-c credential.helper=` (reset) then `-c credential.helper=!"<exe>" --gitpoulpi-git-credential`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, an empty `core.askPass`, `http.extraHeader` reset, and the environment variables `GITPOULPI_CREDENTIAL_GRANT` (128-bit random hex) and `GITPOULPI_CREDENTIAL_SESSION`. The token is only in the grant, in the daemon's memory.
- **Tests.** `gitpoulpi-daemon/tests/account_source.rs` (loopback only; the helper is the stand-in binary `gitpoulpi-credential-stub` of the daemon crate, wrapped on Unix by a script that logs its arguments and environment): the token arrives as `x-access-token:<token>`; the log holds no token; the grant answers nothing after the operation; a `url.<x>.insteadOf` to a second loopback server gets no header and the error is `GrantRefused` (falsified by removing the host check: red); an edited account address and a removed account refuse the transfer; a connection test reports `accepted_identity`. Unit tests: grant registry (host binding, lookalikes, shared and counted grants, `store`/`erase` read-back), the built `NetworkAuth` holds no secret, helper request parsing, `git_username`, `redact_register_secret` and the log (a prefix-less token is masked in the ring and the file). `AuthHttpRemote::start_accepting()` answers `200` with an empty-repository advertisement so a transfer succeeds and git sends `store`.
- **Traps.** The grant host is the trust **record's** origin, not `config.toml`'s URL (whoever can edit the config must not be able to move a token); an integration that was never connected has only a provisional in-memory record from the config, so the migration case keeps today's level of trust. A fetch and a push share one transfer lock, so a grant must be shared by repository and credential and counted, or an overlapping operation finds a just-revoked grant. A hook that runs during the operation (`core.hooksPath` can point into the tree) can call `git credential fill` and receive the token for the bound host: no new power, since the same hook can already read the token stored by the user's helper. The helper command quotes the executable path with `sh_quote_path` (Windows: forward slashes, double quotes; the flag is plain ASCII so MSYS path conversion leaves it alone) and accepts CRLF on stdin.
- **`RepoAuthGet` / `Set`** report and accept the integration source now; helper and token command came with the next section.
- **Windows hand-checks still owed:** the helper command under Git for Windows' `sh` with an install path that has spaces (`C:/Program Files/GitPoulpi/...`); the credential manager reset (`credential.helper=manager` is cleared for that invocation only); the broker through the real `gitpoulpi-tui.exe` of an embedded daemon (port and token files in `%APPDATA%`).

**Landed: the helper and token-command HTTPS sources.** Decisions 18 to 20 of the ADR.

- **Pieces.** `gitpoulpi-daemon/src/command_trust.rs` (the two hashes, `verify`, carrying a confirmation over an edit of the other fields), `token_command.rs` (the runner, the cache, `apply` for both sources), `grants.rs` (a grant may carry a token command; `answer` is async and runs it outside the registry lock; `GrantGuard::command_failure`), `gitpoulpi_cli_backend::run_command` (direct spawn under the same `TreeGuard` as network git, a timeout kills the tree with SIGKILL / the Job Object), `Error::TokenCommandFailed` and `Error::RepoAuthCommandChanged` (typed on the wire).
- **Who may confirm.** `RepoAuthSet { confirm }` is the only door. The daemon cannot tell a person from an agent on the socket (as for hook trust), so each surface decides: the CLI sends `confirm` only from a session that `may_trust()`, the TUI after its confirm dialog, MCP has no set at all. The daemon computes the hashes itself.
- **Traps.** git can ask the helper twice at once (two processes of one fetch): without the one-at-a-time lock the command ran twice and an erase raced the second get. `RepoAuthGet` must map `RepoAuthCommandChanged` into `unavailable` too, or the view of a refused profile looks healthy. An in-tree file is any argv word (or `--opt=value` value) that canonicalizes to an existing file under the canonical root; a file outside the tree is not pinned (the repository cannot change it). A pinned script that vanished counts as changed.
- **Tests.** `gitpoulpi-daemon/tests/token_command.rs` (Unix, loopback only, `sh` scripts): the token reaches the server and is cached across two fetches (counter file), runs in the repo root, is in neither the helper's argv nor its env, and the view blanks the argv; a 401 server (`AuthHttpRemote::start_rejecting`) erases the cache; `hosts` bind the grant (`GrantRefused`, the command never ran); an edited in-tree script is `RepoAuthCommandChanged` until confirmed again; a failing command gives a redacted stderr tail; a helper source with a forged confirmed hash or a changed in-tree helper script is refused; an unconfirmed or forged-hash set is refused and nothing is stored. Unit tests: output parsing, cache/invalidate/forget, a timeout kills the process tree (the grandchild is gone), `run_command`, the hashes. Falsified: removing the `erase` invalidation, the script-hash comparison and the confirm requirement each turns its test red.
- **Windows hand-checks still owed:** a `pwsh -NoProfile -File` token script, and a `.cmd` command (CreateProcess starts `.exe` only: a `.cmd` needs `cmd /c`); an argv and an in-tree script path with spaces; the Job Object really killing a `pwsh` child that started its own child on timeout; a helper string with `C:/Program Files/...` and its `script_sha256` (drive letters and `\\?\` canonical paths).

**Landed: the helper reaches an unpublished daemon, and says so when it cannot.** Decisions 21 to 23 of the ADR.

- **The bug.** `Session::connect(.., publish: false)` (every CLI run that finds no live daemon) starts an embedded daemon that does not write `sessions/<session>.port`. The helper used to find the daemon only through that file, so with no TUI or MCP open for the session a token-command or integration profile failed with `AuthFailed` ("could not read Username ... terminal prompts disabled"); with `-s <session of an open TUI>` it passed because that daemon's port file existed. The daemon tests had hidden it by publishing the port themselves (`Rig::new`).
- **The fix.** `grants::GrantGuard::endpoint_file` writes `sessions/<session>.grant-<id>.cred` (port, newline, token hex; user-only) while the grant lives, and `account_source::grant_env` adds `GITPOULPI_CREDENTIAL_ENDPOINT=<that path>`. `server::run` registers its endpoint per session name (`grants::register_endpoint`, dropped when the server returns; the one registry is process-wide, so two daemons of the same session name in one process would shadow each other: only tests do that). The helper (`git_credential::ask_daemon`) reads the file first, then the port file. Never the token in an environment (ADR decision 22).
- **Diagnostics.** `Entry::asked` is set by every request that names the grant; `reads_as` maps "no credential / prompts disabled" plus "never asked" to `CredentialHelperUnreachable`. `take_outcome` resets `asked` per remote, which is how `RepoAuthTest` flags one remote at a time. The dispatch path checks the failure's message and its `detail.stderr`.
- **Tests.** CLI: `gitpoulpi-cli/tests/repo_auth.rs` `a_token_command_works_from_a_standalone_cli_run` (loopback, no published daemon, `repo-auth test` and `fetch` succeed, no `.cred` or `.port` file is left; red on the code before the fix). Daemon: `tests/credential_helper_unreachable.rs` (the stub helper told to stay deaf: `CredentialHelperUnreachable`, and the connection test's stderr starts with the sentence; falsified by dropping the `!asked` branch), the endpoint variable and the absence of the IPC token in the helper's log in `tests/token_command.rs`, grant unit tests.
- **Windows hand-check owed:** CLI `repo-auth test --push` with a `sh.exe` token command (`--command "C:/Program Files/Git/bin/sh.exe" <path>/token.sh --hosts github.com`), no TUI open.

## Traps: a ref to a missing object, and a daemon that "stopped" mid-read

- **`%(*objectname)` aborts git on a ref whose object is gone.** git 2.5x double-frees (SIGABRT, exit 134) when `for-each-ref` formats `%(*objectname)` or `%(*objecttype)` for a ref to a missing object, after listing a valid ref first; `%(objectname)` and `%(upstream:short)` print the raw id and exit 0. `refs()` on the system backend therefore never asks for the peeled fields: it peels only tag and mirror rows with one `cat-file --batch-check` (`<oid>^{}`; a missing object keeps its raw id, like `Git2Backend`). The other reads that still use them (`filtered_tips`, `messages::tips`, the rewrite `history::refs::list`) go through `read_with_retry` (one retry after 100 ms, then the error with its exit code and stderr), and `filtered_tips` returns that error instead of an empty list (an empty list made the Log walk only `HEAD`). Contract check: `check_refs_survive_a_ref_to_a_missing_object`.
- **`DaemonHandle::shutdown` drains.** It used to stop only the accept loop; the connection tasks and their blocking dispatches kept running, so a test deleting its fixture right after raced a `for-each-ref` still reading it. Shutdown now stops every connection's reader and waits (up to 5 s, so a long clone cannot hold the process) for the in-flight dispatches; a test that spawns the TUI link loop stops it first (`daemon_link/link_guard.rs`) and drops the fixture last.
- **The Clone dialog opens on a remembered source (`repos.clone_start_source`).** `last` (default) reopens the source the user last moved the dialog to, `url` always the URL form, anything else is a profile id. The last source is state, not config: `clone-source.toml` in the config folder (`gitpoulpi_session::CloneSource`), a profile id or nothing for URL, never an index (profiles get reordered). `CloneDialog::take_source_change` reports only a choice made by the user (opening on the start source, or the Radar's `with_target`, is not one), and `RepoDialogs::on_clone` (run after every key, click and poll) writes it, so every way of closing is covered. Only `ManagementEntry::Clone` applies the start source; `open_clone_for_review` keeps its own. A profile that is gone or not a source is URL, silently.

### Per-repository authentication: remedies, the pin move and key permissions

- **Readings.** `RepoAuthUnavailable`, `GrantRefused`, `RepoAuthCommandChanged`, `TokenCommandFailed` and the `NeedsConfirmation` of an unconfirmed command (`COMMAND_NEEDS_PERSON`) have catalog sentences (`typed_error`) and lead to `Remedy::OpenRepoAuth`. A failed login inside a repository offers that page and, only when an integration account may be its source (the identity says so, or the profile is default), the Integrations page too. `GrantRefused` carries the host and the expected address, not the account, so its sentence says "the account token". Every path that transfers under a grant words its failure through `GrantGuard::explain` (network dispatch, submodule update, the remote step of an undo).
- **The old pin.** The tab menu row and the palette entry that opened the pin menu open the page; `RemoteUiAction::PinSshKey` and `Action::SetSshCommand` are gone (the CLI `ssh-command` command and `OperationKind::SetSshCommand` stay). The page shows a local `core.sshCommand` read-only (git reports the key lower-cased: compare ignoring case) and "Move into GitPoulpi" saves the equivalent key-file profile and only then unsets the pin, as two daemon requests. Undo story: the profile write is a setting and not undoable; `SetSshCommand` is not journaled (changing the SSH command never was), and nothing is lost since the key is in the profile. A failed save leaves the pin; a failed unset leaves both, which sign in with the same key.
- **Key permissions.** `secure_fs::readable_by_others` / `restrict_to_user` (Unix mode bits, `chmod 600`; Windows `has_foreign_access` / `protect_user_only`) run on a thread, once per key text the draft names, never on the render thread.

## The movable dialog frame (`dialog_kit/frame.rs`)

The frame is a process-wide registry, not an object each dialog owns. A dialog adopts it with one `const WIN: dialog_kit::Win = Win::movable("id")` (position only: the size follows the content) or `Win::resizable("id", min_w, min_h)`, and two calls: `WIN.place(screen, default_rect)` for the rect (the kept geometry, clamped to the screen, else the dialog's own default, so the default geometry is unchanged) and `WIN.decorate(buf, rect, close_cross, enabled)` after it painted (corner grips, the arrange banner, pointer shapes, and the registration the gestures are routed from). `CloseHit::framed` / `DiscardGuard::framed` do both the panel and the decoration. `place` is pure, so the `*_rect(area)` helpers that the mouse handlers also call are simply wrapped in it.

- **Routing is global.** `event_loop::input::handle_mouse_inner` offers every mouse event to `frame::on_mouse` first (the topmost registered dialog under the press decides; a press on its title bar or, for resizable ones, a border or bottom corner starts a gesture), and `handle_key_inner` offers every key to `frame::on_key` (arrange mode) and handles `Ctrl+N` (`dialog_frame_key`) for a framed dialog or popup that has no badges of its own: it arms `frame::armed`, the last paint badges `frame::top_badges` (the close cross, every hand or field the dialog registered in `pointer_map` since its `place`, plus what it `frame::offer`s), the key after it is `m` (arrange), a badge (`frame::inject_click`: a left click the input drain reads as the pointer's, so a surface that owns the mouse sees the real thing) or a cancel (dialogs with badges go through `InputMode::Navigation`, whose `m` is `NavigationAction::ArrangeDialog`). Three more paths reach `m`: the commit message form and the rewrite page badge their controls with a flag (`form.badges`, `view.badges`) instead of a mode, and `keys_modes::arrange_after_prefix` reads the key after their `Ctrl+N` (the footer then shows `form_badges_hint`, which names `m`); a dialog opened by the rewrite page (question, purge, AI dialogs) badges nothing (`badge_ready()` is false), so there `Ctrl+N` arms the frame's prefix like over any plain dialog; and the palette family (`frame::POPUPS`) takes `Alt+G` (`palette.arrange`), because `Ctrl+N` is its "down". `frame::begin_frame()` runs with `pointer_map::begin_frame()` and clears the registry, so a dialog that stopped painting is no longer on top. The palette rows `Dialog: Move or Resize` / `Reset Position and Size` call `arrange_top` / `reset_top`, so they act on whichever dialog is on top (the palette is reachable over the Undo history, Review and Board dialogs; elsewhere the chord is the twin).
- **One setting for all dialogs.** `ui.dialog_geometry` applies to every dialog; each is keyed by its id. `session` keeps the geometry in a process-wide map, `saved` also writes the whole map to the one `<data>/dialog_geometry.txt` (`id x y w h` per line, read once, written when a gesture ends) and restores every id at the next launch, `never` forgets a dialog's geometry on the first frame painted without it (`ephemeral` ids).
- **A gesture owns the pointer**: once a press starts a drag, every event until the release is the frame's, wherever the pointer is, so a release outside never reaches the dialog's "click outside closes". A new drag does not start while `enabled` is false (a popup of the dialog is up); the close cross wins the title bar.
- **Ids**: `safety`, `activity_log`, `jobs`, `folder_picker` (resizable); `confirm` (every `Confirm`, so they share a place), `confirm_prompt` (the legacy `InputMode::Confirm`), `about`, `updates`, `trust`, `conflict_popup`, `ai_sent`, `tag_delete`, `stage_guard`, `mermaid`, `init`, `sparse`, `worktree`, `clone`, `flow`, `issue_flow`, `pull_request`, `issue`, `workspace`, `workspace_ai`, `meta_picker`, `review_*`, `board_columns`, `merge_notice`, `compose_move`, `settings_*_editor`, `repo_auth`, `rewrite_*`, `review_choice`, `reword`, `palette`, `tab_picker`, `picker_theme`, `picker_language`, `workspace_search`. Dialogs that share an id share a place (the three Settings editors have their own).
- **The palette family** (`palette`, `tab_picker`, `picker_theme`, `picker_language`, `workspace_search`, and the `help` overlay) is framed and resizable; `Ctrl+N` is their navigation prefix, never "down" (`popup_list::nav` has no `Ctrl+letter` pair at all: `Ctrl+N` is the prefix, `Ctrl+P` the palette's chord everywhere and a filter field owns the letters, `Ctrl+K` included), and each offers its filter box and first row to the badges (`frame::offer_list_popup`); `Alt+G` (`palette.arrange`) arranges whatever dialog is in front: `place` wraps the `centered_rect`, `decorate` runs **after** the hint band is drawn (the band's border would otherwise paint over the corner grips), and `mouse_modes::popup_list_mouse` (which owns the mouse before `handle_mouse_inner` while they are open) offers the event to `frame::on_mouse` first. The list already derives its rows from the painted rect (`popup_list::View`), so growing the popup grows the list and the page size. Running the palette row `Dialog: Move or Resize` / `Reset` from the palette acts on the palette itself and keeps it open (`keys_modes`, Enter arm); those two rows are always available (they used to be greyed unless the Undo history was open).
- **Resizable forms use the room**: Clone's list rows (`CloneDialog::list_rows`, from the painted height), the Pull request and New issue description (`body_cap` grows by the rows over the default height), "What is sent" and the rewrite AI dialogs (lines re-wrapped to the placed width, the pane takes the rows), the Settings editors and the workspace AI box (the text area is the whole rect), Mermaid (the preview). Review options only gains width (its fields), it has no text area.
- **Excluded on purpose**: full-page screens (Settings, the Review and Board pages), anchored menus and dropdowns (context menus, the revert-parent dropdown, the Open-in popup, combo lists), tooltips. Not yet framed: the keybindings page's popups, the remotes form, the split-commit editor, the AI explanation popup.
- **Trap**: the dialog's `ListState::clamp` used to run in the paint and `keep_visible` pulled any scrollbar-set offset back to the cursor, so a scrollbar press seemed to do nothing. Paints now only clamp the cursor (`clamp_selected`); keys and clicks scroll to the cursor.
- **Trap**: a divider's grab zone must leave the first cell of every pane's top border to the pane (the `Ctrl+N` badge presses a cell there), so the horizontal divider is one row, the files box's bottom border.
- **Trap**: `m` after `Ctrl+N` is a keymap action (`nav_prefix.arrange_dialog`), so it is also never handed out as a badge character.
- **Trap**: layouts that wrap text to a width computed before the rect exists (the update panel, About, security check) are movable only; making one resizable means computing the wrap from the placed rect.
- **Tests**: `frame.rs` unit tests (including two dialogs under `saved`, one under `never`), `frame::testing::check_gestures` on each adopting dialog, `tests/pty_dialog_frames.rs`, `tests/pty_dialog_frames_more.rs` and `tests/pty_nav_everywhere.rs` (`Ctrl+N` badges and `m` on the palette, a picker, the search, the help and two ordinary dialogs, `Alt+G`; palette move / resize / reopen, `Alt+G`, a resized editor, `Ctrl+N m` on the commit message form and a rewrite dialog).
